# FlacronAI — Persistent Project Context (CONTEXT.md)

> **Source of truth for project state across sessions.** Read this file AND `PHASES.md` before starting any implementation work.
> This file must be updated immediately after every implementation session (see "Session/Change Log" at the bottom).
> This supplements — does not replace — the existing `CLAUDE.md` (golden rules/tech stack) and `PROGRESS.md`/`TASKS.md` (existing micro-task tracker). When a phase from `PHASES.md` is implemented, its micro-tasks should still be logged in `PROGRESS.md` per `CLAUDE.md`'s existing working method; this file tracks phase-level state specifically.

---

## Project Overview

**FlacronAI** is a web platform that generates AI-assisted draft insurance inspection reports. Core loop: a user (independent adjuster, adjusting firm, inspector, agency, TPA, restoration company, etc.) enters claim/inspection details, uploads supporting documents and up to 100 photos, the "FLACRON ENGINE" (Claude-based AI service) analyzes the material and drafts a structured report, a human reviews/edits/approves it, and it's exported (PDF/DOCX/HTML) or shared.

Stack: React 18 + Vite + Tailwind (frontend), Node/Express + Firebase Firestore/Auth/Storage (backend), Anthropic Claude (primary AI, via `@anthropic-ai/sdk`) with IBM WatsonX as a text-only fallback, Stripe (billing), AWS SES (email). Full technical detail lives in `CLAUDE.md` section 4 — do not duplicate it here; refer to that file for env vars, run commands, and stack specifics.

## Main Goal

On 2026-08-14, a detailed 50-item + 33-item product requirements spec (external document, not previously part of this repo) was audited against the actual codebase to find gaps between the intended product vision and what's actually built. This roadmap (`PHASES.md`) exists to close those gaps in a controlled, dependency-aware, one-phase-at-a-time way — without breaking what already works, and without violating the Golden Rules in `CLAUDE.md` (no fabricated claims, cautious AI language, mandatory human review, server-side entitlements, consent, security, micro-changes, git safety on `flacron/improvements`).

## Important Findings From the Audit (full detail in `PHASES.md` per-phase "Reason" sections and in the spreadsheet's Notes column)

- ~~**Critical correctness bug:** `backend/services/aiService.js:233` only sends the first 10 of up to 100 uploaded photos to AI analysis, contradicting the marketed/enforced "up to 100 photos per report" capability.~~ **FIXED 2026-08-14 (Phase 1)** — `analyzeImages()` now batches and analyzes all uploaded photos (up to whatever the caller's own cap is, currently 100), with accurate failure/skip reporting. See `PHASES.md` Phase 1 for implementation detail.
- ~~**No async processing architecture at all.**~~ **RESOLVED 2026-08-16 (Phase 7):** `POST /generate` now responds immediately after upload; photo analysis and report generation run as a background in-process pipeline (`backend/services/photoJobService.js`) with real per-photo/per-stage status tracking (`analysisJobs` collection) and resumable progress via `GET /:id/analysis-status`.
- ~~**Brand token conflict**~~ — **RESOLVED 2026-08-15 (Phase 2):** `#FD4403`/`#002A64` confirmed authoritative. In resolving it, found and fixed a much bigger real bug: 417 occurrences across 28 files were using Tailwind's generic default `orange-*` classes (a muted `#f97316`-family color) instead of the brand tokens, sitewide — corrected to the true brand orange.
- **Generate Report wizard is 5 steps, not the spec's 7** — Inspection Information and Documentation-upload steps don't exist at all; several Claim Info fields (Policy Number, Insurance Company, Insured First/Last split, real Claim Type) are missing.
- **Report Editor is plain textareas**, not rich-text — no formatting controls, no section add/delete/reorder; only 1 of 7 spec'd AI panel functions exists (generic "Suggest").
- Several public pages don't exist at all: `/features`, `/photo-analysis`, `/solutions` (+ per-vertical pages), `/login`, `/signup` (as distinct URLs — currently unified under `/auth`).
- Several authenticated pages/views don't exist at all: dashboard home/metrics, ~~`/analytics`~~ (**RESOLVED 2026-08-18, Phase 15** — a real general analytics page, server-side organization-vs-personal scoped via Phase 14's role model, computed entirely from real Firestore data), `/integrations`, `/photos` (photo library), `/organization`, `/audit-logs` (viewer — the backend logs everything, there's just no UI), `/help`.
- ~~Templates and Team features are real but shallow versus spec (Templates = flat field-defaults bag, not a structural template builder; Team = 4 roles not 7, no member profile).~~ **RESOLVED:** Templates — 2026-08-18 (Phase 13), a real structural template builder with personal/organization/Flacron scopes. Team — 2026-08-18 (Phase 14), the full 7-role (+ legacy alias) model with a real member-profile page.
- No in-app notification center, no global search, no post-signup onboarding flow (except a separate enterprise-subdomain-specific one), no report comments, sharing links have no permission levels or expiry.
- Engine branding is inconsistent across the site — three different name variants in use ("Flacron Engine", "FlacronAI Engine", literal "FLACRON ENGINE" never used in app code, only in spec docs).

## Major Technical Decisions & Constraints

- **Hosting:** Backend on Render (single Node web service, no persistent worker/queue infra currently provisioned); frontend on Vercel. This directly constrains Phase 7's job-queue technology choice (see Open Questions).
- **Database:** Firestore, no ORM — any new data model (per-photo records, notifications, comments, templates) must be designed as Firestore collections/subcollections consistent with existing patterns in `backend/routes/*.js`.
- **AI provider:** Claude (Anthropic) is primary; WatsonX is a text-only fallback with no vision capability — image-analysis phases (1, 7, 8) are Claude-dependent and degrade to "unavailable" without `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`.
- **No TypeScript** anywhere in the codebase — plan changes as plain JS/JSX consistent with existing style.
- **Golden Rules in `CLAUDE.md` are non-negotiable** and apply to every phase: no fabricated claims, cautious AI language only, human review before finalize, server-side entitlement enforcement, opt-in consent for marketing, security-first for claim data, micro-changes with one task per commit, and strict `flacron/improvements`-only git workflow (no push/merge to main, no force-push).
- **Local dev environment gap:** per `CLAUDE.md`, the local backend `.env` is still missing a real `FIREBASE_API_KEY`, which blocks local testing of `/api/auth/login`, MFA login-verify, and account-deletion's password re-check. Verify this is resolved (or test against a non-local environment) before relying on local testing for phases touching those flows (notably Phase 3).

## ⚠️ Critical, cross-cutting finding (2026-08-16) — read before doing anything else

`git log` shows the last real commit on `flacron/improvements` is `d3435d6`. **All of Phase 1 through 5's work — every file this whole roadmap has touched — is uncommitted**, sitting only in this one local working directory (`git status`/`git diff --stat`: 40 files changed, never staged, never committed, therefore never deployed anywhere). The client reported real, confirmed-broken functionality in the live app (`/login`/`/signup` not rendering, `/auth` not redirecting, dashboard greeting missing) that this session's live-browser testing could NOT reproduce in the local dev server — every one of those routes/features works correctly against this exact working tree. **The most likely explanation: the client tested a deployment or checkout that predates all of this work**, since none of it has ever left this machine. This is a process/deployment gap, not (primarily) a code defect, and should be raised with the client directly — committing and deploying this work is very likely the actual fix still needed, independent of any further phase audits.

### 2026-09-23 (same day, follow-up) — Phase 47's RealtyAPI ToS/data-retention written confirmation RECEIVED, code compliance verified, zero mismatches, zero code changed

The client obtained written RealtyAPI approval (10 specific confirmed terms, via RealtyAPI's own authenticated dashboard support chat) covering: use/storage of processed facts in reports, a ban on raw-feed resale/republishing, a 30-day cache ceiling with confirmed-fields-on-reports permitted indefinitely, no required UI/export attribution, no special restriction on owner/MLS/valuation fields, photo URLs treated as source-CDN links (not FlacronAI-owned assets), valuation figures requiring third-party-estimate labeling, no mandatory deletion/refresh schedule, PRO-plan commercial-use permission, and confirmation RealtyAPI stores no FlacronAI end-user data.

**This session's job was review-only, per explicit instruction ("do not change code unless a real mismatch is found; report any mismatch before editing").** Each behavior-constraining term was checked against the actual shipped Phase 47 code (not the docs' own claims), with file/line evidence — full detail in `PHASES.md`'s Phase 47 section, "2026-09-23 — Phase 47 RealtyAPI ToS/data-retention written confirmation RECEIVED" entry. Headline findings: the raw provider response is never stored or exposed anywhere (only `normalizePropertyResult`'s small named-field map survives past the initial request); the cache TTL (`propertyIntelligenceStore.js`'s `CACHE_TTL_DAYS`) already defaults to exactly 30 and is enforced at read time; no photo/image field and no true valuation/AVM field exist anywhere in this codebase's RealtyAPI schema or field mapping at all (both are simply never ingested), so terms 6 and 7 are satisfied by the code never surfacing that class of data, not by an existing field carrying the right label — stated precisely rather than overclaimed. **Zero mismatches found. Zero production code changed.**

This closes external blocker #1 of Phase 47's 2 remaining items (see Phase 49's updated list above). **Blocker #2 — Render production key/config activation — remains explicitly open**, per the client's own instruction, until the RealtyAPI PRO plan and the production key are separately confirmed; nothing was staged, committed, pushed, merged, or deployed, and no secret was requested, printed, or exposed this session.

### 2026-09-22 — Phase 48 (Pricing Page, Admin Configuration UI & Cross-Surface Consistency), previously "Current Active Phase", now superseded above by Phase 49 — kept below for its own historical detail, unchanged

**Phase 48 (Pricing Page, Admin Configuration UI & Cross-Surface Consistency) was implemented 2026-09-22**, in the isolated worktree `.claude/worktrees/docs+web-estimating-phases`. Status: **Complete** — unlike Phases 43/45/46/47, this phase had no external provider dependency (no OpenAI/Stripe/Google/RealtyAPI credentials needed), so it required no live-validation-pending caveat; those four phases' own pending-live statuses are completely unchanged by this work. Phase 47's own entry (previously "Current Active Phase") is preserved below, unchanged, under its own historical heading.

**The gap this phase closed:** the public pricing page (`Pricing.jsx`) never mentioned photo capacity or add-on packs at all, and six other pages/data files (`Home.jsx`, `Features.jsx`, `FAQs.jsx`, `Security.jsx`, `PhotoAnalysis.jsx`, `ApiDocs.jsx`, `data/solutions.js`) independently hardcoded a flat "up to 100 photos" claim — the exact precedent CLAUDE.md already flags once (the Home pricing-preview report-count drift, fixed in T-1.8). Since Phase 44 the REAL mapping is 25/100/250/unlimited, so every one of those seven files was quietly wrong for 3 of 4 tiers. `EnterpriseDashboard.jsx`'s own upload wizard label ("Damage Photos (up to 100)") was doubly wrong — Enterprise is unlimited — and had no live capacity check backing it at all (unlike `Dashboard.jsx`'s wizard, already correctly wired to `photoCapacityDisplay.js` since Phase 44).

**Admin configuration architecture — one authoritative catalogue, extended (not replaced):** `backend/config/planConfig.js` (Phase 44) gained exactly one new field on its existing single-doc write path (`setPlanConfig`): a plain integer `revision`, computed from whatever's currently stored (0 for a doc that predates this field) and incremented on every write — added directly inside the existing non-transactional get+set flow so `plan-config.test.js`'s own narrow single-collection fake Firestore keeps passing completely unmodified (0 changes needed there). A NEW file, `backend/config/planConfigAdmin.js`, sits ONE LAYER ABOVE `planConfig.js` and is the only thing Phase 48's admin routes call: `validatePatchShape`/`mergeAllowedPatch` (a strict allowlist — `plans.<tier>.basePhotoLimit`, `addOnsEnabled`, `watermarkPolicyEnabled`, `displayLabels.<tier>` — any other field, including `reportsPerMonth` smuggled inside a `plans` patch, is rejected outright, never silently dropped), `updatePlanConfigFields` (read-current → check `expectedRevision` → merge → `validatePlanConfig` → snapshot the previous doc to a NEW `planConfigHistory` collection → `setPlanConfig`), and `rollbackToLegacyPlanConfig` (the ONLY code path that can write Phase 44's pre-existing `LEGACY_ROLLBACK_CONFIG` — a separate function from the normal patch path, so a normal edit can structurally never reach `status:'legacy_rollback'`). `backend/config/photoAddOnPacks.js` (Phase 45) gained one read-only addition, `getAdminCatalogue()` — capacity/amountCents/currency/active/order plus a per-mode (test/live) `priceIdStatus: 'configured'|'not_configured'`, never the Price ID itself. Pack economics stay deliberately NON-editable in this phase (see "Stripe Price-ID boundary" below) — there is exactly one photo-pack source of truth (`photoAddOnPacks.js`'s `CATALOGUE`) and exactly one plan-limit source of truth (`planConfig.js`'s Firestore doc / built-in fallback), and this phase adds an editing layer on top of the second without ever creating a second instance of either.

**Authorization model (reused verbatim from Phase 44's own precedent, `routes/sales.js`'s pre-existing `admin/update-tier`):** every new route is `authenticateToken` + the existing `requireAdmin` middleware (`req.user.email !== process.env.ADMIN_EMAIL` → `403 ADMIN_REQUIRED`) — single-admin-email, not role-based, exactly matching this codebase's documented current model (CLAUDE.md's own "no RBAC" note). A forged client-side claim (`role:'admin'`/`isAdmin:true` on the decoded token) is proven rejected by a dedicated test — authorization is a server-side EMAIL match only, never anything client-supplied. No recent-auth/MFA gate was added: the existing `requireRecentAuth` middleware is scoped specifically to the password-change flow (its own hardcoded user-facing copy says so) and no other admin-write route in this codebase (`admin/update-tier`, `admin/user/:uid` DELETE, etc.) uses any recent-auth/MFA requirement either — adding one here alone would be an inconsistent, one-off security posture rather than following an established pattern, so Phase 48 matches the existing precedent exactly. Writes are rate-limited (`planConfigWriteLimiter`, 20/min per admin uid, same shape as every other Phase 43/45/46/47 limiter), validated via `express-validator`, and every write/rollback calls the existing `recordAuditLog` (action `admin_plan_config_update` / `admin_plan_config_legacy_rollback`, actor uid+email, changed-field summary, never a secret).

**Configuration versioning/audit:** `revision` (plain integer) + a required `changeSummary` string on every write; `updatePlanConfigFields`/`rollbackToLegacyPlanConfig` both accept an optional `expectedRevision` — when passed and stale, the write is rejected with `409 PLAN_CONFIG_CONFLICT` + the real current revision, and NOTHING is written (the previously-active config remains in effect verbatim — proven by a dedicated test reading the doc back unchanged after a rejected conflicting write). A snapshot of the PRE-write doc is appended to `planConfigHistory` before every real update (never on the very first write out of the built-in fallback, since there is no real prior doc to snapshot yet) — `GET /admin/plan-config/history` surfaces it, admin-only. An invalid patch (bad shape, or a merge result that fails Phase 44's own `validatePlanConfig`) is rejected before any write happens — no partial publication is structurally possible, since `updatePlanConfigFields` validates the FULL merged config, not just the patch fragment, before calling `setPlanConfig` once. The legacy flat-100 profile is reachable only via its own dedicated route (`POST /admin/plan-config/legacy-rollback`), requiring both a `changeSummary` AND a literal `confirm:'true'` body field — never the default, never reachable via the normal patch endpoint, never silent.

**Canonical plan/pack mapping (unchanged from Phase 44/45, reused verbatim, never re-typed):** `starter`=25, `professional`=100, `agency`=250, `enterprise`=unlimited photos/report; packs `photos_25`=+25=$4.99, `photos_50`=+50=$7.99, `photos_100`=+100=$12.99, `photos_250`=+250=$24.99 (integer cents 499/799/1299/2499, USD, report-specific one-time).

**Public sanitized model — the actual new capability:** `GET /payment/public-plan-config` (new, unauthenticated, mirrors the `ai-status`/`property-lookup/config` no-auth precedent exactly) returns `{plans: {<id>: {label, basePhotoLimit|null, unlimited}}, addOns: {enabled, reportSpecific:true, checkoutAvailable, packs:[{id,label,capacity,amountCents,currency,active,available}]}, configSource}` — reading the SAME `getPlanConfig`/`getPublicCatalogue`/`resolveStripeMode`/`getPackPriceId` functions Phase 44/45 enforcement already uses, never a second read path. A dedicated test asserts the serialized response never contains `price_`/`stripePriceId`/`updatedBy`. `checkoutAvailable` is only ever true when a pack is both `addOnsEnabled` AND has a real configured Price ID for the CURRENT Stripe mode — it can never claim purchasing is live while Price IDs remain unset (true today, everywhere).

**Pricing page (`frontend/src/pages/Pricing.jsx`):** now fetches this endpoint via a new `usePublicPlanConfig()` hook (graceful client-side fallback to the same built-in 25/100/250/unlimited mapping on a network/config failure — proven never to fabricate a different number or grant unlimited). Each plan card gained a photo-per-report line; the comparison table gained a "Photos per report" row; a new "Photo Add-On Packs" section renders the live catalogue with an explicit "purchasing is not currently available" disclaimer whenever `checkoutAvailable` is false (true today) — never claims live Stripe purchasing without configured Price IDs. All pre-existing plan names/subscription prices/reports-per-month/export entitlements/CRM/white-label rows are completely untouched.

**Admin UI (`frontend/src/pages/AdminPlanConfig.jsx`, new page at `/admin-plan-config`, same client-side email-gate + `ProtectedRoute` pattern as `AdminTierUpdate.jsx`/`AdminDashboard.jsx`):** edits only the allowlisted fields above (per-tier photo limit with an explicit "Unlimited" checkbox — never a magic `-1`, display labels, add-ons-enabled, watermark-policy-enabled) with a diff-only preview-before-publish modal (via the extracted pure `buildPlanConfigPatch`/`validatePlanConfigForm` helpers in `frontend/src/utils/planConfigPatch.js`, unit-tested without rendering the page — this repo's established convention), an unsaved-changes `beforeunload` warning, explicit loading/saving/saved/error/conflict states (a 409 surfaces a "Reload latest" action), and a read-only pack-catalogue table + a safe integration-status panel (configured/not-configured/live-validation-pending/contract-pending badges, never a key/Price-ID value). A separately-gated, red-bordered "Emergency Legacy Rollback" panel requires typing a reason AND an explicit acknowledgement checkbox before its button even enables — structurally distinct from the normal save flow. No Stripe/OpenAI/Google/RealtyAPI credential field exists anywhere on this page.

**Cross-surface consistency matrix (every stale flat-"100" occurrence found and fixed):**

| File | Before | After |
|---|---|---|
| `Pricing.jsx` | no photo-capacity mention at all | live server-derived per-plan limit + pack catalogue |
| `Home.jsx` (5 spots: features list, how-it-works step, photo-analysis section ×2, animated counter) | "up to 100 damage photos" | `MAX_PHOTO_LIMIT` (250) + "depending on your plan (unlimited on Enterprise)" |
| `Features.jsx` (2 spots) | "up to 100 damage photos" | same pattern |

| `data/solutions.js` (2 persona feature blurbs) | "up to 100 photos" | `MAX_PHOTO_LIMIT` |
| `EnterpriseDashboard.jsx` wizard label | "Damage Photos (up to 100)" — doubly wrong, Enterprise is unlimited, and unenforced | "Damage Photos (unlimited on Enterprise)" |
| `Dashboard.jsx` (main wizard) | already correct (Phase 44) | unchanged — confirmed already live-wired to `photoCapacityDisplay.js` |

Marketing-prose fixes (all but `Pricing.jsx`) use ONE new shared static constant, `frontend/src/data/photoLimits.js` (`PHOTO_LIMITS = {starter:25, professional:100, agency:250}`, `MAX_PHOTO_LIMIT = 250`), explicitly documented as mirroring `planConfig.js`'s `FALLBACK_CONFIG.plans` — a deliberate, disclosed scope decision: these are prose mentions in six static marketing pages, not a live data table, so live-wiring every one of them (loading states, error fallbacks, six extra network calls on page load) would be a broad rewrite for a few words of copy; `Pricing.jsx` (the actual commercial/checkout surface, and the ONE page PHASES.md's own Phase 48 task 1 names explicitly) is the one page wired to the live endpoint. If the canonical mapping ever changes, both the live Pricing page AND every static mention update from the same two places (`planConfig.js` server-side, `photoLimits.js` client-side) — never six independently-drifting literals again.

RealtyAPI is always reported `contract_pending` regardless of env vars, exactly mirroring Phase 47's own `propertyIntelligenceService.getPublicConfig()` hardcoded `configured:false` (live transport genuinely isn't implemented yet).

### 2026-09-22 (same day) — Phase 48 cross-surface consistency CORRECTION: the shared static constant was itself a second source of truth

**Issue:** the paragraph above candidly disclosed a scope decision — six marketing pages read `frontend/src/data/photoLimits.js` (a static constant) instead of a live fetch, while `Pricing.jsx` alone read the real `GET /payment/public-plan-config`. On review this was correctly identified as still a second source of truth: an admin publishing a `PlanConfig` change updates Pricing.jsx immediately but never those six pages, which would keep showing whatever number was correct at the time this phase shipped.

**True single-source correction, now in effect:** `photoLimits.js` is FALLBACK-ONLY — its header comment says so explicitly, and it is imported by exactly one file, the new `frontend/src/utils/publicPlanConfigStore.js` (a pure, framework-free fetch/cache module: a 60s-TTL cache plus in-flight-promise sharing, so N simultaneously-mounted consumers of the `usePublicPlanConfig` hook trigger at most ONE network request, never a duplicate-fetch storm; a response failing `validatePublicPlanConfigResponse`'s shape check — missing `plans`, a non-boolean `unlimited`, a negative/fractional `basePhotoLimit`, a missing `addOns.packs` array — is treated exactly like a network failure and is never cached, so a malformed response can never be rendered as a real entitlement). `usePublicPlanConfig` itself is now a thin `useSyncExternalStore` wrapper around one shared module-level store instance — same external return shape (`{plans, addOns, configSource, loading, error, reload}`), so `Pricing.jsx` needed no changes at all.

**Per-surface resolution (the exact 8 named in the correction):**
- **`FAQs.jsx`** — the one marketing page making an EXACT per-tier comparison ("25 on Starter, 100 on Professional, 250 on Agency, unlimited on Enterprise") — now calls `usePublicPlanConfig()` and renders that one FAQ's answer through a new pure `frontend/src/utils/faqPhotoLimitAnswer.js` (`buildPhotoLimitAnswer(plans)`), substituted at render time for a stable-id'd FAQ entry whose static `a` field is now just a safe, numberless placeholder (used only if JS hasn't hydrated yet — SSR isn't in use here, so in practice this never renders).
- **`Home.jsx`, `Features.jsx`, `Security.jsx`, `PhotoAnalysis.jsx`, `ApiDocs.jsx`, `data/solutions.js`** — none of these need an exact tier comparison (each was a single generic blurb), so their numeric claims were replaced with non-numeric wording ("capacity scales with your plan", "a plan-based photo allowance", "up to unlimited on Enterprise") — the safer of the two options the correction offered, and the one that structurally cannot go stale since it makes no numeric claim at all. The `MAX_PHOTO_LIMIT`/`PHOTO_LIMITS` imports were removed from all six files entirely. Two purely illustrative, explicitly-labeled UI-mock counters (Home.jsx's and PhotoAnalysis.jsx's "Photo Batch (example)" panels — decorative batch-processing visualizations, never a claim about the viewer's own plan) were detached from any tier number and now show a fixed decorative `42`.
- **`EnterpriseDashboard.jsx`** — no longer shows a static label at all (neither the original wrong "up to 100" nor the first correction's still-static "unlimited on Enterprise"). It now calls `reportsAPI.getPhotoCapacity()` (Phase 44's own per-user, per-plan authoritative endpoint — the SAME one `Dashboard.jsx`'s wizard has used since Phase 44) on mount, and a new pure `frontend/src/utils/enterpriseDashboardPhotoLabel.js` (`deriveEnterpriseDashboardPhotoLabel`) renders "Damage Photos (checking limit…)" until that response actually arrives, then "unlimited" or "up to N" from what the server said — never an assumed/unverified value, and actual upload enforcement remains 100% server-side (Phase 44), completely untouched by this correction.

**Data-flow summary (the actual fix):** `planConfig/active` (Firestore) → `GET /payment/public-plan-config` (sanitized, unauthenticated) → `publicPlanConfigStore` (shared cache/dedupe) → `usePublicPlanConfig` → `Pricing.jsx` + `FAQs.jsx`. `EnterpriseDashboard.jsx` reads the same PlanConfig through a parallel, already-existing, per-user path (`GET /reports/photos/capacity` → `resolvePlanContext`). Every other surface makes no numeric claim, so it has nothing left to desynchronize. `photoLimits.js` is reachable only as the network-failure/loading branch inside `publicPlanConfigStore.js` — never a page's primary source again.

**Exact automated validation (real run, 2026-09-22):** 5 new frontend test files, **41 new tests** — `publicPlanConfigStore.test.js` (16: response validation incl. malformed-shape rejection, fallback-on-error, concurrent-call dedupe proving exactly one fetch, TTL freshness/expiry, forced `reload()` propagating a changed value, subscriber notification, failed-fetch-never-cached retry), `faqPhotoLimitAnswer.test.js` (3), `enterpriseDashboardPhotoLabel.test.js` (3: loading/unlimited/finite states), `crossSurfacePhotoCopySweep.test.js` (17: a real repository sweep reading each of the 6 non-numeric pages' actual source and asserting no bare tier-agnostic "N photos" claim survives outside the labeled illustrative example, that none of them import `photoLimits.js` any more, that `FAQs.jsx`/`Pricing.jsx` do use the live hook, that `EnterpriseDashboard.jsx` no longer contains either the original or the first-correction's static label, and that unrelated Pricing.jsx content — the 5/50/200 reports-per-month strings — is untouched). `photoLimits.test.js`'s own 2 tests updated to describe its new fallback-only role (values unchanged: 25/100/250). Full frontend suite: **301/301 passing, 33/33 files** (0 regressions vs. this session's own 263/263). Lint: **0 errors, 14 warnings** (unchanged baseline — none from any changed file). Production build: succeeds cleanly. No backend file was touched by this correction — `GET /payment/public-plan-config` and `GET /reports/photos/capacity` were already correct Phase-44/48 endpoints, so the backend suite was not re-run for this correction (no backend diff exists to regress). `git diff --check`: clean (the same one harmless LF→CRLF notice as every prior phase). Secret/key scan of every new/changed file: clean. Scope confirmed unchanged: `mobile/` untouched; the main working tree (`feature/mobile-initial-phases`) still exactly 53 uncommitted entries; worktree HEAD unchanged at `6a6df97`; Phase 49 and Phase 43/45/46/47's own pending-live-validation statuses completely untouched; nothing committed/staged/pushed/merged/deployed/opened as a PR.

**Phase 48 remains Complete** — this correction closes the gap between "Pricing.jsx is live" and "every other surface making an exact claim is also live," without which Phase 48's own cross-surface-consistency objective was only half met.

### 2026-09-22 — Phase 47 (Property Intelligence: RealtyAPI U.S. Adapter & Report Integration), previously "Current Active Phase", now superseded above by Phase 48 — kept below for its own historical detail, unchanged

**Phase 47 (Property Intelligence: RealtyAPI U.S. Adapter & Report Integration) was implemented 2026-09-22**, in the isolated worktree `.claude/worktrees/docs+web-estimating-phases`. Status: **`Implementation Complete — RealtyAPI Contract & Live Validation Pending`** (explicitly NOT "Completed" — the exact RealtyAPI product, documentation link, base URL, authentication method, subscribed plan, and field coverage are not yet confirmed; no real property-data network call was made anywhere, no key was ever placed in source/tests/logs, and no live U.S. lookup validation is claimed). Phase 46's own entry (previously "Current Active Phase") is preserved below, unchanged, under its own historical heading.

**Provider-independent architecture, exactly mirroring Phase 46/43's own established registry pattern:** `backend/services/propertyIntelligenceProviders/registry.js` selects an adapter purely from server env (`PROPERTY_INTELLIGENCE_PROVIDER`, default `realty_api`) — a client request can never choose provider/base URL/key/field mapping. `realtyApiProvider.js` is the RealtyAPI adapter boundary, but its `lookupProperty` **unconditionally throws `PROPERTY_PROVIDER_NOT_CONFIGURED`** — there is no `fetch()`/network call to any host anywhere in that file, by design, so even a premature/partial env configuration (key + base URL + enabled flag all set) can never trigger a guessed real network call (proven by a dedicated test that stubs `global.fetch` to throw if it's ever invoked, then confirms it never is). `mockPropertyProvider.js` satisfies the identical contract shape for tests only — it is never registered in the registry and cannot be selected via any env value.

**Normalized schema, additive and separate from Phase 46's address fields on purpose:** `backend/utils/propertyIntelligence.js` defines its OWN `FIELD_KEYS` (parcel/APN, property type, year built, living area + unit, lot size + unit, beds/baths/stories, garage type/spaces, roof/exterior/foundation/HVAC, assessed value + currency, property tax + currency, last sale date/price + currency, owner-on-record, flood zone, hazard summary, footprint area + unit) rather than extending Phase 46's `addressNormalization.js` FIELD_KEYS list — a deliberate boundary: those are address/geocoding components, these are building/parcel/tax/hazard/ownership facts from a conceptually different provider, so this phase never touches a single line of Phase 46's own schema/validation. Every field is the same `{value, source, verificationStatus, userOverride}` shape, `source: 'Third-Party Property Data'` exactly as PHASES.md's Phase 47 task 1 specifies. Lives at `report.propertyProfile.propertyIntelligence` — a SIBLING of Phase 46's `propertyProfile.fields`, one `PropertyProfile`, not a second source of truth. Every optional field has its own validator (parcel-number safe-character/length check, year-built range, area/lot finite-non-negative + supported-unit check, bedroom/bathroom/stories/garage count checks — bathrooms uniquely allow `.5` steps — money/currency checks reusing `canonicalEstimate.js`'s own `validateMoney`/`isValidCurrency`, past-only date check, capped/sanitized free text for roof/exterior/foundation/HVAC/ownership, and a footprint-geometry size/depth guard); an invalid individual field is dropped to an editable blank, never coerced into an apparently-verified value, and the rest of an otherwise-usable result is never discarded over one bad field.

**Trust boundary, mirroring Phase 46's `addressNormalizationStore.js`/Phase 43's `pricingProposalStore.js` patterns:** `backend/utils/propertyIntelligenceStore.js` persists a short-lived, REPORT-SCOPED (`reports/{reportId}/propertyLookups/{lookupId}`, default 30 min TTL) lookup record — report-scoped rather than top-level like Phase 46's address store, since a property lookup only ever happens against an EXISTING report's already-confirmed address (no pre-report-creation case exists here). Reusable until expiry (not single-consume), same re-open-the-review-step allowance as Phase 46's address token. `PUT /:id/property-intelligence` never accepts a client-claimed `source`/`verificationStatus` as trusted: `provider_confirmed` mode requires a valid, owned, unexpired `lookupId`, and only fields the user explicitly lists in `selectedKeys` are pulled from that SERVER-STORED record — an unselected field is left completely untouched, matching "user applies selected fields only." A field the user already owns (`userOverride: true`) survives a later apply UNTOUCHED unless that exact key is re-overridden in the very same call — proven by a dedicated test using a real lookup record. A cross-report or cross-user `lookupId` is rejected (each proven by its own test); an unknown/expired one is rejected safely. `manual` mode never touches the lookup store at all.

**Minimal shared cache, deliberately narrow:** a `propertyIntelligenceCache/{addressFingerprint}` doc (`propertyIntelligenceFingerprint.js`, a pure SHA-256 hash of normalized address components + provider + schema version — no reportId/userId/PII, same safety property as `pricingFingerprint.js`) lets two different reports looking up the SAME real U.S. address legitimately share non-ownership data; the ONE field with a genuine per-person privacy dimension (`ownerOnRecord`) is explicitly stripped before anything is ever written to this shared doc (proven by a dedicated test), and is only ever stored inside the report-scoped lookup record above. An explicit `recheck: true` bypasses the cache; an expired entry is treated as a miss, never served.

**Eligibility, exactly matching Phase 46's own handoff contract:** `propertyIntelligenceService.computeEligibility` requires a CONFIRMED (`propertyProfile.status === 'confirmed'`) address, `propertyLookupEligible === true` (i.e. `countryCode === 'US'`, Phase 46's own flag), and the minimum address components present — anything else (`address_not_confirmed`, `country_not_supported`, `incomplete_address`) is a normal, non-error state, never a thrown exception and never something that blocks report creation/editing. The frontend mirrors this exact function (`frontend/src/utils/propertyIntelligence.js`).

**Section 3 integration — the one explicitly approved exception to "sections outside Section 7 stay unchanged," and it APPENDS rather than replaces:** `backend/utils/propertyIntelligenceContent.js` builds a `### Confirmed Property Details (Public Records)` markdown block from ONLY `verificationStatus === 'user_confirmed'` fields (an unconfirmed provider suggestion structurally cannot reach it) and splices it in BENEATH Section 3's existing narrative — unlike Phase 42's Section 7 detail, which REPLACES the legacy body, Section 3's existing adjuster-typed narrative is always preserved byte-for-byte, per the phase brief's explicit instruction not to blindly replace it. The server computes this block ONCE inside `GET /:id` (`reportOut.propertySection3Markdown`) — the same architecture as Phase 42's `detailMarkdown` — so the desktop preview never duplicates money/label FORMATTING logic in a second language; `frontend/src/utils/propertyIntelligenceContent.js` only duplicates the tiny generic splice algorithm, exactly like `canonicalEstimateContent.js`'s own frontend mirror. The export route (`POST /:id/export`) composes this injection on top of the existing Section 7 injection before handing `content` to `generatePDF`/`generateDOCX`/`generateHTML`, so all three formats and the desktop preview render the identical block from the identical source, proven by a dedicated cross-format integration test (PDF text extraction, DOCX `document.xml` inspection, and the router's own `_test.generateHTML` export). A report with nothing confirmed yet renders byte-identical to before this phase (a documented no-op), and a legacy report with no `propertyIntelligence` at all gets a safe, empty, read-only synthesized view from `GET /:id` (mirroring Phase 46's own `buildLegacyPropertyProfileView` precedent) — as a NEW object, never a mutation of the underlying Firestore doc-data object.

**API surface, mounted automatically at both `/api` and `/api/v1`** (the mobile-phase versioning work already wires every router under both prefixes — no extra work needed here): `GET /reports/property-lookup/intelligence/config` (public, sanitized, never a provider name/key/base URL — a dedicated test asserts this), `POST /reports/:id/property-lookup/intelligence` (requests/serves-from-cache a lookup; returns a normal 200 with `status: 'not_eligible'` for non-US/unconfirmed, never an error), `GET /reports/:id/property-lookup/intelligence` (re-reads persisted status without a new provider call), `PUT /reports/:id/property-intelligence` (the only endpoint that ever persists reviewed/confirmed fields). Every route enforces the same access/finalized-immutability pattern as every other report-mutation route (`getReportAccess` + `canEditReports`/review-grantee, `409 REPORT_FINALIZED`, `{success, error, code}` envelopes, `recordAuditLog`) via a small shared helper introduced only because these three new routes need the identical check (no pre-existing route was touched/refactored).

**Frontend:** `PropertyIntelligenceReview` (a new export from the EXISTING `components/PropertyProfileReview.jsx`, per the phase brief's explicit "extend, don't duplicate" instruction) covers eligibility-gated messaging, loading, no-match/ambiguous/error states, a per-field checkbox-select + inline-edit review list for a full/partial result, and a confirmed-fields read-only view with a "Re-check" action. `PropertyIntelligenceModal` (`ReportPreviewPage.jsx`, mirroring `CanonicalEstimateEditor`'s own modal pattern) wires it to the three API calls; a new "Property Details (Section 3)" button sits alongside the existing "Detailed Estimate (Section 7)" button, gated to the same document types and `canEdit`.

**Exact automated validation (real run, 2026-09-22):** 8 new backend test files, **84 new tests** (schema/validators 16, fingerprint 4, provider registry/realtyApiProvider/mockPropertyProvider 8, trust-boundary + cache store 9, orchestration service 18, route-level 14, Section 3 content 12, cross-format export integration 3). ### 2026-09-22 — Phase 46 (Property Intelligence: Address Normalization & Google Integration), previously "Current Active Phase", now superseded above by Phase 47 — kept below for its own historical detail, unchanged

**Phase 46 (Property Intelligence: Address Normalization & Google Integration) was implemented 2026-09-22**, in the isolated worktree `.claude/worktrees/docs+web-estimating-phases`. Status: **`Implementation Complete — Live Google Validation Pending`** (NOT "Completed" — Google Cloud project access has not been granted; no real Google API calls, client key, or unrestricted demo key were used anywhere). Phase 45's own entry (previously "Current Active Phase") is preserved below, unchanged, under its own historical heading.

**Provider-independent architecture:** `backend/services/addressProviders/registry.js` selects an adapter purely from server env (`ADDRESS_PROVIDER`, default `google`) — mirrors `pricingProviders/registry.js`'s already-established pattern exactly, so a client request can never choose a provider/key/base-URL/field-mapping. `googleAddressProvider.js` is the first (only) implementation: `getPlaceDetails(placeId, {signal})` calls the Geocoding API's `place_id`-lookup mode with the SERVER key (field-masked to exactly `address_components,formatted_address,geometry,place_id` — data minimization), and `normalizeAddress(rawResult)` is a pure, Google-shape-aware mapping function. Everything downstream (`backend/utils/addressNormalization.js`'s schema, `backend/services/propertyService.js`'s orchestration, every route) only ever sees plain, provider-neutral values — provider-specific shapes never cross that boundary, proven by dedicated component-mapping tests for US/Canada/UK/subpremise/missing-field cases.

**Key separation, the actual design chosen:** `GOOGLE_MAPS_SERVER_KEY` (backend-only env var, `backend/config/googlePlaces.js`, never logged/returned/exposed) is completely independent of `VITE_GOOGLE_MAPS_BROWSER_KEY` (frontend Vite build-time env, `frontend/src/config/googleMaps.js`) — backend code never reads the browser var and frontend code never sees the server var; there is no code path that could leak one as the other. The browser key is delivered via **build-time frontend config**, not through the new public-config endpoint (the phase brief's documented alternative) — this keeps autocomplete latency lower (no round trip before the widget can load) and means the key never transits our own API surface at all. `GET /api/reports/property-lookup/config` (new, public, unauthenticated — modeled directly on the pre-existing `GET /reports/ai-status` precedent) returns only `{enabled, browserAutocompleteConfigured, serverNormalizationConfigured, schemaVersion, capabilities}`; a dedicated test asserts the serialized response can never contain the server key, and `propertyService.getPublicConfig()`'s return shape has no key-shaped field to begin with.

**Normalized address schema (schemaVersion 1, `backend/utils/addressNormalization.js` + frontend mirror `frontend/src/utils/propertyProfile.js`):** 17 tracked fields (`formattedAddress`, `addressLine1/2`, `streetNumber`, `route`, `neighborhood`, `city`, `county`, `state`/`stateCode`, `postalCode`/`postalCodeSuffix`, `country`/`countryCode`, `latitude`/`longitude`, `placeId`), each an independent `{value, source, verificationStatus, userOverride}` — satisfying "Value + Source + Verification Status + User Override" per-field, not just per-record. `source` ∈ `manual_entry|suggested|provider_selected|provider_normalized|user_overridden|legacy_manual|unavailable`; `verificationStatus` ∈ `unverified|provider_normalized|user_confirmed|unavailable|ambiguous|stale_recheck_required`; profile-level `status` ∈ `unconfirmed|confirmed|stale`. Coordinates are validated finite + in-range (`-90..90`/`-180..180`) and dropped to `null` (never clamped/guessed) when invalid — proven by test. `propertyLookupEligible` (the Phase 47 handoff flag) is `true` only for `countryCode === 'US'`; every other country is `false` (a normal, valid, non-eligible report), never treated as invalid.

**Trust boundary (mirrors `pricingProposalStore.js`'s already-established pattern exactly):** `POST /reports/property-lookup/normalize` resolves a browser-selected Google `placeId` through the SERVER key and persists a short-lived (default 15 min, `ADDRESS_NORMALIZATION_TTL_MINUTES`) `addressNormalizations/{token}` record scoped to the requesting uid — a top-level collection (not a report subcollection), since normalization routinely happens BEFORE a report exists (the wizard's property step). `PUT /:id/property-profile` (new) never accepts a client-claimed `source`/`verificationStatus`/coordinate as trusted: `provider_confirmed` mode requires a valid, owned, unexpired `normalizationToken`, and `propertyService.buildConfirmedPropertyProfile` reconstructs every trusted value from that SERVER-STORED record only — any `overrides` in the same request explicitly flip just those specific fields to `user_overridden`, and everything else becomes `provider_normalized`/`user_confirmed`. A field the user already owns (`userOverride: true`) survives a later re-confirm/re-lookup UNTOUCHED unless that exact key is re-overridden in the very same call — "never silently overwrite a confirmed value" is enforced structurally (a dedicated test proves a stale re-lookup can't clobber a user's already-set unit number). `manual` mode never touches the normalization store at all — pure caller-supplied overrides, nothing else trusted.

**Persistence/authorization:** `PUT /:id/property-profile` reuses the IDENTICAL access pattern already proven by `PUT /:id/canonical-estimate`/the Phase 43 pricing route — `getReportAccess` (owner-with-`canEditReports` or a `review` grantee), 404 on no access (never leaks report existence), `409 REPORT_FINALIZED` immutability, `{success, error, code}` envelopes throughout, `recordAuditLog`. `GET /:id` now synthesizes a non-destructive, read-only `legacy_manual`/`unavailable` `propertyProfile` VIEW from a pre-Phase-46 report's plain `propertyAddress`/`propertyStreet`/etc. fields whenever none exists yet — never written back by the GET handler itself, never claims Google verification for data that was always plain text, and an already-existing Phase-46-era `propertyProfile` is left completely untouched (both behaviors directly tested).

**Frontend:** `AddressAutocompleteField.jsx` is an accessible ARIA combobox (`role="combobox"`/`listbox`, full keyboard nav, `aria-live` status announcements, debounced + out-of-order-response-protected via the pure `frontend/src/utils/addressAutocomplete.js`, Google Places session tokens rotated on selection) dropped directly into `Dashboard.jsx`'s existing Step 2 "Property Address" field — gated behind the sanitized config check, so with no key configured anywhere (true right now, everywhere) it renders as the EXACT SAME plain manual `<input>` as before Phase 46, zero behavior change for any current user. `PropertyProfileReview.jsx` is the required review/accept/edit/reject step; nothing is treated as confirmed until the user explicitly confirms it there. Confirmation lives in wizard-local state until the report is created, then persisted with one follow-up `savePropertyProfile` call — a failure there is toasted and logged, non-blocking, since the report itself was already created successfully with its plain-text fields intact either way (satisfies "Google unavailable/failed never blocks report creation" for both the lookup AND the save step).

**Phase 43 regional-input handoff — the scope note, stated plainly:** PHASES.md's own Phase 46 entry lists "wiring property data into estimate pricing region" as out-of-scope/a Phase-43 follow-on, while the fuller Phase 46 change request that drove this session separately asked for a Phase 43 mapping plus tests. Both are honored without contradiction: `mapPropertyProfileToLocationContext`/`computeLocationFingerprint` (`frontend/src/utils/propertyProfile.js`) are pure, fully-tested mapping utilities producing the exact `{country, state, city, postalCode}` shape `pricingSuggestions.buildPriceSuggestionsRequestPayload` already sends — no PII, no lat/long, no raw provider payload, no room-size/living-area substitution (Phase 43's own line-item quantities are completely untouched). The only WIRING added is a single, additive, one-time prefill inside `ReportPreviewPage.jsx`'s `CanonicalEstimateEditor`: its four manual pricing-location inputs are pre-filled from `report.propertyProfile` exactly once, only while all four are still blank, never overwriting anything the user already typed — deeper wiring (e.g. auto-invalidating a pricing cache when a confirmed address changes) remains the documented Phase 43 follow-on, not built here. `backend/config/openai.js`/`backend/services/pricingService.js` were NOT touched, per the standing instruction not to modify the OpenAI integration.

**Phase 47 handoff:** `buildPropertyLookupHandoff`/`computePropertyLookupEligibility` expose `{propertyLookupEligible, countryCode, confirmed}` — `true` only for a CONFIRMED US address; every other case (non-US, unconfirmed) is `false`, never "invalid." RealtyAPI itself is never called anywhere in this phase.

### 2026-09-19 — Phase 45 (Stripe Report-Specific Photo Add-Ons), previously "Current Active Phase", now superseded above by Phase 46 — kept below for its own historical detail, unchanged

**Phase 45 (Stripe Report-Specific Photo Add-Ons) was implemented 2026-09-19**, in the isolated worktree `.claude/worktrees/docs+web-estimating-phases`. Status: **`Complete`** (updated 2026-09-22 — real Stripe test-mode + Firebase-emulator live validation finished, user-confirmed via a real browser; see the addendum after this section). Phase 44's own entry (previously "Current Active Phase") is preserved below, unchanged, under its own historical heading.

**Confirmed packs/prices (client-confirmed, unchanged from PHASES.md):** `photos_25`=+25=$4.99 (499¢), `photos_50`=+50=$7.99 (799¢), `photos_100`=+100=$12.99 (1299¢), `photos_250`=+250=$24.99 (2499¢); USD; one-time; tied to one report.

**Trusted pack catalogue (`backend/config/photoAddOnPacks.js`, new):** the single, hardcoded, server-authoritative source for the 4 packs above — `{id, label, capacity, amountCents, currency, active, stripePriceId:{test,live}}`. `resolveStripeMode(secretKey)` derives test/live from whether `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY` itself starts with `sk_live_` (tamper-proof — never trusts `NODE_ENV` or a client value); defaults to `'test'` whenever the key is absent/malformed, never silently `'live'`. `resolveActivePack(packId)` rejects an unknown OR inactive pack identically (no enumeration oracle). `getPackPriceId(pack, mode)` never mixes test/live slots. `getPublicCatalogue()` strips `stripePriceId` entirely for the client-facing `GET /payment/photo-packs` response, which additionally computes a per-pack `available` flag (`addOnsEnabled && priceId configured for the active mode`) so an unconfigured pack is shown-but-disabled, never hidden or crashing. **Every Price ID slot is genuinely empty right now** (read from 8 new env vars, `STRIPE_PRICE_PHOTOS_{25,50,100,250}_{TEST,LIVE}`, none set anywhere in this repo) — no fabricated `price_xxx`-looking default was ever written, proven by a dedicated test asserting every slot is `null`.

**Checkout-intent design (`backend/utils/photoAddOnPurchases.js`, new) — one collection, not two:** rather than a separate `checkoutIntent` collection plus a separate `PhotoAddOnPurchase` collection kept in sync by hand, ONE Firestore collection (`photoAddOnPurchases`) holds a single lifecycle document per purchase attempt that IS both, at different stages of its own `status` state machine: `pending → session_created → fulfilled | expired | failed`, then `fulfilled → disputed → fulfilled (won) | dispute_lost`, or `fulfilled → refunded`. `createCheckoutIntent(db, {uid, reportId, pack, mode})` is itself one atomic transaction that reads the report, authorizes ownership (`report.userId !== uid` → `NOT_FOUND`, same never-distinguish convention as `loadOwnedReport`), rejects `archived` (`NOT_FOUND`) and `finalized` (`REPORT_FINALIZED`) reports, persists the trusted intent (server-resolved capacity/amount/currency/mode — nothing client-supplied), AND appends the intent id to a small bounded `report.photoAddOnPurchaseIds` array — all in the same write, so a report can never end up eligible-but-unrecorded. That bounded array is also what makes the sanitized purchase-history read possible with zero Firestore query support needed (this repo's lightweight `FakeFirestore` test double has no `.where()`, and a real per-report query would need a composite index) — `listSanitizedPurchasesForReport` just fetches each id directly by doc key.

**Checkout creation (`POST /payment/photo-pack-checkout`, new route on the existing `routes/payment.js`, reachable at both `/api` and `/api/v1` since that router is already mounted at both prefixes via `server.js`'s `API_PREFIXES` loop):** client sends only `{reportId, packId}`. Order of checks: (1) unlimited-tier short-circuit using ONLY the requester's own account tier (never report data, so it can't be used to probe another report) → `{success:true, unlimited:true}`, zero Stripe calls, zero intent created; (2) `PlanConfig.addOnsEnabled` → `503 ADDONS_DISABLED` if off (the whole feature's kill switch, left `false` in both `RECOMMENDED_DEFAULT_CONFIG`/`FALLBACK_CONFIG` — unchanged from Phase 44 — so this phase ships fully dark by default until an operator deliberately flips it on, post-Stripe-access); (3) `resolveActivePack` → `400 INVALID_PACK`; (4) `getPackPriceId` for the resolved mode → `503 STRIPE_ADDONS_NOT_CONFIGURED` if that slot is empty (today, always, since no Price ID exists yet) — this is the safe, non-breaking degradation the phase spec required, verified by a test that also confirms an ordinary photo upload/report-generation flow is completely unaffected while add-ons are unconfigured; (5) `createCheckoutIntent` (ownership/finalized/archived, above); (6) `stripe.checkout.sessions.create({mode:'payment', payment_method_types:['card'], line_items:[{price: <server-resolved priceId>}], success_url, cancel_url, metadata:{checkoutIntentId, reportId, schemaVersion}, payment_intent_data:{metadata:{checkoutIntentId, reportId}}}, {idempotencyKey: `photopack_${intent.id}`})` — the idempotency key protects against stripe-node's own automatic network-retry creating a duplicate session for the same stored intent (its documented purpose), not against a user double-clicking (each click makes a fresh intent/session by design — since a Checkout Session never charges until the user completes it on Stripe's page, an abandoned duplicate session is a zero-cost, self-expiring artifact, not a double-charge risk). Success/cancel URLs are built server-side ONLY from `process.env.FRONTEND_URL` + the fixed `/reports/:id/preview` route — never a client-supplied redirect. On a Stripe API failure, the intent is marked `failed` (`markSessionFailed`) and `502 CHECKOUT_SESSION_FAILED` is returned; a fresh retry (a brand-new intent) is unconditionally safe. On success, `markSessionCreated` records the session id and the response returns only `{checkoutIntentId, url}` — no price, amount, or Price ID.

**Webhook trust boundary (extends the EXISTING `POST /payment/webhook` — same raw-body middleware, same `STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET`, same `processedWebhooks`-by-event-id idempotency gate checked BEFORE the switch, same signature verification; nothing about the subscription path's parsing/ordering/behavior was touched):** `checkout.session.completed` now branches FIRST on `session.metadata?.checkoutIntentId` (a photo-pack session always carries it; a subscription session never does, so the existing `uid`+`tier` branch below is untouched and re-verified by a regression test) into `fulfillCheckoutIntent(db, checkoutIntentId, {stripeSession, eventId, source:'webhook'})`, which — inside ONE Firestore transaction — re-reads the trusted intent, checks it's still in a fulfillable status (`pending`/`session_created`; `fulfilled` is a no-op, anything terminal is rejected), then cross-checks the Stripe session's `mode`/`livemode`/`currency`/`amount_total`/`payment_status`/`id`/`metadata.checkoutIntentId` against the STORED intent (never the session/metadata alone) — any mismatch flips the intent to `failed` with a reason and grants nothing. On a full match, the SAME transaction marks the intent `fulfilled` AND increments the report's own `purchasedPhotoCapacity` snapshot (see capacity-integration below) together — there is no window where one is true and the other isn't. New events wired, using ONLY real Stripe event names for the installed SDK (stripe v14.25.0) — **no invented event names**: `checkout.session.expired` (grants nothing, ever), `charge.refunded` (full → revoke; partial → flag for manual review, capacity UNCHANGED — see reversal policy below), `charge.dispute.created` (suspend), `charge.dispute.closed` (Stripe's own `dispute.status` field, `'won'`/`'lost'` — there is no `charge.dispute.won`/`charge.dispute.lost` event in the real API, so those two outcomes are read off the ONE `closed` event, exactly the adjustment PHASES.md asked to be reported precisely if an assumption didn't match the real SDK). `charge.refunded`/`charge.dispute.*` events carry a Stripe `payment_intent`/Charge id, not our own `checkoutIntentId` directly — resolved via `stripe.paymentIntents.retrieve(id).metadata.checkoutIntentId` (set at checkout-session creation via `payment_intent_data.metadata`), a genuinely-required extra server-side Stripe call, never guessed. Payment-method scope note: `payment_method_types: ['card']` only (matching the existing subscription checkout's own convention) means Checkout's delayed-notification events (`checkout.session.async_payment_succeeded`/`_failed`) are not reachable in practice and were deliberately NOT wired — documented here rather than adding dead code for an unused payment method.

**Atomic fulfillment/idempotency, concretely proven:** duplicate delivery of the SAME event id is caught twice over — the pre-existing outer `processedWebhooks` check (unchanged), AND (belt-and-suspenders against that check's own known non-atomic read-then-write race under a true concurrent redelivery) `fulfillCheckoutIntent`'s own transactional status-gate, proven directly by a 6-way concurrent-call barrier test asserting exactly one grants capacity and the report's `purchasedPhotoCapacity` ends at exactly 25, never more. Out-of-order dispute delivery (a real risk PHASES.md called out explicitly) converges correctly via two small bookkeeping fields on the intent (`activeDisputeId`/`disputeOutcome`/`resolvedDisputeId`, keyed by the Stripe Dispute's own id) rather than assuming `created` always arrives before `closed` — proven by two dedicated tests: `closed(lost)` arriving with no prior `created` still revokes, and a LATE `created` for that same already-resolved dispute is a correct no-op (and the mirror case for `won`).

**Reversal/dispute policy exactly as specified:** existing report photos are NEVER deleted by any refund/dispute/expiry path (proven by test — a full refund on a report with existing photos leaves `report.photos` untouched, only `purchasedPhotoCapacity` changes). A FULL refund revokes exactly the purchased capacity once (idempotent replay is a no-op). A PARTIAL refund is flagged (`intent.manualReview = {reason:'partial_refund', amountRefundedCents, flaggedAt, eventId}`, surfaced in the sanitized purchase-history response as `needsManualReview: true`) WITHOUT changing capacity at all — no fractional photo credits are ever invented, exactly the simplest secure policy the spec allowed. `charge.dispute.created` suspends (capacity removed from the live snapshot immediately); `charge.dispute.closed(won)` restores exactly once; `charge.dispute.closed(lost)` keeps/reapplies the revocation permanently. An expired Checkout Session (`checkout.session.expired`) grants nothing, full stop, and can never resurrect an already-fulfilled purchase (tested).

**Phase 44 capacity-integration (`effectiveCapacity = basePlanCapacity + verified fulfilled add-on capacity`, exactly the formula Phase 44's own resolver already defined):** rather than a separately-queried collection scan for "sum this report's fulfilled purchases" (again, no `.where()` support in the test double, and a real composite index isn't worth it for a value that changes only on the rare fulfillment/reversal event), the report document itself carries a small, ALWAYS-transaction-updated snapshot: `purchasedPhotoCapacity` (the current sum) + `photoAddOnCapacityGrantedFor` (a Set-backed array of which intent ids are currently counted, making every grant/revoke/restore idempotent by construction, never a blind `+=`/`-=`) + a bounded `photoAddOnHistory` audit trail (no sensitive payment data). Every fulfillment/refund/dispute transaction in `photoAddOnPurchases.js` updates this snapshot in the SAME transaction as the owning intent's own status flip — it can never drift from the intents that justify it. Two real routes were extended to actually USE it: `GET /:id/photo-capacity` (now reads `report.purchasedPhotoCapacity` as `verifiedAddOnCapacity` into the EXISTING `resolvePhotoCapacityForRequest`/`resolvePlanContext` call, and additionally returns a new sanitized `purchases: [...]` array) and `POST /:id/images` (the actual Phase 44 atomic-upload-enforcement route — its capacity ceiling now includes the report's own add-on capacity, proven end-to-end: a Starter report already at its base-3 cap accepts more uploads once a +25 pack shows `fulfilled`, and does NOT without it). `POST /photos/stage`/`POST /generate` were deliberately left untouched — those are pre-report-creation, draft-based surfaces with no report to own a purchase against yet (add-ons are explicitly report-specific, never account-global, per the spec). A report whose effective capacity later DROPS below its current usage (simulated via a refund/dispute test) keeps every existing photo and only blocks further uploads — proven by test.

**Frontend (`frontend/src/pages/ReportPreviewPage.jsx`, extended — the natural home, since `POST /:id/images` itself has no other web UI yet; `frontend/src/utils/photoAddOnDisplay.js`, new, pure/Vitest-tested, matching the `photoCapacityDisplay.js`/`canonicalEstimateEditor.js` extraction convention):** a new "Add Photo Pack" button (owner-only, hidden on derivative document types exactly like the Repair-Estimate/Detailed-Estimate buttons beside it) opens `PhotoAddOnModal`, which loads `GET /:id/photo-capacity` (base/purchased/effective/used/remaining + sanitized purchase history) and `GET /payment/photo-packs` (sanitized catalogue) in parallel and renders every required state: loading, load-error+Retry, unlimited (no purchase needed), not-configured/ineligible (finalized/archived), available (pack list with per-pack disabled-if-unavailable + price), per-purchase-attempt button states (creating/redirecting/error/network_retry via `deriveCheckoutButtonLabel`), and a purchase-history list mapping every server `status` to a stable display bucket (`deriveSanitizedPurchaseDisplay`/`PURCHASE_STATUS_BUCKETS`) — a `pending`/`session_created` purchase is NEVER displayed as fulfilled (its own dedicated test). Buying calls `POST /payment/photo-pack-checkout` with only `{reportId, packId}` and redirects the browser to Stripe's own returned `url` — no client-side amount/price/capacity is ever constructed. After Stripe redirects back to `/reports/:id/preview?photoPackCheckout=success&intentId=...&session_id=...`, `ReportPreviewPage` (NOT the modal, so it fires even before the user re-opens it) strips the query string immediately (same pattern as `Dashboard.jsx`'s existing subscription-checkout redirect handling) and opens the modal with that `intentId`; the modal calls the new `POST /payment/photo-pack-checkout/:intentId/sync` endpoint (owner-checked, retrieves the session server-side, and ONLY IF Stripe reports it `paid` runs it through the exact same `fulfillCheckoutIntent` the webhook uses — never a separate, weaker fulfillment path) and then reloads capacity from the server. **The query string itself is never treated as proof of payment** — `shouldSyncAfterRedirect` returns only a boolean + an id to look up, nothing resembling a `paid`/`fulfilled` field a caller could misuse, proven by a dedicated test. The webhook remains the authoritative, required fulfillment path for reliability; the sync call only shortens the visible wait when the redirect lands first. `paymentAPI.getPhotoPacks`/`createPhotoPackCheckout`/`syncPhotoPackCheckout` added to `services/api.js`; `reportsAPI.getReportPhotoCapacity` (already existed from Phase 44, previously unused by any frontend surface) is now actually wired up.

**Exact automated validation (real run, 2026-09-19):** 5 new backend test files — `photo-addon-catalogue.test.js` (10, pure catalogue), `photo-addon-purchases-store.test.js` (22, direct transactional tests against the shared `FakeFirestore`, including the 6-way concurrency-barrier test and both out-of-order-dispute convergence tests), `photo-addon-checkout-route.test.js` (10, real Express app + real `payment.js` router, faking only `config/firebase` and the `stripe` package), `photo-addon-webhook.test.js` (12, same convention, including the existing-subscription-path regression test), `photo-addon-capacity-integration.test.js` (6, extends `photo-capacity-route.test.js`'s exact convention to prove the Phase 44×45 integration and the no-add-ons regression case) = **60 new tests**. Full backend suite: **956/956 passing** (0 regressions vs. this session's own 896/896 starting point — the delta is exactly the 60 new tests). Backend lint: **0 errors, 63 warnings** (unchanged from the verified `origin/main` baseline — no new warnings introduced). Frontend: 1 new file, `photoAddOnDisplay.test.js` (21 tests) — full suite **204/204 passing, 21/21 files** (0 regressions), lint **0 errors, 14 warnings** (unchanged baseline), clean production build. `git diff --check`: clean (one harmless LF→CRLF notice, same as every prior phase in this worktree). Manual secret/credential/Price-ID scan of every new/changed file: clean (the one `sk_live_` string match is a code comment describing the prefix-detection logic, not a real key). Scope scan: no Phase 46+/mobile code introduced; `mobile/` and the main working tree (`feature/mobile-initial-phases`, still exactly 53 uncommitted entries) confirmed byte-for-byte unchanged; worktree HEAD unchanged at `6a6df97`; nothing committed/staged/pushed/merged/deployed/opened as a PR.

**Exact live-Stripe-validation checklist (genuinely NOT done — Stripe account access has not been accepted):** (1) accept the client's Stripe account access securely; (2) verify the correct Stripe account and TEST mode; (3) create/confirm the 4 one-time Products/Prices at the confirmed amounts (499/799/1299/2499, usd); (4) store the 4 test Price IDs in `STRIPE_PRICE_PHOTOS_{25,50,100,250}_TEST`; (5) add `checkout.session.completed`/`.expired`, `charge.refunded`, `charge.dispute.created`/`.closed` to the EXISTING webhook endpoint's subscribed-events list (the endpoint/secret already exist for subscriptions — this is an event-type addition, not a new endpoint) and verify raw-body signature handling still passes for these new event types; (6) flip `PlanConfig.addOnsEnabled` to `true` (via `setPlanConfig`/a future Phase 48 admin action) only once the above is done; (7) complete one real test-mode Checkout purchase end-to-end and confirm `purchasedPhotoCapacity` activates ONLY after the webhook (not merely the redirect) fires; (8) redeliver the same webhook event from the Stripe dashboard and confirm no duplicate entitlement; (9) test an expired/abandoned checkout session and confirm zero capacity granted; (10) issue a real test refund and confirm capacity reverses safely, with existing photos untouched; (11) exercise the dispute lifecycle as far as Stripe's test tooling allows (create + close, both outcomes); (12) verify the pre-existing subscription webhooks are still completely unaffected end-to-end; (13) inspect real logs/responses for any secret/payment-data leakage; (14) configure LIVE Price IDs (`_LIVE` vars) and switch `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY` to a live key ONLY when the client explicitly authorizes going live; (15) remove any temporary local test credentials/artifacts; (16) only THEN mark Phase 45 fully **Complete** — not before.

**Client has instructed (standing, from prior sessions): do not commit, push, or deploy anything.** No live Stripe Products/Prices/keys were created or used; no network call to Stripe was made by the automated test suite (`stripe` itself is fully mocked in every test file). Phase 43's status/checklist is completely unchanged. Phase 46+ remain **Not Started**.

**Addendum, 2026-09-22 — real Stripe test-mode + Firebase-emulator live validation, Status → Complete:** performed entirely in this same worktree against Stripe **test mode** only (CLI authenticated solely to the Flacron Enterprises LLC sandbox) and a local Firebase Emulator Suite (demo-only project `demo-flacronai-test` — no real Firebase project touched). A temporary, env-gated (`VITE_USE_FIREBASE_EMULATOR=true`, no-op otherwise) emulator-connect block was added to `frontend/src/config/firebase.js` for the session and fully reverted afterward (confirmed zero diff via `git status`). `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY`/`STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET` were removed from the local `.env` at cleanup (never committed, never printed); the four `STRIPE_PRICE_PHOTOS_*_TEST` Price IDs were kept as legitimate local test config.

**Real bug found and fixed:** `applyRefund`/`applyDisputeCreated`/`applyDisputeClosed` (both branches) each wrote the intent document before reading the report document inside the same Firestore transaction — violating real Firestore's "all reads before any writes" rule (confirmed via the real `@google-cloud/firestore` SDK error against the emulator on every refund/dispute webhook; invisible to all 72 pre-existing mocked tests since the shared `FakeFirestore` double never enforced that ordering). Fixed by reordering each read before its write (zero logic change, matching `fulfillCheckoutIntent`'s already-correct pattern). Regression-proofed via a new opt-in `strictTransactionOrder` flag on `FakeFirestore` (default `false`, zero effect elsewhere) plus 4 new targeted tests.

**Live-confirmed, real HTTP + real Stripe test-mode objects + real webhook deliveries:** full purchase→webhook→capacity increase (25→50) in a real browser with a real test card; idempotent redelivery of `checkout.session.completed` (no double-grant); refund (full) correctly reverses capacity and is idempotent — this failed with a 500 before the fix above, confirmed working after; `checkout.session.expired` verified for `photos_50`/`photos_100` via real create-then-expire (no payment needed), idempotent; cancellation confirmed as "no webhook fires" by design (Stripe has no cancel event for one-time Checkout); `charge.dispute.created` (suspends within ~1s of fulfillment) and `charge.dispute.closed(lost)` (the only outcome Stripe's test API can trigger on demand — "won" has no force-trigger API, disclosed limitation, covered instead by mocked/regression tests) both confirmed correct and idempotent on redelivery. All four packs verified against real Stripe test-mode objects (`photos_25`=$4.99, `photos_50`=$7.99, `photos_100`=$12.99, `photos_250`=$24.99) using exactly two real paid checkouts (the minimum technically necessary — one for the base purchase/refund flow, one for the dispute flow) plus two unpaid create-then-expire sessions for price verification.

Full backend suite **1162/1163** — the one failure (`googleAddress: not_configured...`) is a pre-existing, unrelated Phase 46 env-state test-isolation artifact from `GOOGLE_MAPS_SERVER_KEY` already being set before this session began, not a Phase 45 regression. `git diff --check` clean (same harmless LF→CRLF notice as every prior phase).
