### 2026-09-19 — Phase 44 (Central Plan Configuration & Atomic Photo-Capacity Enforcement), previously "Current Active Phase", now superseded above by Phase 45 — kept below for its own historical detail, unchanged

**Phase 44 (Central Plan Configuration & Atomic Photo-Capacity Enforcement) was implemented 2026-09-19**, in the isolated worktree `.claude/worktrees/docs+web-estimating-phases`. Status: **Complete**. Phase 43's own entry (previously "Current Active Phase") is preserved below, unchanged, under its own historical heading.

**PlanConfig schema/source (`backend/config/planConfig.js`, new):** a single Firestore document, `planConfig/active` (deliberately one doc, not one-per-plan, so a read is always one consistent snapshot). Shape: `{schemaVersion, plans: {starter|professional|agency|enterprise: {reportsPerMonth, basePhotoLimit}}, addOnsEnabled, addOnPackSizes, addOnPackPrices, allowedMimeTypes, maxFileSizeBytes, watermarkPolicyEnabled, status, createdAt, updatedAt, updatedBy}`. Every limit is a non-negative integer or the explicit string `'unlimited'` (`UNLIMITED` constant) — never a magic number like `-1`. `validatePlanConfig()` rejects any single defect in the whole document (unrecognized/missing plan id, negative/fractional/string-typed limit, empty MIME list, non-positive file size, negative add-on price, unsupported `schemaVersion`) — never a partial/mixed-trust config. `getPlanConfig(db)` is a short in-process TTL cache (default 60s, `PLAN_CONFIG_CACHE_TTL_MS`, explicit NaN/negative-safe parsing so `=0` genuinely disables caching rather than silently falling back to 60s) with an explicit `invalidatePlanConfigCache()` called by the validated write path `setPlanConfig()` so a change takes effect on the very next read. **Safe-fallback contract (corrected 2026-09-19 — see the dated correction note below for the original bug):** a missing document, a failed validation, a thrown read, or an unsupported `schemaVersion` all resolve to `FALLBACK_CONFIG`, which **is** the accepted mapping — reportsPerMonth 5/50/200/unlimited (`tiers.js`'s own pre-Phase-44 values, unchanged) and Starter 25 / Professional 100 / Agency 250 / Enterprise unlimited for photo capacity. `RECOMMENDED_DEFAULT_CONFIG` holds the identical change-request values as the single source for seeding the real Firestore document — this worktree has no Firestore infra-as-config file (same situation Phase 43 documented for its own collection), so seeding `planConfig/active` in the real project remains a one-time manual/deployment step (`backend/scripts/seedPlanConfig.js`, not run), not something this phase's code does automatically. The OLD flat-100-for-every-tier behavior still exists as `LEGACY_ROLLBACK_CONFIG` (`status: 'legacy_rollback'`), but is reachable ONLY via the explicit `applyLegacyRollbackConfig(db, opts)` export (or `seedPlanConfig.js --legacy-rollback`) — never automatically. Both sanitized capacity endpoints return `configSource: 'firestore' | 'fallback'` (safe, non-sensitive) so the frontend/an operator can tell which source is live.

**Canonical plan mapping used:** `starter→25`, `professional→100`, `agency→250`, `enterprise→unlimited` — the repo's existing 4 tier ids from `tiers.js`'s `TIER_ORDER`; no new tier names were invented. `reportsPerMonth` (5/50/200/unlimited) is unchanged from `tiers.js`'s original values, now also centralized in the SAME PlanConfig document per task 1's instruction to centralize "already-duplicated report/export entitlement fields... safe to centralize now" — `tiers.js`'s static `TIERS` object is otherwise completely untouched (still used as-is by every pre-existing sync caller: `middleware/auth.js`, `routes/payment.js`, `routes/users.js`, and this file's own `getTier()`/`canGenerate()`).

**Effective-capacity formula:** `effectiveCapacity = basePlanCapacity + verifiedReportAddOnCapacity` (`resolveEffectivePhotoCapacity()` in `planConfig.js`) — unlimited short-circuits to `{capacity: Infinity, unlimited: true}` regardless of any add-on value; `verifiedAddOnCapacity` defaults to `0` everywhere in this phase (Phase 45 doesn't exist yet) and is NEVER read from a client-supplied value — only a future server-verified purchase record could ever populate it, and this phase's own resolver signature makes that the only way in. `resolvePlanContext(db, tierName, {verifiedAddOnCapacity})` is the one-call convenience every route actually uses, combining the live PlanConfig read + tier-limit lookup + capacity resolution, and additionally returning `reportsPerMonth` translated back to `tiers.js`'s own existing `-1`-means-unlimited sentinel (so `canGenerateAsync` needed zero changes to its comparison logic).

**`tiers.js` retrofit (task 3), scoped narrowly to avoid a wide blast radius:** `getTier()`/`canGenerate()` (both fully synchronous) are **completely unchanged** — every existing sync caller (`middleware/auth.js`'s `requireTier`, `payment.js`, `users.js`'s tier-info endpoint, the pre-existing `tiers.test.js`) keeps working identically, zero regression risk. Two NEW async siblings were added instead: `getEffectiveTier(db, tierName)` (same static tier object, `reportsPerMonth` overridden from live PlanConfig) and `canGenerateAsync(db, userTier, reportsThisMonth)`. Only the 5 actual monthly-quota ENFORCEMENT call sites in `backend/routes/reports.js` (the repair-estimate/invoice/coverage-letter/mold-supplement/main-generate routes, all sharing the identical `getTier`+`canGenerate` block) were switched to the async pair — display-only consumers (`payment.js`, `users.js`) were deliberately left reading the static value, since full cross-surface consistency is explicitly Phase 48's job per its own PHASES.md scope ("Cross-Surface Consistency"), not Phase 44's.

**Route-enforcement matrix (every photo-adding/staging route enumerated):**

| Route | Enforces capacity? | Where | Idempotency source | Rollback/release behavior |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `POST /photos/stage` | Yes (retargeted from flat `MAX_PHOTOS=100`) | Pre-check (`existingPhotos.filter(uploaded).length` vs live `resolvePhotoCapacityForRequest`) + authoritative transactional re-check inside `appendStagedPhoto` (now counts only `status==='uploaded'`, so a 'failed'/'duplicate' record never blocks on a full cap) | N/A (single-photo-per-request; existing content-hash dedup is the natural retry guard) | Reject with `MAX_PHOTOS`; the pre-check avoids uploading bytes for a common-case reject, the transaction is authoritative under contention |
| `DELETE /photos/stage/:draftId/:photoId` | N/A (release, not consume) | New `removeStagedPhoto()` (`photoDraftStaging.js`), made transactional (was a plain `get()`/`update()`) for the same reason `appendStagedPhoto` is | N/A | Capacity needs no explicit release step — it's always derived fresh from the current array length, so the delete itself IS the release |
| `POST /generate` | Yes (task 6: re-validated at COMMIT time, not just staging time) | After folding staged-draft + inline-upload photos together, `partitionRecordsByCapacity()` (pure) against a freshly-resolved live capacity — no transaction needed here since a brand-new report has no other writer to race against; the only real concurrency risk (a duplicate submission) is already closed by `claimDraftPhotos`' own atomic claim | The pre-existing `claimDraftPhotos` idempotent-claim (Phase 25/generate-perf fix), unchanged | Overflow photos are demoted to `status:'failed', capacityRejected:true`, excluded from `imagePaths`/`analyzableImages`, and their already-written Storage objects are deleted (best-effort, exact-object) |
| `POST /:id/images` | **Yes — the actual retrofit target** (previously ZERO capacity check, confirmed non-atomic read-append-write) | New `appendReportPhotosAtomic()` (`backend/utils/photoCapacity.js`), reusing `appendStagedPhoto`'s exact `tx.get→validate→tx.set` idiom | Client-supplied `attemptId` (multipart field) scoped to that report's own `photoUploadAttempts` map, keyed with a `computeBatchFingerprint()` (sorted content-hash list) so a genuine retry (same key + same content) replays with zero double-count, while the same key with DIFFERENT content is a hard `IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_CONFLICT`. A legacy client with no key gets a fresh server-generated `uuidv4()` PER REQUEST — explicitly NOT replay-safe (documented in the route's own comment), a deliberate, disclosed limitation rather than a claimed guarantee | Storage upload always happens BEFORE the transaction (same reasoning as `appendStagedPhoto`: a transaction retry must never re-run an upload); the transaction's `REPORT_FINALIZED`/capacity-exceeded/idempotency-conflict rejections all trigger an exact-object, best-effort orphan cleanup of whatever bytes were already written for that request |
| `POST /analyze-images` | No — confirmed, not a gap | N/A | N/A | Doesn't persist to any report's `photos` array at all; explicitly out of scope per the phase spec, re-confirmed by reading the route (it only ever returns an ephemeral analysis result) |

`/api` vs `/api/v1` alias duplication: confirmed a non-issue by construction — `server.js`'s `API_PREFIXES` mounts the SAME `reports.js` router object at both prefixes, so every route above (new or retrofitted) is automatically covered at both paths with zero extra code.

**Transaction/reservation design, honestly described:** this phase does NOT implement a separate two-phase "reserve, then upload, then finalize" pipeline — it reuses this codebase's own established, already-tested idiom (`appendStagedPhoto`): upload bytes first (cheap, deterministic Storage I/O, safe to leave outside a retryable transaction), then a SINGLE atomic Firestore transaction that reads the fresh committed state, re-checks content hashes against it (closing the race a pre-upload-only hash check can't), partitions the incoming batch against the live effective capacity, and commits — accept-and-write are the same atomic step, not a separate "reservation" record with its own lifecycle. This is a deliberate, disclosed design choice: it satisfies every one of the phase's atomicity/idempotency/no-overshoot requirements (proven by the concurrency-barrier tests below) without inventing new machinery beyond what this codebase already trusts.

**Usage-counting rule (defined once in `photoCapacity.js`, applied identically everywhere):** a record with `status==='uploaded'` consumes one unit; `'failed'`/`'duplicate'` never did; an excluded-from-analysis photo (`review.status==='excluded'`) still consumes (upload `status`, not the separate review lifecycle, drives counting); a thumbnail/original/display path is a FIELD on one record, never a second record, so it never double-counts; capacity is always DERIVED fresh from the live `photos` array (never a separately-maintained counter), so a delete's "release" is automatic by construction, with zero extra bookkeeping.

**Storage cleanup behavior:** every cleanup call is exact-object (`deleteObjects([originalPath, objectPath, thumbnailPath])`), never a prefix/wildcard delete; always best-effort (`.catch(...)`, logs only a report id + error message, never a signed URL/credential); idempotent by construction (deleting an already-deleted/nonexistent object is a no-op in the existing `config/storage.js` abstraction). **Bounded reconciliation boundary (disclosed, not built as new infrastructure):** if a cleanup call itself fails, the orphaned object is a pure Storage-cost/housekeeping issue, never a capacity-accounting one (the rejected record was already excluded from `photos`/`imagePaths` before cleanup was even attempted) — this repo has no existing scheduled-cleanup service to hook into (confirmed by checking), and the spec explicitly says not to build a new one for this scoped phase; a future prefix-scoped sweep (`users/{uid}/reports/{reportId}/` objects with no matching `photos[].objectPath`) is the natural follow-up, flagged here rather than built.

**Public API (task 8, sanitized, read-only):** two new GET routes sharing one response shape — `GET /reports/photo-capacity` (optional `?draftId=`, for the pre-generate wizard) and `GET /reports/:id/photo-capacity` (report-specific, for the post-generation "add more photos" surface). Response: `{plan, basePhotoLimit, addOnCapacity, effectiveCapacity, unlimited, used, remaining, uploadAllowed}` — `basePhotoLimit`/`effectiveCapacity`/`remaining` are explicitly `null` (never a magic number) when unlimited. Never returns Stripe price ids, secrets, env values, `addOnPackPrices`, or `status`/`updatedBy` — proven by a dedicated test. A `draftId` owned by someone else (or unknown) reads as an empty draft, never a 403/404 (draft existence isn't sensitive; a report id the caller can't access IS treated as 404, matching every other report route's convention via `getReportAccess`). Client-supplied capacity-shaped query params are proven ignored (a dedicated test passes `?basePhotoLimit=999999` and confirms it changes nothing).

**Frontend (Dashboard.jsx only — the sole surface with the hardcoded "100" wizard UI, confirmed by grep; marketing-copy "100 photos" mentions on Home/Features/PhotoAnalysis/FAQs/Security/ApiDocs are explicitly Phase 48's cross-surface-copy job, untouched here):** new `frontend/src/utils/photoCapacityDisplay.js` (pure, Vitest-tested, no React/rendering dependency, matching this codebase's established extraction convention for big-page logic) derives `{unlimited, effectiveLimit, atLimit, nearLimit, message, counterLabel, browseHint}` from the server's sanitized response — `null` (not-yet-loaded/failed fetch) safely falls back to the flat-100 display, mirroring the backend's own fallback contract. `Dashboard.jsx` gained a `photoCapacity` state + `refreshPhotoCapacity()` (called on mount and after every upload success/failure/duplicate/delete), replacing the old module-level `MAX_PHOTOS=100` constant (removed) everywhere it was previously enforced/displayed. Unlimited plans show "Unlimited" instead of a numeric cap; a new near-limit amber warning (last 3 slots) was added alongside the existing at-limit blocked state. `reportsAPI.getPhotoCapacity(draftId)` / `getReportPhotoCapacity(id)` added to `services/api.js`. No purchase/upgrade button (Phase 45) and no admin UI (Phase 48) were added, per the phase's explicit scope.

**Photo record metadata:** no new fields were needed beyond what Phase 6/24/41/42 already persist (`id/fileName/size/mimeType/status/contentHash/originalPath/objectPath/thumbnailPath/analysisStatus/analysis/review/position/qualityWarning/roomOrArea/annotations/relatedLineItemIds`) except one additive, transient marker — `capacityRejected: true` on a record demoted from `'uploaded'` to `'failed'` for exceeding capacity, so the frontend/tests can distinguish "rejected for capacity" from a genuine upload/corruption failure without a new field on every record. The idempotency ledger (`report.photoUploadAttempts: {[attemptId]: {fingerprint, photoIds, createdAt}}`) lives on the REPORT document, not on individual photo records, since it's an attempt-scoped fact, not a per-photo one.

**Backward compatibility:** a pre-Phase-44 report/draft with no `photoUploadAttempts` map or no `capacityRejected` field on any photo works identically to before — both are purely additive and default to absent/false everywhere they're read. A report already over a newly-lowered configured limit (e.g. an existing 80-photo Starter report after Starter dropped from the flat-100 fallback to the real 25) keeps every existing photo untouched (nothing in this phase's write path ever removes an existing photo for being "over limit") and simply blocks any FURTHER upload via the same `committedCount >= capacity` check every other over-limit case already uses. Legacy `imagePaths`/`photos` array syncing (`POST /:id/images`'s existing merge behavior) is unchanged in shape, only gated differently on write.

Full backend suite: **890/890 passing** (0 regressions vs. this session's 814/814 starting point — the delta is exactly the 76 new tests, confirmed by direct count). Backend lint: **0 errors, 65 warnings** (baseline 64; +1 net — two new `catch(err)`-without-using-`err` blocks in the two new GET routes, following this file's own already-pervasive, already-downgraded-to-warning pattern; renaming to `_err` was tried and confirmed NOT suppressible here since this ESLint config's `no-unused-vars` has no `caughtErrorsIgnorePattern`, so the existing convention of just accepting the warning was kept instead of a cosmetic, ultimately-ineffective rename). **A real, fixed-in-place bug found while writing tests:** `planConfig.js`'s cache-TTL env parsing used `Number(process.env.PLAN_CONFIG_CACHE_TTL_MS) || 60_000` — since `0` is falsy in JS, a deliberate `PLAN_CONFIG_CACHE_TTL_MS=0` (disable caching) would have silently been ignored and fallen back to the 60s default. Fixed to an explicit `Number.isFinite(...) && ... >= 0` check before writing any of this phase's own tests against it (caught by the test-writing process itself, not shipped).

**A real, fixed-in-place test-harness bug found while writing the route tests:** `middleware/auth.js` and `config/tiers.js` both destructure their Firestore/PlanConfig dependency at their OWN module-top-level, once. The route test file originally only reset `config/firebase`/`config/storage`/`routes/reports`/etc.'s `require.cache` entries between tests (mirroring `pricing-route.test.js`'s existing pattern) — but since THAT file's tests never vary a seeded user's tier across tests, it never exposed this. This file's Enterprise-vs-Starter test DID vary tier, and reproducibbly got back `tier:'starter'` on the "enterprise" test (the first test's stale cached `auth.js` module, still bound to the first test's fake Firestore instance). Fixed by additionally resetting `require.cache` for `middleware/auth.js`/`config/tiers.js` between installs — documented in the test file's own header comment so a future test author extending this pattern doesn't rediscover the same gap.

(2) `payment.js`/`users.js`'s tier-display endpoints still read the STATIC `tiers.js` reportsPerMonth value, not the live PlanConfig one — a deliberate, scoped decision (full cross-surface consistency is Phase 48's explicit job), not an oversight. (3) The real Firestore `planConfig/active` document has NOT been seeded in any real project by this session — `RECOMMENDED_DEFAULT_CONFIG`/`backend/scripts/seedPlanConfig.js` are ready to seed, but doing so is a deployment action, and the client's standing instruction (below) is not to deploy/commit/push. Until seeded, PRODUCTION now runs on the built-in accepted-mapping FALLBACK (Starter 25/Professional 100/Agency 250/Enterprise unlimited, original reportsPerMonth) — see the 2026-09-19 correction note below; this was flat-100 before that correction. (5) `POST /:id/images`'s advisory pre-finalized-check + authoritative transactional check together mean a request that arrives AFTER a report is already finalized (the common case) never uploads bytes at all; the rarer race (finalization happening DURING an in-flight upload) is what the transactional path's own orphan cleanup exists for, and is unit-tested directly against `appendReportPhotosAtomic`/via the idempotency-conflict route test, not via a live-timed race reproduction (which would be inherently flaky) — this is stated explicitly rather than glossed over.

### 2026-09-19 — focused Phase 44 PlanConfig activation/fallback correction

**The bug (real, confirmed).** `FALLBACK_CONFIG` reproduced the OLD flat-100-for-every-tier behavior, not the accepted mapping. Since `planConfig/active` was never seeded, a normal deployment would silently keep running flat-100 forever — expanding Starter from 25 to 100 by default, the opposite of the accepted requirement.

**Fix.** `FALLBACK_CONFIG` now equals the accepted mapping (`{...RECOMMENDED_DEFAULT_CONFIG, status: 'built_in_default'}`); the old flat-100 profile is renamed `LEGACY_ROLLBACK_CONFIG` and reachable only via the new explicit `applyLegacyRollbackConfig(db, opts)` export — never automatic. `reportsPerMonth` untouched. A valid authoritative Firestore doc still always overrides both built-in constants. Both capacity endpoints now also return `configSource`.

**Seed mechanism.** New `backend/scripts/seedPlanConfig.js` (idempotent, validates before writing, no embedded credentials, `--dry-run`/`--legacy-rollback` flags) — not run this session.

**Tests/validation.** 6 new/rewritten tests in `plan-config.test.js` (missing/malformed/read-failure/unsupported-version all assert Starter=25; explicit "can't expand to 100"/"can't grant unlimited" cases; legacy-rollback-is-explicit-only; seed idempotency) plus the no-doc route test in `photo-capacity-route.test.js` updated to expect 25/`'fallback'`. Targeted Phase 44 tests 100/100 passing. Full backend suite **896/896 passing** (0 regressions vs. 890/890; +6 new). Backend lint 0 errors, 65 warnings (unchanged). `git diff --check` clean. Secret scan clean. Frontend untouched (no frontend file changed — the new `configSource` field is additive and not yet consumed by the UI).

**Confirmed:** Phase 45+/`mobile/`/main working tree (53 entries) untouched; worktree HEAD unchanged; nothing committed/staged/pushed/merged/deployed/PR'd. Phase 44 remains **Complete**, now inclusive of this correction.

### 2026-09-19 — Phase 43 (OpenAI Preliminary Pricing Service), previously "Current Active Phase", now superseded above by Phase 44 — kept below for its own historical detail, unchanged

**Phase 43 (OpenAI Preliminary Pricing Service) was implemented 2026-09-19**, in the isolated worktree `.claude/worktrees/docs+web-estimating-phases`. Status is **"Implementation Complete — Live Provider Validation Pending"** (NOT "Completed") — no OpenAI API key exists in this worktree, so live-provider validation (the 11-point checklist below) is genuinely un-done, not just unmentioned.

**Architecture:** `backend/config/openai.js` (new) is a lazy, never-throws-at-require-time adapter modeled on `config/anthropic.js`, used ONLY by this feature — `aiService.js`'s Claude/watsonx report/vision pipeline is completely untouched, per PHASES.md Phase 43's own reconciliation note (the 2026-07-18 OpenAI removal was a provider-strategy choice for narrative/vision generation, not a ban on a new, isolated adapter). It builds a Chat Completions structured-output request (`response_format: {type:'json_schema', strict:true}`) and maps every SDK failure into one of 8 categorized `PRICING_*` codes (`.transient`/`.retryAfterMs`), logging raw SDK detail server-side only. `backend/services/pricingProviders/` holds a small provider CONTRACT (`generatePricing(normalizedInput, options) -> {items, meta}`) + `openaiPricingProvider.js` (thin — sanitized prompt/schema construction and raw-response parsing only, zero business validation) + `registry.js` (selects the ONE provider purely from server env `PRICING_PROVIDER`, never a client-supplied choice — a future `claudePricingProvider.js` plugs in without touching the route/service). `backend/services/pricingService.js` is the provider-agnostic orchestration: request validation, Firestore-backed caching (`pricingSuggestionsCache/{fingerprint}`, pure fingerprint function in `backend/utils/pricingFingerprint.js` hashing ONLY normalized locale+repair-scope fields — no reportId/userId/PII, proven by test, which is what makes a cache document safe to reuse across different reports/users by construction), bounded transient-only retry honoring `Retry-After`, strict per-item validation reusing `canonicalEstimate.js`'s own money/unit/currency primitives, and server-side `unitPrice`/`lineTotal` computation that never reads a provider-claimed total (the schema doesn't even request one).

**Structural safety design (not just a prompt rule):** the provider is asked ONLY for `materialUnitCost`/`laborUnitCost`/`equipmentUnitCost`/`trade`/`category`/`confidence`/`assumptions`. `room`/`damageType`/`repairAction`/`description`/`material`/`quantity`/`unit` always come from the caller's OWN request, never the provider's response — proven by a test injecting an adversarial provider response that tries to change the repair scope, showing the returned proposal item's scope is unchanged. This makes "AI never invents what is being repaired" structural rather than merely requested.

**Endpoint:** one new route, `POST /:id/estimate-detail/price-suggestions` on the existing `reports.js` router, following the `GET`/`PUT /:id/canonical-estimate` access pattern exactly (owner-with-`canEditReports` or `review`-grantee, 404/403/409-`REPORT_FINALIZED` before generation, a new dedicated `pricingLimiter` rate limit, an audit-log entry with counts/cache-status only — never prompt/response text). **Generates and returns a proposal only — persists nothing;** a finalized report is blocked from even generating one. Missing `OPENAI_API_KEY` → a clean 503 `PRICING_PROVIDER_UNAVAILABLE`, never a crash, and the rest of report editing/creation is entirely unaffected (proven by a test hitting a sibling route with the key unset).

**Frontend (extends, doesn't rebuild, Phase 42's editor):** `frontend/src/utils/pricingSuggestions.js` (new, pure, Vitest-tested — same no-jsdom convention as `canonicalEstimateEditor.js`) holds the request builder, a client pre-validator, an error classifier, and the safety-critical `mergeProposedPricingIntoLineItems` (matches a proposal to an existing draft item by `sourceProposalId` first — for idempotent re-apply, no duplicate on regenerate — then `targetLineItemId`; SKIPS, never overwrites, an item with `overrideActive === true`; appends a new draft item when unmatched). `ReportPreviewPage.jsx`'s `CanonicalEstimateEditor` gained a "Preliminary Pricing (Flacron Engine)" panel (hidden when `readOnly`; per-item "Price this" selection; country/state/city/postal inputs; Generate/Cancel(AbortController)/Regenerate/Retry; per-suggestion Accept checkboxes; Apply merges into the SAME draft state, never auto-saves) feeding into the pre-existing, completely unmodified Save button / `PUT /:id/canonical-estimate` / revision-conflict banner.

**Scoped completion of a Phase 41/42-flagged gap (2 minimal, additive, regression-tested edits to those "Complete" files):** (1) `canonicalEstimate.js`'s `validateLineItems` now preserves a submitted `providerModel` (3 length-capped strings, never money) instead of discarding it, so it survives accept→apply→save for audit — a legacy omitted-providerModel caller is unaffected (tested). (2) `validateAndComputeCanonicalEstimate`'s `pricingSourceMeta.source` is now computed server-side from the line items (`'ai_suggested'` if any is, else `'manual'`) instead of hardcoded `'manual'` — proven end-to-end through the real, unmodified `buildSection7DetailMarkdown` pipeline: an ai_suggested item now genuinely renders "Pricing Source: Pricing Source generated by: Flacron Engine"; an all-manual estimate is byte-identical to before. Nothing else in Phase 41/42 (the $550/$55/$44/$649 math, finalized-immutability, exports, watermarking) was touched.

**Privacy/security:** prompt payload is locale+repair-scope only (tested against a fabricated PII field list — none appear); a raw OpenAI SDK error never reaches an HTTP response, a report, or a log line outside `config/openai.js`'s own server-side `console.error`; the cache document schema (`{fingerprint,item,generatedAt,expiresAt}`) has no report/user/PII field (tested).

### 2026-09-19 — Phase 43 trust-boundary correction (same day, appended to the entry above without rewriting it — see `PHASES.md`'s matching correction entry for full detail)

A real, confirmed security gap in the just-landed Phase 43 was found and fixed the same day: the frontend echoed a client-held `providerModel` object back into the `PUT /:id/canonical-estimate` save payload, and `canonicalEstimate.js` preserved whatever the client sent there verbatim — a raw devtools/API call could set `providerModel:{provider:'OpenAI', model:'gpt-99-fake'}` on any line item with nothing server-side ever having verified it came from a real suggestion. A second, previously-unnoticed leak was also found and closed in the same pass: the generation endpoint was returning `providerModel` to the client in the HTTP response body (never rendered by the UI, but present and inspectable).

**Final, corrected design — opaque proposal references, trusted server-side.** A generated proposal is now ALSO persisted at `reports/{reportId}/pricingProposals/{proposalId}` (new `backend/utils/pricingProposalStore.js`, TTL `PRICING_PROPOSAL_TTL_MINUTES`, default 20 minutes) — a report/user-SCOPED sibling to Phase 41's own subcollections, deliberately kept separate from the report/user-AGNOSTIC `pricingSuggestionsCache` doc so that doc's own "no PII" guarantee is never touched. The generation response now returns a top-level opaque `proposalId` and each item its own opaque `suggestionId` (renamed from `proposalItemId`) — `providerModel` is stripped from every item before the response is built; the client no longer receives it at all. `PUT /:id/canonical-estimate` gained an optional `appliedProposals: [{proposalId, acceptedSuggestionIds}]` array; `canonicalEstimateStore.upsertCanonicalEstimate`'s transaction re-verifies each entry (existence/ownership/expiry/report-scope/revision) against the report's own proposal record BEFORE validating the estimate, and strictly AFTER the existing `REPORT_FINALIZED` guard. `canonicalEstimate.js` no longer reads `row.providerModel` from the request under any circumstance — a line item's trusted pricing metadata now comes ONLY from a fresh, atomically-reverified proposal match, a legitimate carry-forward of an already-trusted PREVIOUS revision's own record, or is downgraded to `manual` with `providerModel` reset to nulls. A matched suggestion also replaces (never trusts) the row's own submitted cost components with the proposal's stored values (tamper protection). An already-consumed suggestion can't be re-applied to a different line item (`PRICING_SUGGESTION_ALREADY_CONSUMED`); a pre-existing user override is never re-trusted by a matched suggestion, mirroring the frontend's own skip rule at the server boundary too.

**A genuine design bug was caught and fixed while writing the replay tests, not merely a documentation exercise:** an unconditional per-proposal `baseRevision` check would have thrown a spurious `REVISION_CONFLICT` on every save AFTER the first time a suggestion was applied (since the frontend keeps the applied-item's handles indefinitely, re-submitting the same `appliedProposals` entry on every later, unrelated save, while the proposal's own frozen `baseRevision` never advances). Fixed: the revision check now only fires when a call is freshly consuming a suggestion for the first time; re-affirming an already-consumed one against its original target line item is exempt and safe at any later revision. Locked in by a dedicated regression test.

**Verification:** new `backend/test/pricing-proposal-trust.test.js` (16 tests, covering the correction brief's full 15-point list plus the revision-check regression above); `pricing-service.test.js`/`pricing-route.test.js`/`pricing-canonical-integration.test.js` (backend) and `pricingSuggestions.test.js`/`canonicalEstimateEditor.test.js` (frontend) patched in place to reflect the new trust boundary rather than deleted. Backend full suite **814/814 passing** (0 regressions), lint 0 errors/64 warnings (unchanged baseline); frontend full suite **175/175 passing, 19/19 files**, lint 0 errors/14 warnings (unchanged baseline), clean production build; `git diff --check` clean; secret/privacy scan clean; scope-check confirmed the main working tree and every other worktree (`mobile/` included) untouched from this session's own starting snapshot; nothing committed/staged/pushed/merged/deployed/opened as a PR.

**Status stays EXACTLY** `Implementation Complete — Live Provider Validation Pending` — the 11-point live-OpenAI-key checklist is completely untouched by this correction and remains genuinely pending. **Client has instructed (standing): do not commit, push, or deploy anything.**

### 2026-09-19 — Phase 43 cleanup: `pricingProposals` Firestore TTL eligibility (same day, appended without rewriting the entries above — see `PHASES.md`'s matching entry for full detail)

An `expiresAt` field alone doesn't get a document auto-deleted — Firestore TTL only ever fires on a native `Timestamp`-typed field, and `pricingProposalStore.js` was storing `expiresAt` as a plain ISO string. The 20-minute *application-level* expiry check (already correct, unchanged, still what actually blocks an expired proposal from being used) is not the same guarantee as Firestore physically deleting the document. Fixed: `expiresAt` is now stored as `Timestamp.fromDate(...)` (reusing `backend/config/firebase.js`'s existing `Timestamp` export, no new dependency); the public ISO-string value returned to the API response is unchanged. **Automatic deletion is still an explicit, un-done deployment step** — this repo has no Firestore infra-as-config file to declare a TTL policy in, so the exact manual step (`gcloud firestore fields ttls update expiresAt --collection-group=pricingProposals --enable-ttl --project=<id>`, or Firebase Console → Firestore → Indexes → TTL) is now recorded as item 2 of `PHASES.md`'s Phase 43 live-validation/deployment checklist. No scheduled cleanup job or lazy delete was added (out of scope, unnecessary — the app-level check already neutralizes an expired proposal regardless of physical deletion timing). Targeted re-run: **87/87 passing, 0 regressions** (`pricing-proposal-trust`, `pricing-service`, `pricing-route`, `pricing-canonical-integration`, `canonical-estimate-persistence`); `git diff --check` clean. Status unchanged: `Implementation Complete — Live Provider Validation Pending`.

### 2026-09-19 — Phase 42 implementation (2026-09-18, corrected 2026-09-19), superseded above as the "Current Active Phase" now that Phase 43 has landed; kept below for its own historical detail

**Phase 42 (Section 7 Rendering, Editor & Report Integration) was implemented 2026-09-18, then corrected 2026-09-19** via a focused completion audit (4 checks), in the isolated worktree `.claude/worktrees/docs+web-estimating-phases`. This renders Phase 41's canonical structured estimate as the report's detailed Section 7 (PDF/DOCX/HTML/desktop preview) and provides the web editor — no AI pricing (still Phase 43) and no change to how Phase 41 calculates anything.

**Architecture:** a pure module `backend/utils/canonicalEstimateContent.js` renders the canonical estimate into ONE normalized markdown string and splices it into an in-memory copy of the report's `content` (never persisted) at the "## SECTION 7" heading. Every export format already parses that same markdown dialect out of `report.content`, so the new detail inherits correct pagination, TOC page-tracking, table-header repeat, and per-format escaping with zero new rendering code. **Selection rule (corrected 2026-09-19): the canonical detail REPLACES the legacy AI-narrative Section 7 body — the two are never rendered together.** `injectSection7Detail` discards everything between the Section 7 heading line and the next top-level heading, replacing it with the detail markdown; the heading line itself and every following section (8+) are untouched. `backend/routes/reports.js`'s export route fetches the canonical estimate once, builds the detail markdown, and passes a shallow-cloned `renderReport` to whichever generator the requested format calls. The frontend desktop preview (`ReportPreviewPage.jsx`) now follows the identical rule: `GET /:id/canonical-estimate` also returns the server-computed `detailMarkdown`, and a small frontend mirror of the same splice algorithm (`frontend/src/utils/canonicalEstimateContent.js`, pure, no deps, tested independently) applies it before rendering — so PDF/DOCX/HTML/desktop preview all show exactly one Section 7 body, consistently.

**Section 7 structure:** a "### Detailed Repair Estimate Breakdown" block (pricing-source/status/revision/currency metadata) replaces the legacy narrative, then one "### Area: {room}" sub-heading per room with a "**Trade / Category: {trade}**" block per trade (a compact table plus an extended per-item bullet covering material, component-cost breakdown, taxable/confidence/status, override provenance, evidence count, assumptions), and a closing "### Financial Summary" table reproducing Phase 41's exact server totals with an explicit note that O&P is never folded into the tax basis.

**Editor:** `frontend/src/pages/ReportPreviewPage.jsx`'s `CanonicalEstimateEditor` (a "Detailed Estimate (Section 7)" button next to Repair Estimate, on the same primary-report document types). Full create/edit/reorder/remove line items with stable client-generated IDs, cost-component inputs with a live computed unit price, a provenance-preserving override, permits/GC/manual-adjustments lists, O&P%/tax%, and a bidirectional evidence-photo picker. Handles loading/load-error+Retry/empty/saving (double-submit-guarded)/preview-replaced-by-server-totals/validation errors/409 conflict (Discard & Reload)/409 finalized+read-only/403 unauthorized/network+Retry/unsaved-changes warning. **Corrected 2026-09-19:** all of this logic was extracted out of the React component into a pure, directly-tested module `frontend/src/utils/canonicalEstimateEditor.js` (this codebase has no React component-render test infrastructure at all — no `@testing-library/react`/`jsdom` anywhere — so the established convention here, matching `estimateInvoiceEligibility.js`/`invoiceTotals.js`, is to test the extracted pure logic directly rather than render the component).

**Bidirectional evidence (corrected 2026-09-19):** Phase 41's photo→line-item reverse index (`photo.relatedLineItemIds`) was persisted but never exposed anywhere. `GET /:id/photos` now returns it, and the shared `PhotoReview.jsx` component (already backing both `Dashboard.jsx`'s gallery and the standalone `/photos` library) shows a small "Linked to N estimate line item(s)" badge — one shared touch-point, no gallery redesign, no duplicated estimate data (count-only, no descriptions fetched).

**Server authority:** `canonicalEstimateStore.upsertCanonicalEstimate`'s optional `body.baseRevision` optimistic-concurrency check (omit it and last-write-wins is preserved) rejects a stale write as `REVISION_CONFLICT` (409) before any validation runs. Every other guarantee (client totals ignored, finalized immutability, ownership/role checks) is Phase 41's, unchanged.

**Legacy fallback:** a report with no canonical estimate (or one with no line items) renders Section 7 exactly as before — the markdown builder returns `''`, making the injector a documented no-op, proven byte-identical by test.

**Test results (post-correction, 2026-09-19):** backend **697/697 passing** (0 regressions; +3 from this audit's replace-not-append regression tests, on top of the 20 already added 2026-09-18), 0 lint errors (64 pre-existing warnings). Frontend **143/143 passing, 18/18 test files** (+45 new this audit: 41 in `canonicalEstimateEditor.test.js`, 4 in `canonicalEstimateContent.test.js`), 0 lint errors (14 pre-existing warnings), clean production build. The previously-reported "94/94 (1 pre-existing environment-only failure)" is now fully resolved and explained: the failing file (`apiMfaHeader.test.js`) crashed at module-load time (`FirebaseError: auth/invalid-api-key`, not a test assertion) because no `frontend/.env` existed in this worktree; supplying a syntactically-valid placeholder `VITE_FIREBASE_API_KEY` (format-only check, no real project/network needed) resolved it with zero code changes. A local-only, gitignored `frontend/.env` with placeholder values was added to this worktree so this and future sessions get the true, complete count rather than an ambiguous partial one.

**Honest limitation:** the editor UI was not visually exercised in a running browser (no browser-automation tool available) — verified via its now-dedicated unit-test coverage plus the underlying API's own tests, not a live click-through. Exports show evidence as a text reference (count + detail bullet), not embedded per-line-item thumbnails — a deliberate scope-minimizing choice against potentially hundreds of line items × up to 30 photos each; the desktop editor and the photo-review surface's new badge both show real linkage, just not photo thumbnails inside exports.

### 2026-09-18 — Phase 41 implementation, superseded above as the "Current Active Phase" now that Phase 42 has landed; kept below for its own historical detail

**Phase 41 (Canonical Structured Estimate Data Model & Calculation Engine) was implemented 2026-09-18, per explicit instruction ("implement Phase 41 only").** This is backend data-model/calculation-engine work only — no Section 7 UI/rendering (that's Phase 42) and no AI pricing (Phase 43).

**Architecture:** the primary report owns/links its canonical, versioned structured estimate as a NEW, separate lifecycle from the existing standalone `RepairEstimate` document (`backend/routes/reports.js`'s `/:id/estimate` routes, `backend/utils/estimateCalculations.js`) — per the approved Option C decision, `RepairEstimate` was reused only for its calculation *style*, never touched, never coupled to the new lifecycle. There is exactly one canonical estimate per report, and one authoritative server-side recomputation path — the client can never persist a total.

**Schema** (`backend/utils/canonicalEstimate.js`, pure/no Firestore): estimate-level — `schemaVersion` (versioned; an unsupported future version is rejected, not silently accepted), `reportId`, `status` ('draft' — no transitions yet, Phase 42+ hook), `currency` (ISO 4217, one per estimate, default `USD`), `pricingSourceMeta`/`pricingStatus` (placeholders for Phase 43's AI pricing), `locationContext` (generic region string, per this phase's own explicit scope limit — no property-data integration yet), `lineItems[]`, `permits[]`/`generalConditions[]`/`manualAdjustments[]` (each a labeled, contractor/adjuster-entered amount — never AI-generated), `totals` (every rollup figure, all cents-based), `userOverrideIndicators`, `revision`, `createdAt`/`updatedAt`. Line-item fields: `id` (stable, duplicate-rejected), `trade`, `category`, `room`, `damageType`, `repairAction`, `description`, `material`, `quantity`, `unit` (validated against a configurable `UNIT_VOCABULARY` superset), `materialUnitCost`/`laborUnitCost`/`equipmentUnitCost` (explicit **per-unit** components), `componentSumCents`, `unitPrice`/`lineTotal` (+ Cents mirrors), `taxable`, `currency`, `assumptions`, `confidence`, `pricingSource`, `providerModel` (Phase 43 placeholder), `lineStatus` ('preliminary'|'confirmed'), `userOverride` (`{active, originalUnitPriceCents, reason, overriddenBy, overriddenAt}` — provenance always preserved, never silently overwritten), `evidencePhotoIds[]`, `createdAt`/`updatedAt` (createdAt survives an update when the line-item id is reused).

**Calculation contract:** `unitPrice = materialUnitCost + laborUnitCost + equipmentUnitCost` (cents) unless a valid, reasoned `userOverride` is present; a submitted `unitPrice` that doesn't reconcile to the component sum (beyond 1-cent rounding tolerance) is rejected, not silently replaced. `lineTotal = round(quantity × unitPriceCents)`, integer cents throughout. **Exact roll-up order** (documented in `canonicalEstimate.js`'s header comment; **corrected 2026-09-18** — see "Correction" note below): (1) line-item subtotal → (2) + permits → (3) + general conditions → (4) + manual adjustments = **direct cost** → (5) taxable basis (ONLY taxable-flagged line items + taxable-flagged permits/GC/adjustments — **O&P is deliberately excluded**) → (6) tax (rate × taxable basis) → (7) overhead/profit (rate × direct cost, computed independently of tax; rate is contractor/adjuster-entered, never AI-decided) → (8) **final total** = direct cost + overhead/profit + tax, each added exactly once. Category/room/trade summaries are informational aggregates derived fresh from the same line items every time (never a separately maintained, driftable copy) and are asserted in tests to reconcile exactly with the line-item subtotal — no double counting. The server never reads a client-submitted `totals`/`grandTotal` field at all.

**Correction (2026-09-18, same-day focused review):** the original Phase 41 writeup incorrectly stated that overhead/profit was folded into the sales-tax basis. Inspection of the two existing, already-verified calculation modules — `estimateCalculations.js`'s `computeTotals` and `invoiceCalculations.js`'s `computeInvoiceTotals` (whose own comment states outright: "never itself taxed -- `taxableCents` below is computed purely from the services line items, unchanged") — confirmed the application's authoritative rule is that **O&P is added to the final total but is never part of the tax basis** (verified reference case: $550.00 taxable services, O&P 10% = $55.00, tax = 8% of $550.00 = $44.00, final total = $649.00 — NOT 8% of $605.00). `canonicalEstimate.js`'s `computeRollups` was not yet exercised by any route/UI outside its own test suite, so this was corrected in place (not treated as a breaking change to any persisted data): `taxableBasisCents` no longer includes `overheadProfitCents`. A dedicated regression test reproducing the exact $550/$55/$44/$649 case, plus tests proving O&P is added exactly once, is never itself taxed, that non-taxable line items are excluded from the tax basis, and that an explicitly-taxable permit is correctly included, were added to `canonical-estimate-calculations.test.js`. Permits/general conditions/manual adjustments were already, and remain, governed by their own explicit per-entry `taxable` flag (defaulted — permits non-taxable, GC/adjustments taxable — but always caller-overridable), so no entry is silently taxed by fiat. No jurisdiction-specific evidence was found requiring O&P to ever be taxed; if that need arises later, the recommended approach is an explicit `overheadProfitTaxable` flag on the estimate, not a hardcoded change to this default.


**Evidence traceability:** a line item's `evidencePhotoIds` are validated against the SAME report's own `photos[]` array (read fresh inside the write transaction) — a photo ID from another report structurally cannot pass, since it's simply never in that set. The reverse index (`photos[].relatedLineItemIds`) is recomputed fresh from the just-validated line items on every write and persisted onto the parent report doc in the same transaction.

**API/persistence:** `backend/utils/canonicalEstimateStore.js` persists to a new singleton doc `reports/{id}/canonicalEstimate/current` plus a bounded (subcollection, not an unbounded array — the exact `RepairEstimate.revisionHistory` gap this phase was told not to repeat) audit trail at `reports/{id}/canonicalEstimateAudit/*`. Reuses `appendStagedPhoto`'s transactional idiom (`db.runTransaction`, 15 max attempts) for concurrency safety. Two new routes on `backend/routes/reports.js`, mounted (like every other report route) under both `server.js`'s `API_PREFIXES` — primary versioned path `GET`/`PUT /api/v1/reports/:id/canonical-estimate`, with `/api/reports/:id/canonical-estimate` continuing to work as the backward-compatible alias: `GET` (owner or any view+ share/assignment grant; returns `{estimate: null, legacy: true}` when none exists — the legacy-fallback contract) and `PUT` (owner-with-`canEditReports` or review-grantee, mirroring `PUT /:id/estimate`'s existing access pattern; returns 409 `REPORT_FINALIZED` when the parent report's status is `finalized`, reusing the exact same rule as the existing `isFinalizedContentEdit` guard — Golden Rule #3 immutability). A rejected write (validation, mixed-currency, unknown photo, finalized-report) never partially applies — confirmed by test.

**Backward compatibility:** a report with no `canonicalEstimate` subdoc simply returns `{estimate: null, legacy: true}` — no destructive migration, no fabricated line items from old narrative content, no historical total ever touched. `RepairEstimate`/Invoice documents and their routes/calculations are completely unmodified.

**Test results (post-correction):** 41 new backend tests (31 pure calculation/validation in `canonical-estimate-calculations.test.js`, 10 transactional persistence in `canonical-estimate-persistence.test.js` against a new shared `backend/test/helpers/fakeFirestore.js`), all passing with exact hand-verified cent values, including the verified $550/$55/$44/$649 reference case. Full backend suite: 671/671 passing (0 regressions). Backend lint: 0 errors (64 pre-existing warnings, unchanged). No frontend file was touched (Phase 42 owns the UI), so no frontend re-test was needed. `git diff --check` clean; no secrets in the diff.

**Known, deliberate limitation (disclosed, not hidden):** true multi-request transactional contention (two near-simultaneous `PUT`s racing on the same report) is exercised only by a sequential-build-on-prior-revision test here, not a barrier-forced concurrent-interleaving repro — `upsertCanonicalEstimate` reuses the identical `db.runTransaction(..., {maxAttempts})` contract that `photoDraftStaging.appendStagedPhoto` already has a dedicated repro for (`photo-draft-staging.test.js`), so the underlying retry primitive is already covered; a dedicated repro for this specific store was judged a low-value duplicate of that existing coverage given the phase's scope.

### 2026-09-18 — Phase 40 implementation, superseded above as the "Current Active Phase" now that Phase 41 has landed; kept below for its own historical detail

**Phase 40 (Watermark Policy Centralization & Approval-Workflow Verification) was implemented 2026-09-18, per explicit instruction ("implement Phase 40 only").** Open Question #16 (Starter-tier branding-watermark-after-approval policy) is now **RESOLVED** by explicit client confirmation: *"Yes, the watermark should stay after approval on the free version."* Confirmed policy: an un-reviewed draft always shows the "DRAFT — PENDING ADJUSTER REVIEW" watermark on any tier; a reviewed/finalized (or legacy `approved`/`completed`) report on Starter/free keeps the "Generated by FlacronAI — Upgrade to remove watermark" branding mark; a reviewed report on a paid, non-watermarked tier exports clean. This was already very close to the codebase's actual pre-existing behavior — the real work was centralizing the previously-inline logic into one authoritative resolver and closing two consistency gaps found along the way.

Implementation: a new pure function `resolveWatermarkPolicy({ reportStatus, tierWatermark })` in `backend/utils/watermarkPolicy.js` (→ `{ show, kind: 'draft'|'branding'|'none', text }`), reading only the server-persisted report status and server-resolved tier config — never a client-supplied field. `backend/routes/reports.js`'s export route now calls it once and threads the same result through the PDF watermark overlay, the DOCX generator, and the HTML export (previously three separately-consumed copies of an inline `tier.watermark || draftWatermark` expression). Its reviewed-status set (`finalized`/`approved`/`completed`) is now shared, via an exported `REVIEWED_STATUSES`, with `reports.js`'s pre-existing `isReviewed()` gate so the two can never drift apart.

**Real bug found and fixed via the new tests (not in original scope):** `properPdfGenerator.js`'s and `documentGenerator.js`'s cover-page "Status" field had its own separate check for the literal string `'finalized'` only — a legacy `approved`/`completed` report (already correctly treated as reviewed by the watermark/export-gate logic) would still show a "DRAFT — PENDING ADJUSTER REVIEW" badge on its own cover page. Both now use the shared `REVIEWED_STATUSES` set too.

**Frontend copy fix:** `ReportPreviewPage.jsx`, `Dashboard.jsx`, and `EnterpriseDashboard.jsx` each fired an unconditional `toast.success('...exports are now clean')` on approval — false for Starter. All three now branch on the signed-in user's tier to state the Starter-keeps-branding-watermark reality explicitly.

Full validation: 629/629 backend tests passing (20 new, covering the full policy matrix plus real rendered PDF/DOCX/HTML output inspection), 0 backend lint errors, 94/94 real frontend tests passing (1 pre-existing unrelated environment-only failure — missing Firebase key in this isolated worktree, not present in the main checkout), 0 frontend lint errors, clean frontend production build. See `PHASES.md`'s Phase 40 section for the complete writeup (files changed, exact test list, manual verification detail).

**Client has instructed (standing, from prior sessions): do not commit, push, or deploy anything.** Phase 41 and every later phase (through 49) remain **Not Started**, per this session's explicit instruction to implement only Phase 40.

**Home.jsx completion:** `features` array 6→9 (added Report Templates, Team Roles & Permissions, Usage Analytics); `steps` array 3→6, rewritten to match the real pipeline (claim/property details → photo upload → FLACRON ENGINE drafting → per-photo review → editor → approve/export); a new "Analyze the Entire Inspection" section added below the product screenshot (photo-grid/counter/progress-bar illustration, satisfying both the "Photo Analysis section" and "Analyze the Entire Inspection section" task bullets as one section rather than duplicating near-identical content); a "Try It Free" primary CTA added next to the pre-existing sample-report download link (kept, not removed).

**Engine-naming standardization:** resolved the 3-variant inconsistency ("Flacron Engine" / "FlacronAI Engine" / "FLACRON ENGINE") to a single canonical **FLACRON ENGINE** everywhere — 12 frontend files via mechanical `sed` replacement (About/CookiesPolicy/EnterpriseDashboard/FAQs/Footer/Dashboard/SharedReport/ReportPreviewPage/PrivacyPolicy/PhotoReview/TermsOfService/Home) plus targeted edits to the 3 backend files that bake the name into generated documents/prompts: `aiService.js`'s report-generation prompt text and "Prepared By" line, `documentGenerator.js`'s DOCX cover table + sign-off paragraph, `properPdfGenerator.js`'s PDF cover table + footer line + sign-off paragraph. ### 2026-08-19 (session 27) — Phase 22 implementation, superseded above as the "Current Active Phase" now that Phase 23 has landed; kept below for its own historical detail

**Phase 22 (Photo Analysis Library) was implemented 2026-08-19 (session 27), per explicit client instruction ("Start Phase 22 only").** No new client-facing scope decisions were needed this session — every design choice (bounded owned+assigned report scan mirroring Phase 20's `search.js` precedent, an opaque offset-encoded cursor given there's no dedicated photos collection, reusing Phase 8's exact review UI) was resolvable from precedent already established in Phases 8/14/19/20 and documented inline.

**Data model constraint:** no dedicated `photos` Firestore collection exists — every photo lives inside its owning report's `photos[]` array (or a legacy flat `imagePaths[]`). The new `GET /api/photos` endpoint (`backend/routes/photos.js`) does a bounded, permission-scoped scan of the caller's own + Phase-19-assigned reports (never a wider organization pool, exactly mirroring `search.js`), flattens/redacts each via a new pure, unit-tested module (`backend/utils/photoLibrary.js`), then filters (claim/location/category/analysis-status/included-excluded/date range)/searches/sorts/paginates that already-authorized in-memory window with a bounded, honestly `truncated`-flagged offset cursor.

**Server-side permission enforcement:** every entry's `canReview` is computed per-report and never trusted from the client — the owner path additionally requires `hasCapability(req.user, 'canEditReports')` (so a restricted team role stays read-only even on a report they Firestore-level "own"), the assignee path requires exactly the Phase 19 'review' permission tier.

**Three pre-existing endpoints needed permission fixes as a direct Phase 22 dependency:** `GET /:id/photos` and `GET /:id/photos/:photoId/image` previously 404'd for ANY non-owner (even a legitimate Phase 19 assignee) — tightened to `getReportAccess`, matching `GET /:id`'s existing model. `PUT /:id/photos/:photoId/review` had owner-only middleware stacked on a hardcoded owner-only service check — rewritten to the same dual-path pattern (owner+`canEditReports`, OR assignee with 'review' tier) `PUT /:id` already established. A raw-Storage-path leak in `GET /:id`'s embedded photos array and the review PUT's response (`objectPath`/`thumbnailPath`/`originalPath`/`imagePaths` returned verbatim) was also closed.

**Frontend:** a new `/photos` page (`frontend/src/pages/PhotoLibrary.jsx`) with filters, debounced search, sort, a live result count, Clear Filters, a responsive grid with cursor-based "Load More" and bounded lazy thumbnail fetching (no `react-window` added — judged unnecessary per `PHASES.md` Phase 27's own guidance). The expanded photo view reuses **Phase 8's exact review UI**, extracted verbatim from Dashboard.jsx's `ReportPhotoGallery` into a new shared `frontend/src/components/PhotoReview.jsx` (zero behavior change to the existing report editor) and generalized with a `canReview` prop for the read-only case. Nav entries added to `Navbar.jsx` and `EnterpriseDashboard.jsx`'s sidebar.

**Verification:** 243/243 backend unit tests pass (15 new, `photo-library.test.js`), 0 backend lint errors (59 pre-existing warnings, unchanged). 17/17 frontend unit tests pass (unchanged), 0 frontend lint errors (14 pre-existing warnings, unchanged), a clean production build. A 50/50-check real-browser Playwright pass against a full seeded account matrix (owner with mixed categories/statuses/an excluded photo/a needs-attention photo/a failed upload/a legacy report/a 35-photo pagination-volume report; a view-tier assignee confirmed read-only both in UI and server-side; a review-tier assignee confirmed their edit genuinely persists; a restricted-role owner confirmed read-only despite Firestore-level ownership; an empty account; a cross-organization stranger confirmed fully isolated via direct API IDOR checks; a suspended team member blocked at the auth layer; an unauthenticated direct-URL redirect) covered every filter/search/sort/pagination/keyboard-accessibility/mobile-viewport path with zero console errors and zero failed requests.

**Four real product bugs were found and fixed during this verification** — none discovered by a simulated user report; all caught by deliberately constructing seed scenarios (a shared report, a restricted-role owner, a cross-org stranger) that exercise exactly the access paths Phase 22 newly opens: (1) the two owner-only 404s above, (2) the review-action dual-path gap, (3) the raw-Storage-path leak, (4) an initial `canReview` derivation that used permission tier alone (owner/review) without the owner-path capability check — would have rendered live Edit/Approve/Exclude controls for a restricted role that would then 403 on click. Several apparent failures during verification were investigated and confirmed to be test-script-only artifacts, not product bugs: fake never-uploaded Storage paths in seed data (fixed by uploading real tiny JPEG bytes for the photos the browser pass actually opens); a Playwright selector that unintentionally also matched Navbar's always-in-DOM mobile menu toggle, whose resulting exception then hung the script indefinitely because the browser was never closed on error (fixed the selector + added a hard try/finally + explicit `process.exit()`); a `waitForResponse` predicate that didn't account for the frontend's versioned `/api/v1` axios base; and React 18 StrictMode's dev-only double-invoked mount effect legitimately aborting and refiring the initial fetch (an already-documented, production-safe pattern in this codebase per `SectionedReportEditor.jsx`'s own comment).

**Client has instructed (standing, from prior sessions): do not commit, push, or deploy anything.** (Phase 22 was confirmed and implemented in session 27 — see above; the client has not yet authorized Phase 23.)

### 2026-08-19 (session 26) — Phase 21 implementation, superseded above as the "Current Active Phase" now that Phase 22 has landed; kept below for its own historical detail

**Phase 21 (Onboarding Flow) was implemented 2026-08-19 (session 26), per explicit client instruction ("Start Phase 21 only").** No new client-facing scope decisions were needed this session — every design choice (existing-vs-new-user detection with zero backfill, enterprise-subdomain exclusion, provider-agnostic gating) was resolvable from precedent already established in Phases 3/14/19/20 and documented inline.

**Persistence:** 4 new fields (`onboardingCompleted`/`onboardingStep`/`onboardingUserType`/`onboardingMonthlyVolume`, plus `onboardingCompletedAt`/`onboardingSkipReason` once finished) live directly on the existing `users/{uid}` doc — seeded ONLY at the exact instant `GET /api/users/profile`'s pre-existing (Phase 3) auto-create transaction first creates a brand-new doc. An existing account's doc already exists and never passes through that branch, so it simply has no `onboardingCompleted` field at all — every gate treats that absence exactly like "already done," which is what makes "existing users must not be incorrectly forced through onboarding" hold with zero migration/backfill risk, and what makes Google vs. email/password signup behave identically (the field's origin has nothing to do with which auth provider created the account).

**Backend** (`backend/utils/onboarding.js` + 2 new routes in `backend/routes/users.js`): `POST /api/users/onboarding/step` validates the 6-value user-type enum and 4-value volume enum, clamps step to 0-4, and enforces prerequisites server-side (step ≥2 needs a user-type already on file, step ≥3 needs volume already on file) — a direct API call can't jump ahead of what a disabled Next button already prevents client-side. `POST /api/users/onboarding/complete` requires both mandatory answers on file (400 `ONBOARDING_INCOMPLETE`) unless called with `{skipped:true}` — used only by the enterprise-subdomain flow. Both routes operate exclusively on `req.user.uid` (no id/target param anywhere), so cross-account modification is structurally impossible; both are idempotent once already completed (`{alreadyCompleted:true}`, never an error or a state regression) — the multi-tab case this phase explicitly calls out.

**Frontend gate:** one new check in `ProtectedRoute.jsx` (`onboardingCompleted === false` → redirect to `/onboarding`) added to the SAME central gate every authenticated route already passes through, so direct URL access to ANY protected page while incomplete redirects back to onboarding, not just `/dashboard`; `Onboarding.jsx` itself handles the inverse (already done → bounce to `/dashboard`).

**The 5-step wizard** (`frontend/src/pages/Onboarding.jsx`): Welcome → user-type (6 icon cards) → monthly volume (4 options) → optional Invite Team (rendered only when `tier==='enterprise'`, reusing Phase 14's exact `teamsAPI.invite`/`getRoles` calls, independently 403'd server-side regardless of what the UI renders) → "Generate Your First Report" CTA, which completes onboarding then navigates to `/dashboard?startWizard=1` — read by `Dashboard.jsx` the same way it already reads the pre-existing `?openReport=` deep-link. Every step transition persists to the server before advancing, with `refreshProfile()` called after each one; a `ready` gate blocks all rendering until the server's step/answers sync into local state once, preventing a flash of step 0 for an account resuming later. Step numbering (0-4) stays fixed regardless of team-invite eligibility.

**Enterprise-subdomain exclusion:** `EnterpriseOnboarding.jsx` now calls `getProfile()` then `completeOnboarding({skipped:true, reason:'enterprise_subdomain'})` immediately after a successful register, before ever reaching `/dashboard` — matching `Auth.jsx`'s own established `getProfile()`-first ordering.

**Verification:** 228/228 backend unit tests pass (5 new, `onboarding.test.js`), 0 backend lint errors (59 pre-existing warnings, unchanged). 17/17 frontend unit tests pass (unchanged), 0 frontend lint errors (14 pre-existing warnings, unchanged), a clean production build. A 30/30-check isolated live-API test covered every enum/step/prerequisite validation path, idempotent-after-completion behavior, the enterprise-subdomain skip path, and confirmed `GET /teams/roles` genuinely 403s a non-enterprise caller. **Real-browser automation was genuinely available this session** (Playwright's Chromium was already installed — unlike the documented gap in Phases 7/13/14/15/19/20) — a 16/16-check Playwright pass against the real dev servers drove real Firebase-Admin-created, pre-verified throwaway accounts through the full flow at both desktop and mobile viewports: new signup → onboarding, forward/back with selections retained, the team step correctly never appearing for a starter-tier account, mid-flow refresh resuming at the exact same step with no flash, direct URL access to `/settings` while incomplete redirecting back, completion handing off cleanly into the real report wizard, `/onboarding` never showing again after completion, and an existing account landing straight on `/dashboard` — zero console errors, zero failed network requests. Google-provider signup itself couldn't be safely automated (a real interactive OAuth consent flow isn't something to script with a throwaway account) — verified instead by code review confirming the gate is provider-agnostic by construction. **Two real issues were found and fixed during the browser pass:** (1) a stale-tab-after-completion race where `persistStep`/`handleFinish` ignored the backend's `alreadyCompleted` flag — fixed to redirect on it; (2) disabled Next/Back buttons had no visual disabled state at all — fixed with explicit `disabled:opacity-50` classes. A third apparent issue (washed-out screenshots) was investigated and disproven as a real bug — purely a framer-motion fade-in-timing artifact of the test script. All seeded data (Firestore + real Firebase Auth accounts) was deleted afterward, independently re-verified with a zero-residue check; this session's own frontend dev server was stopped afterward, the pre-existing backend server was left running untouched.

**Client has instructed (standing, from prior sessions): do not commit, push, or deploy anything.** (Phase 21 was confirmed and implemented in session 26 — see above; the client has not yet authorized Phase 22.)

### 2026-08-19 (session 25) — Phase 20 implementation, superseded above as the "Current Active Phase" now that Phase 21 has landed; kept below for its own historical detail

**Phase 20 (Notifications Center & Global Search) was implemented 2026-08-19 (session 25), per explicit client instruction ("Start Phase 20 only").** No new client-facing scope decisions were needed this session — every design choice was resolvable from precedent Phases 18/19 already established (documented inline, not asked as an open question).

**Notifications:** a new top-level `notifications` collection (`backend/utils/notificationService.js`) backs a real bell/feed — `GET /api/notifications` (bounded window + JS-sorted pagination, the same no-composite-index-available constraint/pattern as Phase 19's `assigned-to-me`), `POST /api/notifications/:id/read` (transactional, ownership-checked, clamped-at-zero counter), `POST /api/notifications/mark-all-read` (one bounded 500-write batch). Unread count lives on `users/{uid}.unreadNotificationCount` (an incremented/decremented counter, never a live count query) for O(1) badge reads.

**All 8 spec'd events fire from their real trigger sites**, each reusing a Phase 18 preference key wherever one already exists: analysis completed/failed and report generated/"ready for review" (the real background pipeline, `photoJobService.js` — these two are the same instant in this codebase's state machine, since a report becomes `draft` exactly when generation completes), review requested/declined and report approved and report-access-granted (`reports.js`, reusing `reviewRequested`/`reportShared` exactly as Phase 19's own emails already do), export completed (`reports.js` — deliberately **ungated**, no Phase 18 toggle covers it, and it's a direct receipt of the caller's own click) and team invitation (`teams.js` — also ungated, matching the pre-existing email's own posture; fires only when the invited email already belongs to an existing account), and subscription issue (`payment.js`'s webhook, reusing `billing`, plus a dedupeKey on the Stripe invoice id as defense-in-depth). `analysis_failed` has no dedicated toggle either — deliberately reuses `analysisCompleted` rather than fabricating a 7th key, the same reuse-not-fabricate posture Phase 18/19 established. A `dedupeKey` option on the shared `notifyUser()` helper gives true at-most-once semantics at the 2 sites with a genuine double-fire risk; every other site already has its own natural one-shot guard upstream.

**Global search:** `GET /api/search` (`backend/routes/search.js`) is scoped EXACTLY to Phase 19's own report-access model — the caller's own reports plus reports specifically assigned to them, never an organization's wider pool, satisfying this phase's own "never expose the entire organization's report pool" requirement. No Firestore full-text search exists, so this is a bounded (300-doc cap per source), permission-scoped read followed by an in-memory substring match — not "client-side filtering," since the scoping/matching both happen server-side and only matches are ever returned. Matches claim number/insured/address/loss-type and, within the same already-authorized reports, photo filename/AI category/location (photos are a sub-array of the report doc, not a separate collection yet — that's Phase 22's job); team-member results are additionally gated by the same `canManageTeam`/`canViewAllProfiles` capability the existing roster already requires.

**Frontend:** `NotificationBell.jsx` (poll + open-refetch, mark-one/all, pagination, the Phase 17/19-established monotonic-requestId stale-response guard) and `GlobalSearch.jsx` (CMD/CTRL+K + a `flacron:open-search` DOM event, 300ms debounce + a genuine `AbortController` cancel, grouped keyboard-navigable results, plain-text `<mark>` highlighting) are mounted in `Navbar.jsx` (every authenticated page) and `GlobalSearch` is additionally mounted once at the `App.jsx` root; `EnterpriseDashboard.jsx`'s separate sidebar layout got its own bell + search trigger too.

**Verification:** 223/223 backend unit tests pass (6 new, `notification-service.test.js`), 0 backend lint errors (59 pre-existing warnings, unchanged). 17/17 frontend unit tests pass (unchanged), 0 frontend lint errors (14 pre-existing warnings, unchanged), a clean production build. **No browser automation tool was available this session** (same documented gap as Phases 7/13/14/15/19). A 35/35-check isolated live-API test (ephemeral Express app + the real Firestore project, email + AI calls monkey-patched to safe mocks) exercised every event via its REAL trigger path (the actual background pipeline, not a simulated write), pagination/unread-count/mark-read/mark-all-read, a true dedupe collision, and the full search matrix including cross-organization/stranger isolation. Two checks initially failed and were root-caused as test-harness artifacts, not product bugs (a malformed seed photo tripped a real Firestore constraint that production AI output can never actually produce; two fire-and-forget writes needed a longer grace period before the test's own follow-up query, not a blocking await added to the response) — both confirmed via debug instrumentation, then re-verified clean. All seeded data was deleted afterward with a verified zero-residue re-check; `git status` shows only the expected new/modified files.
