### 2026-08-19 (session 24) — Phase 19 implementation, superseded above as the "Current Active Phase" now that Phase 20 has landed; kept below for its own historical detail

**Phase 19 (Sharing Permissions, Expiry, Comments & Review Requests) was implemented 2026-08-19 (session 24), per explicit client instruction ("Start Phase 19 only").** Three genuinely open design questions were presented with concrete, code-grounded recommendations and explicitly confirmed by the client BEFORE any implementation began: (1) direct "Invite User" sharing is scoped to *existing* FlacronAI accounts only this phase — the full invite-a-stranger-by-email + accept-at-signup flow (mirroring `teams.js`'s pending-invite machinery) is a documented follow-up, not built here; (2) Comment/Review-permission shares may target a not-yet-finalized draft report, provided the shared page always shows an explicit "DRAFT — pending review" banner, since that's what makes the supervisor review-request workflow usable *before* finalization — View-permission links stay exactly as finalized-only as before (Golden Rule #3); (3) an anonymous Review-permission link gets elevated comment-management (resolve/reopen) but never a formal approve/reject — that attestation is reserved for an authenticated, identified reviewer (the review-request workflow, or a named Invite-User grant), never a no-login link.

**Sharing:** the legacy single-token, view-only, non-expiring link (`shareToken`/`sharedAt`, `POST/DELETE /:id/share`, `GET /shared/:token`) is untouched for backward compatibility. A new top-level `reportShares` collection (deliberately NOT a subcollection, so a public token lookup stays a single-field equality query needing no new Firestore index — this session has no ability to deploy one) backs `POST/GET /:id/shares` + `DELETE /:id/shares/:shareId`: View/Comment/Review permission tiers, optional 24h/7d/30d server-computed expiry, enforced live (never cached) on every fetch.

**Direct Invite User sharing** (`POST/DELETE /:id/share/invite[/:uid]`) grants a named existing FlacronAI account per-report access via a new `assignedUsers`/`assignedUserUids` pair on the report doc, independent of team membership — works cross-organization by design.

**Supervisor review requests:** `POST /:id/request-review` (owner assigns an in-organization reviewer — same-org membership enforced via `resolveOrganizationId`, cross-org rejected with `REVIEWER_NOT_IN_ORG`), `POST /:id/review-response` (the assigned reviewer declines or asks for changes; approval itself reuses the pre-existing `POST /:id/approve`, now reachable by a `review`-tier grantee), and a new `GET /reports/assigned-to-me` (a strict array-contains query on the caller's own uid, proven live to never leak the organization's wider report pool). The `reviewer` role's capability flags already existed from Phase 14 with a doc comment noting cross-member access was "Phase 19 — not built yet"; the only missing piece was the per-report grant itself.

**Access-control design:** a new `backend/utils/reportAccess.js` (`getReportAccess`/`hasReportAccess`) is the single source of truth for what a non-owner may do on one specific report — deliberately independent of the grantee's own account role/tier, since every solo account resolves to "owner" of itself in `orgRoles.js` and would otherwise leak full capability onto someone else's report. Ownership checks were surgically widened (never blanket-loosened) on exactly `GET /:id`, `PUT /:id`, and `POST /:id/approve` — `requireCanEditReports`/`requireCanApprove` middleware was removed from the latter two and replaced with an inline check reproducing the exact prior owner-path behavior. Export/download, delete, duplicate, restore, and the AI-assist routes were deliberately left strictly owner-only — a documented scope trim, not an oversight.

**Comments:** a new `reports/{id}/comments` subcollection (matching the existing `versions` subcollection precedent) with add/view/reply(one level)/resolve/reopen, anchored to a section by a content-based title slug (`slugifySectionTitle`) rather than position, since `parseReportSections` recomputes purely-positional `section-N` ids on every parse — a slug survives reordering, and only a literal rename/removal breaks the anchor, falling back to a "General" bucket with the original title preserved for context. An anonymous Comment/Review-permission link can add comments via a guest display name (never an authUid); only Review-permission (anonymous or authenticated) or the owner can resolve/reopen; View-only access is blocked from comments entirely.

**Notifications:** `reviewRequested` (defined by Phase 18 but never wired) now genuinely fires on `request-review`, gated by the REVIEWER's own preference, via a new `sendReviewRequestedEmail`; a new `sendReviewResponseEmail` notifies the owner on decline/changes-requested, reusing the same `reviewRequested` toggle for both directions (a deliberate scope choice — no new toggle key was fabricated for a feature not yet exposed in Settings' UI); a new `sendReportAccessGrantedEmail` notifies a direct-invite recipient, gated by their own `reportShared` preference.

**Security:** raw share tokens are never written to an audit-log `meta` (only `shareId`/`permission`/`claimNumber` — live-verified against 24 real audit entries across 4 real tokens); comment bodies are control-character-stripped/length-capped (`sanitizeCommentBody`) and rendered only as plain React text nodes (never `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`); tokens/ids have no enumeration surface; every widened or new route returns a uniform `404 NOT_FOUND` to a party with no grant at all, never a data-revealing 403.

**Frontend:** new `ShareReportModal.jsx` (link creation with permission/expiry, list/copy/revoke, Invite User) and `CommentsPanel.jsx` (shared verbatim between `ReportPreviewPage.jsx`'s authenticated view and `SharedReport.jsx`'s anonymous view, reusing Phase 17's `AuditLogViewer.jsx`-established monotonic-request-id guard against stale-response races). `ReportPreviewPage.jsx` gained Share/Request-Review buttons, an in-review banner, and Approve/Return-for-Changes/Decline actions for an assigned reviewer, every action gated by the server-returned `myAccess` field on `GET /:id`, never assumed client-side. `SharedReport.jsx` gained expired/revoked states and the DRAFT banner. `Dashboard.jsx`'s home view gained a small "Shared With You" widget, rendered only when non-empty.

**Verification.** Backend: 217/217 unit tests pass (10 new, `report-access.test.js`), 0 lint errors (59 pre-existing warnings, unchanged baseline). Frontend: 17/17 unit tests pass (2 new), 0 lint errors (14 pre-existing warnings, unchanged baseline), a clean production build. **No browser automation tool was available this session** (same documented gap as Phases 7/13/14/15) — the frontend UI layer was verified by code review + the clean build only. A 51/51-check isolated live-API test (an ephemeral Express app on its own port + the real Firestore project, `emailService.js`'s 5 relevant send functions monkey-patched to no-op spies BEFORE `reports.js` was first required, so no real SES email was ever sent, and no real Anthropic call was made since no AI/generation route was ever exercised) covered every permission level/expiry option, direct invites at all 3 tiers plus an upgrade, anonymous guest comments, expired (`410`)/revoked (`404`) access, add/reply/resolve/reopen both authenticated and anonymous, the full review-request→changes-requested→re-request→edit→approve lifecycle, a non-assigned member and the owner both correctly refused at `review-response`, a suspended member blocked entirely at the auth layer, cross-organization access refused in both directions with `assigned-to-me` proven to never leak beyond the one specifically granted report, and server-side denial via direct API calls for every restricted case (never relying on a hidden UI button as the only gate). **One check initially failed and was root-caused as a test-ordering bug, not a product bug:** the cross-org-rejection assertion ran against a report an earlier assertion had already put into a `pending`-review state, so the correct `REVIEW_ALREADY_PENDING` guard fired first; re-run in isolation against a fresh report, the exact `REVIEWER_NOT_IN_ORG` rejection passed cleanly. All seeded data (9 users, 3 reports, their `reportShares`/`comments`/`auditLogs`) was deleted afterward, independently re-verified with a zero-residue Firestore query pass; the two temporary verification scripts (run from the backend directory so `require()` could resolve `node_modules`) were deleted before the session ended — `git status` shows only the expected new/modified source files.

**Client has instructed (standing, from prior sessions): do not commit, push, or deploy anything.** (Phase 20 was subsequently confirmed and implemented in session 25 — see the current entry above.)

### 2026-08-19 (session 23) — Phase 18 implementation, superseded above as the "Current Active Phase" now that Phase 19 has landed; kept below for its own historical detail

**Phase 18 (Settings Completion) was implemented 2026-08-19 (session 23), per explicit client instruction ("Start Phase 18 only").** Two genuinely unresolved open questions from `PHASES.md` — Branding tab tier-gating and account-export scope — were presented to the client with concrete, code-grounded proposals and explicitly confirmed BEFORE any implementation began, per the client's own instruction to stop and ask rather than guess at either one.

**Branding (resolved):** visible to every tier now, not Enterprise-only. Non-Enterprise accounts get Company Name (moved here from the Profile tab, since it's really an org-scoped field) + a new Report Footer Text field, both genuinely wired into the PDF/DOCX/HTML export pipeline, plus an honest "Upgrade to Enterprise" prompt — no fabricated access. Enterprise accounts get a summary card linking out to the already-built `/white-label` portal rather than a second, duplicated branding surface (the same "summary tabs link out" pattern Phase 17 established for Members/Templates/Usage). Full logo/color/custom-domain white-label stayed exactly as Enterprise-gated as it already was in `tiers.js`/`whitelabel.js` — no entitlement was expanded.

**A real, pre-existing defect (flagged but left unfixed by Phase 13) was fixed in the process:** `reports.js`'s export-branding resolution only ever suppressed the literal "FlacronAI" placeholder for a TEMPLATE's own branding, never for a real company name the user/org had set themselves — so a non-enterprise user's Company Name silently never appeared in their own exports. Fixed: `hideFlacronBranding` now engages whenever ANY company name resolves (white-label, org, or template), and company/footer resolution now checks the ORGANIZATION (`resolveOrganizationId`) rather than only the caller's own doc — a solo account is its own organization (no behavior change, no extra Firestore read), but a team member's export now correctly inherits the org's Settings instead of their own likely-blank profile fields.

**Account export (resolved):** a single JSON export via `GET /api/users/export-data` — profile/settings (incl. the new Organization sub-object), this account's own reports (metadata + full content + photo/document references, never re-embedding binary bytes), personal templates, owned CRM records, and safe API-key/webhook metadata. Explicitly excludes `mfaSecret`/recovery-code hashes/`tokenVersion`/`stripeCustomerId`/API-key `keyHash`/webhook signing secrets (found via a targeted grep — `webhookEndpoints` docs store a raw, unhashed `secret` field, a real credential that must never appear in a self-export) and any other account's data. Audit-logged (`account_data_exported`) same as everything else in this app.

**Notifications** expanded from the old 3 placeholder preferences (never actually wired to anything) to the full spec'd 6: `reportCompleted`/`analysisCompleted`/`reviewRequested`/`reportApproved`/`reportShared`/`billing`. A new shared `backend/utils/notificationPrefs.js` is the single source of truth for the 6 keys (sanitizes/strips unknown keys, defaults every key to enabled — opt-out, not opt-in, since these are transactional account-activity emails, not marketing, so Golden Rule #5 doesn't require opt-in here). Four NEW gated email hooks were wired in: report-completed and analysis-completed inside `photoJobService.js`'s real generation pipeline, report-approved in `reports.js`'s `/:id/approve`, report-shared in `/:id/share` (only on first link creation, not every re-open). The pre-existing payment-failed email (`payment.js`) was gated by the new `billing` preference too. `reviewRequested` is persisted and shown in the UI but honestly labeled "No trigger yet" — no supervisor-review-request feature exists yet (that's Phase 19 territory), so fabricating a working toggle for it would have violated Golden Rule #1.

**Data tab:** a real, bounded bulk-delete (`POST /api/reports/archived/delete-all`) permanently removes every ALREADY-ARCHIVED report (Storage files included) — deliberately scoped to reports the user already marked for removal via Archive, never touching drafts/finalized reports, and distinct from the pre-existing full-account-deletion flow (left untouched, still in Settings' Security tab). An honest retention-policy disclosure (no automatic expiry exists — stated plainly, not glossed over) rounds out the tab.

**Server-side enforcement:** Organization-field writes are gated by the pre-existing Phase 14 `canManageTeam` capability (Owner/Admin/Manager) — a restricted role gets a real read-only view (disabled inputs, no Save button, an explicit "Only Owners, Admins, and Managers can edit" note), never a silently-broken form or a raw 403 page. All new fields (company/website/industry/address/timezone/reportFooter) are validated server-side (trimmed, length-capped) via express-validator, matching every other endpoint in this codebase.

**Verification.** Backend: 207/207 unit tests pass (7 new for `notificationPrefs.js`), 0 lint errors. Frontend: 0 lint errors, a clean production build. A 41/41-check isolated live-API test (an ephemeral Express app on its own port + a real Firestore project, with `emailService.js`'s send functions and `aiService.js`'s AI-calling functions monkey-patched to no-op spies BEFORE the routers were first required — so no real email was ever sent despite AWS SES creds being live/production here, and no real Anthropic call was made) proved: organization role-gating and cross-org isolation exactly as designed; the export-branding fix for both a solo account and a team member inheriting the org's branding; notification-preference sanitization (unknown keys stripped, non-boolean values coerced, untouched keys default-enabled); all 4 new gated email hooks genuinely turning on and off with their preference — including inside the real (AI-mocked) generation pipeline, not just at the API layer; the account-data export's shape/ownership/secret-exclusion; and the archived-reports bulk-delete (scoped correctly, drafts untouched, empty-set handled cleanly). All seeded data was deleted afterward with a verified zero-residue re-check.

**Real-browser (Playwright, the system's real installed Chromium) verification covered every account type the client asked for** — solo Professional, solo Agency, Enterprise Owner, Enterprise Admin, Enterprise Manager, a restricted Adjuster, a suspended member, and a second cross-organization Enterprise Owner — with every distinct check passing cleanly in at least one full run (most across several): full Settings tab walkthroughs, Organization-field edit+save+persist-across-reload, the Branding tab's tier-appropriate content, all 6 notification toggles present and a toggle-off round-tripping through a reload, the Agency-vs-Professional API-tab gating difference, a real triggered file download from "Export My Data," the delete-archived-reports confirm/cancel modal flow, a restricted role's read-only Organization view, a suspended member's login not crashing the app, and cross-organization isolation. Mobile viewport (390×844) confirmed no horizontal overflow.

**Test-environment friction encountered and honestly resolved, not mistaken for a product bug:** (1) the already-flagged (Phase 16) cookie-consent-banner UX defect intercepted real clicks in the test script, worked around there (not fixed — still out of scope); (2) the shared dev server's rate limiter was exhausted twice by the sheer request volume of 8 sequential real logins plus full tab walkthroughs in one script run — resolved (with explicit user confirmation each time, since a prior session this same day had found genuine concurrent-session interference and the user asked to be careful about backend restarts) by restarting the local dev server, and by splitting the verification into two smaller batches to stay under budget; (3) a few individual browser checks showed intermittent timing flakiness (an org-data-load check firing before an extra Firestore round-trip for a team member finished, framer-motion's exit-animation keeping a "closed" modal mounted for a beat) — each was root-caused, and in every case the exact same underlying behavior was ALSO proven correct either by the isolated API-level test or by another clean browser run, distinguishing generic Playwright/dev-server timing noise from an actual defect.

### 2026-08-19 (session 22) — Phase 17 implementation, superseded above as the "Current Active Phase" now that Phase 18 has landed; kept below for its own historical detail

**Phase 17 (Organization Admin & Audit Log Viewer) was implemented and fully closed out 2026-08-19 (session 22), including a real Golden Rule #1 fix (real logins weren't recording `login_success` at all before this session's work) and a clean, complete 49/49 real-browser verification pass.**

Backend: `backend/services/organizationService.js` is a pure, unit-tested aggregation layer (mirroring `analyticsService.js`'s established pattern) plus thin Firestore-orchestrating wrappers, consumed by three new routes in `backend/routes/organization.js` (all mounted under `authenticateToken` + `requireTier('enterprise')` + a `canViewAllProfiles` (Owner/Admin/Manager) gate, matching the existing `teams.js`/`templates.js` precedent that this whole surface is admin-only, not personal): `GET /metrics` (users/reports/photos/org-template counts + a role breakdown, with storage and departments HONESTLY reported as `{available: false}` per Golden Rule #1 rather than fabricated), `GET /security-summary` (MFA adoption percent, suspended-member count, legacy-`editor`-role count), and `GET /audit-logs` (filterable by date range/action/actorUid/targetType, paginated, defaulting to the last 90 days to keep the underlying full-collection-per-org-roster fetch bounded in practice — the same disclosed tradeoff `analyticsService.js` already documents elsewhere in this codebase). A separate new `GET /api/users/login-history` (`backend/routes/users.js`) is available to **every** account regardless of tier or role — it finally puts real data behind `Security.jsx`'s pre-existing claim ("Settings → Security → Login History"), scoped strictly to the caller's own uid and only login-shaped actions (`login_success`/`login_failed`/`suspicious_login_new_device`/`logout`), never the noisier org-wide audit stream.

A defense-in-depth safety net was added, not a fix for a found leak: `redactMeta()` recursively blanks any audit-log `meta` key that looks like a secret/token/password/credential/api-key (case-insensitive, one level of nested-object recursion) before a log entry is ever serialized to a client. A dedicated code audit confirmed none of the ~41 existing `recordAuditLog()` call sites currently pass a sensitive value — this guards against a future call site being added carelessly, matching the same "prove it first, then harden" discipline Phase 16's SSRF fix used.

Frontend: `frontend/src/pages/OrganizationAdmin.jsx` (`/organization`) is a 6-tab page (Members/Teams/Templates/Usage/Security/Audit Logs). Per this phase's own risk note ("avoid two divergent enterprise-admin surfaces," aligned with Phase 14's decision to keep team management inside `EnterpriseDashboard` rather than rebuilding it standalone), the Members/Templates/Usage tabs are real org-scoped **summaries** that link out to the already-built, already-tested `EnterpriseDashboard`/`Templates.jsx`/`Analytics.jsx` pages for actual mutation — nothing here re-implements invite/role-change/template-editing a second time. Teams (a role-breakdown bar chart) and Security (the MFA/suspension/legacy-role summary) are genuinely new content that exists nowhere else. A shared `frontend/src/components/AuditLogViewer.jsx` (range chips incl. a custom date picker, action/actor/resource-type filters, an expandable per-row detail view, pagination) is embedded in the Audit Logs tab AND rendered standalone at a second route, `/audit-logs` — "two doors, one component," not a duplicated implementation. `Settings.jsx`'s Security tab gained a real "Login History" section (paginated, wired to the new endpoint) so `Security.jsx`'s existing claim is now backed by an actual feature rather than needing a copy-softening fix (task 3 of this phase's own task list was resolved by building the view, which was judged better than downgrading honest copy to match a gap).

**A real Golden Rule #1 gap, found by building the Login History view honestly rather than assuming the data would be there:** `AuthContext.jsx`'s real login flow calls the Firebase client SDK directly and never touched any backend route, so `login_success` had only ever been recorded for MFA-enabled accounts (via the separate, pre-existing `/mfa/verify` route) — every ordinary login was invisible to its own "Login History." Fixed by reviving the previously-100%-dead `POST /api/auth/verify` endpoint (it now conditionally calls `recordAuditLog({action:'login_success',...})` only when `!userProfile.mfaEnabled`, so MFA accounts are never double-counted) and wiring `login()`/`loginWithGoogle()` in `AuthContext.jsx` to fire-and-forget-call it immediately after a real Firebase sign-in succeeds.

**Two more real bugs, both found only through live-browser testing (neither caught by lint, unit tests, or the production build):** (1) `OrganizationAdmin.jsx`'s Usage and Security tabs crashed on their very first render — before the lazy-fetch `useEffect` had run, `usageData`/`securityData` were still `null`, and the loading branch's condition only guarded the *error* sub-case, not the null-data case, so `usageData.metrics.x` threw straight into the `ErrorBoundary`. Fixed by widening the loading-branch condition to `loading || !data`. (2) `AuditLogViewer.jsx` had a genuine race condition: changing filters rapidly could let a slower, stale request's response land after a newer, correct one and silently overwrite it, since nothing sequenced the responses. Fixed with a `useRef`-based monotonically-incrementing `latestRequestId` guard applied to all three promise branches (`.then`/`.catch`/`.finally`), so only the response matching the most recently issued request is ever applied to state.

**Verification.** Backend: 200/200 unit tests pass (11 new, covering `redactMeta`/`shapeAuditLog`/`filterAuditLogs`/`paginate`/`buildRoleBreakdown`), 0 lint errors/44 warnings (unchanged pre-existing baseline). Frontend: 15/15 unit tests pass, 0 lint errors/14 warnings (unchanged baseline), a clean production build. **Full real-browser (Playwright, the system's real installed Chrome) verification: 49/49 checks passing in one complete, uninterrupted 8-phase run**, covering every account type the phase called for against a real Firestore project (a fresh throwaway org with Owner/Admin/Manager/Adjuster/a suspended member, a fully separate second org, and an unrelated solo Professional-tier account): Owner — direct navigation, a hard refresh, all 6 tabs, the 5 real-action filter checks plus a narrowing-to-exactly-1-row filter check, row-expand with a confirmed no-secret-leak, a real live report-approval action appearing in the audit trail within seconds via the app's own Refresh button, the standalone `/audit-logs` entry point surviving a refresh, and a real "Login History" section in Settings → Security with real entries; Admin and Manager — identical full same-org access; a restricted Adjuster — denied at both `/organization` and `/audit-logs` with the correct message, while their own personal Login History still works; a suspended member — blocked before ever reaching a working dashboard; the second organization's Owner — correct roster size, zero cross-org data leakage in either metrics or audit entries; the Professional-tier account — a tier-upgrade prompt, not the admin page; unauthenticated access — redirected to `/login` from both routes; and a 390×844 mobile viewport — zero horizontal overflow on either page, zero console errors. Zero unexpected console errors and zero 5xx responses throughout.

Two test-script bugs (not product bugs, confirmed by cross-checking directly against Firestore and the live API) were found and fixed en route to this clean run: a fixed 600ms wait that could sample the audit table while it was still showing its loading skeleton (fixed by waiting for an actual table row instead), and an unscoped `getByText` lookup that was deterministically matching a hidden `<option>` inside the closed action-filter `<select>` — which renders the same formatted label text — instead of the real, visible table row (fixed by scoping the lookup to the table body). A genuine process-level crash was also found and fixed in the test harness itself: the 8-phase script never closed most of its Playwright browser contexts across the many accounts it drove, and the accumulated memory eventually triggered a Node/V8 "Zone Allocation failed - process out of memory" crash near the end of a full run — fixed by closing each context as soon as its phase finishes using it. All QA data (10 Firebase Auth accounts across 2 organizations, their `users`/`enterpriseTeams` docs, the seeded report + 6 version subdocs, the organization template, the webhook endpoint, and 65 accumulated `auditLogs` entries) was deleted afterward via a dedicated cleanup script, with a verified zero-residue re-check and a clean `git status`.

### 2026-08-18 (session 21) — Phase 16 implementation, superseded above as the "Current Active Phase" now that Phase 17 has landed; kept below for its own historical detail

**Phase 16 (Integrations Page & Webhook Management UI) was implemented 2026-08-18 (session 21), per explicit client instruction ("Start Phase 16 only").** This session had real browser automation available for the first time (Playwright driving the system's real installed Chrome via `channel: 'chrome'`) — every prior phase's live verification was API-level only, with frontend rendering verified by code review + a production build. This mattered directly: live testing against a real browser and a real running server is what caught both real bugs described below, neither of which a code-review pass or an API-only test would have surfaced.

**A real, serious SSRF gap was found and fixed while verifying the "preserve existing SSRF protection" instruction from the client's own task list — not a pre-planned task.** The original inline `isSafeWebhookUrl` in `backend/routes/webhooks.js` only blocked the single literal string `'127.0.0.1'` (not the other ~16.7 million addresses in `127.0.0.0/8`), had a dead IPv6 loopback check (compared against `'::1'` but Node's URL parser returns `'[::1]'` with brackets, so the comparison could never match), and had no DNS-rebinding protection at all (a wildcard-DNS hostname resolving straight to a blocked IP would sail through, since the check only ever inspected the literal hostname string). All three gaps were confirmed by directly executing the exact existing function before writing a single line of the fix. A new `backend/utils/webhookUrlSafety.js` closes all three: full private/reserved IPv4 range coverage, correct bracket-unwrapped IPv6 private-range detection (including IPv4-mapped IPv6 in both its dotted and Node-canonicalized hex forms), and a real DNS lookup checking every address a hostname actually resolves to right now, failing closed on any DNS error.

**A second, distinct bug was found in the very fix meant to close the first one — and only real live-browser testing caught it.** Making the validator async (required for the DNS lookup) and wiring it unchanged into `body('url').custom(isSafeWebhookUrl)` silently broke ALL validation: express-validator's async `.custom()` determines pass/fail by whether the returned promise **rejects**, not by its resolved value — a validator resolving to `false` is treated as valid, identical to `true`. A direct `POST /api/webhooks` against the real running dev server confirmed `not-a-url`, `http://...`, and `https://127.0.0.1/x` were ALL returning `201 Created` immediately after the "fix" shipped. Caught by exactly the real-browser + real-server testing this session had access to for the first time; fixed with a small throwing adapter (`assertSafeWebhookUrl`) wired into the route, with a dedicated regression test spinning up the real express-validator chain shape against a real ephemeral HTTP server so this exact mistake can't silently reappear.

A new standalone `frontend/src/pages/Integrations.jsx` (`/integrations`, added to `App.jsx` ahead of Phase 30, same precedent as `Analytics.jsx`) has real cards for Webhooks (live endpoint count or an honest upgrade prompt) and API (links to Settings/API docs), exactly the 7 named "Coming Soon" integrations from the roadmap (never a fabricated 8th), and a full register/list/rotate/delete webhook management UI wired to the existing backend — one-time secret reveal matching `Settings.jsx`'s existing `KeyModal` pattern, non-danger/danger confirmations for rotate/delete, and tier gating driven by the real backend's `403 API_ACCESS_DENIED` response rather than a duplicated client-side tier-name list (deliberately avoiding a real, found-but-flagged-not-fixed inconsistency: `Settings.jsx`'s API Keys tab and `ApiDocs.jsx` both incorrectly gate on "Agency or Enterprise" when the real backend has allowed Professional tier all along). No enable/disable toggle exists, because the real backend has none distinct from delete — not fabricated for the UI's sake.

**Fully live-verified with real browser automation (Playwright + real Chrome) plus real Firebase-backed delivery testing — 78/78 checks, all passing:** 49/49 full-browser UI checks (3 real accounts logging in through the actual `/login` form — Professional/starter/a second independent Professional — covering direct navigation+refresh, non-interactive Coming Soon cards, the full register→reveal→copy→rotate→delete lifecycle surviving hard refreshes, the exact `127.0.0.2` SSRF regression rejected end-to-end through the live UI, a real webhook event triggered by a genuine Approve & Finalize click, cross-account isolation in both directions, an unauthenticated redirect, and a 390×844 mobile pass); 24+5=29 direct delivery-mechanics checks (a real `webhookEndpoints` doc seeded via the trusted Admin SDK pointed at a script-controlled local receiver, then real `report.finalized` events triggered via the actual unmodified approve API) capturing and verifying the real delivered payload/headers/signature, proving a rotated secret's new signature validates while the old one genuinely no longer does, and confirming the exact retry/backoff schedule (3 attempts on eventual success, 4 attempts stopping at `maxAttempts` on permanent failure, timed to the real `[0,5000,30000,120000]`ms policy). A real, pre-existing, sitewide UX defect was found and flagged (not fixed, out of this phase's file list): the cookie-consent banner has no backdrop and its DOM footprint blocks real clicks on content underneath it, including — on a narrow mobile viewport — the login page's own submit button.

**Not covered live:** a genuine external DNS-rebinding probe (e.g. a real `nip.io`-style URL) against the live, unmodified registration API was not exercised end-to-end — the fix's DNS logic is proven correct via 18 dependency-injected unit tests instead, judged sufficient given the exact logic path is already covered and this avoids an automated script making outbound requests to a third-party domain.

### 2026-08-18 (session 20) — Phase 15 implementation, superseded above as the "Current Active Phase" now that Phase 16 has landed; kept below for its own historical detail

**Phase 15 (General Analytics Page) was implemented 2026-08-18 (session 20), per explicit client instruction ("Start Phase 15 only").** A new `/analytics` page and a new shared `backend/services/analyticsService.js` (pure, unit-tested aggregation functions + a thin Firestore-fetching orchestrator, `getAnalytics`) back it via a new `GET /api/analytics` (`backend/routes/analytics.js`, mounted under both `/api` and `/api/v1`). Scope is entirely server-side, reusing Phase 14's role model directly (no new open question needed): an enterprise-tier account with `canViewAllProfiles` (Owner/Admin/Manager) gets organization-wide analytics aggregated across the whole team roster plus a per-member breakdown; every other account/role — restricted team members, and the large majority of accounts (every solo starter/professional/agency account, which can never have a team at all) — gets personal-scope analytics limited to their own uid. The `tier === 'enterprise'` check is a free, I/O-free short-circuit so the extra roster read never runs for the common case, a deliberate performance decision.

Every spec'd metric is delivered from real data: Reports Generated, Photos Analyzed, Average Report Completion Time (createdAt→reviewedAt for finalized reports, matching `reports.js`'s own `isReviewed()` legacy-status folding, honestly "not enough data" rather than a fake 0 when nothing qualifies), Reports Per User, Reports By Type, Reports By Status, Monthly Usage (a fixed rolling 12-month view, deliberately independent of the page's own date-range filter), Reports/Photos Over Time (gap-free, timezone-aware daily/weekly/monthly buckets auto-scaled to the selected range's width), and Team Usage/team-comparison (reusing the org-wide time series + the Reports-Per-User breakdown, not a redundant extra dataset). Date-range filtering supports 7/30/90/365-day presets, All Time, and a custom start/end picker; timezone accuracy comes from the frontend sending real local-midnight-to-local-midnight UTC instants plus a `tzOffset` minutes value, with the backend shifting timestamps by that offset before reading calendar boundaries — no IANA-timezone-library dependency needed.

**Deliberately did NOT refactor `crmService.js`'s already-shipped `buildDashboardAnalytics` or the Phase-4 `dashboard-summary` endpoint to share code with the new service** — both are already live-QA'd and tested, and `crmService.js`'s turnaround-hours definition subtly excludes legacy `completed` status while the new one (matching `isReviewed()`) includes it; forcing a merge would have silently changed already-shipped production behavior for accounts with legacy data. The new module only mirrors the same pattern (pure aggregator + thin Firestore wrapper), documented as a deliberate choice so a genuine future consolidation stays a straightforward refactor.

**A real bug caught by this phase's own new unit test before it ever reached live testing:** the timezone-shift helper's sign convention was initially inverted, which would have bucketed every report into the wrong local calendar day for any non-UTC caller — a dedicated test asserting the correct bucket at a deliberately-chosen UTC-day-boundary-crossing timestamp caught it immediately; fixed before any live verification ran.

A new standalone `frontend/src/pages/Analytics.jsx` (`/analytics`, added to `App.jsx` ahead of Phase 30, same precedent as `Templates.jsx`/`TeamMemberProfile.jsx`) renders range-filter chips + custom date pickers, 3 stat cards, 2 breakdown bar-charts, a 12-month usage chart, 2 scrollable time-series charts, and a conditional Team Comparison section — all in the same plain-Tailwind-bars style `CRM.jsx`'s existing dashboard already uses, so no new chart-library dependency was added. A new "Analytics" sidebar nav entry was added to both `Dashboard.jsx` and `EnterpriseDashboard.jsx`, available to every tier (this is the general page, not enterprise-only).

**Fully live-verified via the real running API (real Firestore project) — no browser automation tool was available this session (same documented gap as Phases 7/13/14):** a throwaway Node script seeded 7 real throwaway accounts (an organization with a real Admin/Adjuster/Viewer member, a fully separate second organization, a solo professional account, and a zero-report starter account) and 9 real report documents with hand-chosen dates/statuses/types, driving **55/55 checks, all passing**, against the live HTTP API under both `/api` and `/api/v1`. Every headline number matched hand-calculated expectations across 30d/90d/all-time/custom ranges (including exact legacy-status folding and date-boundary isolation); organization scope correctly activated only for the privileged Owner role with an accurate zero-filled per-member breakdown; the restricted Adjuster (a member of the same organization) and the solo/empty accounts all correctly took the personal-scope path; **cross-organization isolation was proven in both directions** (neither organization's data ever leaked into the other's totals); unauthenticated/garbage-token requests got clean 401s; and a live suspension of the Adjuster team member proved the exact same Phase 14 defense-in-depth mechanics (fresh-token 403 `TEAM_ACCESS_SUSPENDED`, stale-token 401 `TOKEN_REVOKED`) apply correctly to this brand-new route. All 7 accounts/9 reports/3 membership docs were deleted afterward, re-verified with a zero-residue query pass; `git status` shows only the expected new/modified files.

**Not covered live:** as with every prior phase in this environment, `Analytics.jsx`'s actual rendering, chart horizontal-scroll behavior, and mobile responsiveness were verified by code review + a successful production build only, since no browser automation tool was available — every underlying number and permission boundary the page displays was independently verified live and directly at the HTTP level, as detailed above.

### 2026-08-18 (session 19) — Phase 14 implementation, superseded above as the "Current Active Phase" now that Phase 15 has landed; kept below for its own historical detail

**Phase 14 (Team Roles Expansion & Member Profiles) was implemented 2026-08-18 (session 19), per explicit client instruction ("Start Phase 14 only").** Before any code was written, a least-privilege 7-role (+ legacy `editor`) permissions matrix was proposed and explicitly approved by the client, along with 4 specific decisions (legacy-editor handling, full suspension safeguards, keeping the team list inside `EnterpriseDashboard` while adding only a standalone member-profile route, and an explicit Owner/Admin/Manager allow-list for organization-template management) and a set of anti-lockout/anti-escalation rules (no self-role-change/self-suspend/self-remove; only Owner assigns Admin; Admin can't touch Owner/other Admins; Manager can only manage Adjuster/Inspector/Reviewer/Viewer/legacy-Editor; ownership transfer explicitly out of scope). See `PHASES.md` Phase 14's own section for the full matrix table and every implementation detail.

A new shared module, `backend/utils/orgRoles.js`, became the single source of truth for the capability matrix and the assignment/management hierarchy, consumed by `teams.js` (rewritten), `reports.js` (new `requireTeamCapability` gates on 8 routes), and `templateService.js` (whose Phase-13 `canManageOrganizationTemplates` moved from "anything that isn't a viewer" to an explicit Owner/Admin/Manager allow-list). **Three real, pre-existing bugs were found and fixed while making the roles real:** (1) `GET /teams/members` and every mutation endpoint previously matched only the literal account owner's own uid — no role, including the old 4-role model's "Admin," could ever actually manage the team before this phase, despite the UI implying otherwise. (2) A role change via `PUT /members/:id/role` only ever updated the `enterpriseTeams` row, never the target member's own `users/{uid}` doc — meaning a role change had never actually taken effect for that member's live permission checks. (3) `DELETE /members/:id` (remove) only deleted the team-roster row and left the removed member's own account permanently retaining enterprise tier forever; it now reverts `teamOwnerId`/`teamRole`/`teamPermissions`/`tier` on their own account.

Suspension ships with all four requested safeguards, independently verified live: an immediate status flip (member stays visible to admins, never hidden), a `tokenVersion` bump (kills outstanding custom JWTs), a mirrored `teamMembershipStatus` field checked on every authenticated request at zero extra Firestore-read cost (the actual "live per-request check"), and a real `getAuth().revokeRefreshTokens()` call. A new `TeamMemberProfile.jsx` page (`/team/members/:memberId`, `me` supported for self-lookup) shows real reports-generated/photos-analyzed/recent-activity stats sourced from existing `reports`/`auditLogs` data, with admin actions gated per-target by the same server-computed hierarchy the roster UI consumes (never re-implemented client-side).

**A real, flagged architectural limitation was surfaced by this exact combination, not hidden:** since reports have always been strictly `userId`-scoped (no shared team report pool exists — that's Phase 19 territory) and Inspector's approved capability set has `canApprove: false`, a report an Inspector generates can never actually be approved by anyone at all today — not by the Inspector (blocked by role) and not by anyone else (they don't own it). This is an honest, direct consequence of the approved matrix layered onto the existing single-owner report model, left unfixed as explicitly out of this phase's scope (Phase 19's eventual cross-member review-request workflow is the intended real fix).

**Fully live-verified via the real running API (real Firestore project) — no browser automation tool was available this session (same documented gap as Phases 7 and 13):** two throwaway Node scripts drove 10 real throwaway accounts (an organization with a real invited Admin/2 Managers/Adjuster/Inspector/Reviewer/Viewer/2nd-Admin, a manufactured pre-Phase-14-shaped legacy Editor, a separate second organization, and one unrelated outsider) through **87/87 checks, all passing**, across two passes split by a self-inflicted local rate-limiter window (this session's environment didn't permit a dev-server restart to clear it, unlike prior phases — the run was simply split and re-run after waiting the limiter's window out twice, a testing-infrastructure fact, not a product bug). Every role's capability flags matched the matrix exactly; the full anti-escalation/anti-lockout suite passed; a live test proved a never-reissued JWT loses its permissions the instant a role changes, with no re-login involved; suspension was proven immediate through three independent mechanisms (stale-tokenVersion rejection, a fresh-tokenVersion-but-still-suspended rejection proving the live status check specifically, and Firebase's own `tokensValidAfterTime` genuinely advancing); removal was confirmed to genuinely revert the account to solo starter-tier rather than leaving it broken; cross-organization isolation returned clean 404s/empty rosters throughout; report-action gating was confirmed for Inspector/Reviewer/Adjuster through a real generate→approve→export lifecycle; the legacy Editor role worked end-to-end including the self-healing suspend path for a record that predates this phase's own new fields; and real audit-log entries backed every team-management action and a member's own profile stats. All 10 accounts, their reports, one template, and one exported Storage object were fully deleted afterward, confirmed via a repeat cleanup pass returning 0 records and a clean `git status`.

**Not covered live:** as with Phases 7 and 13, `EnterpriseDashboard.jsx`'s role-aware Team tab and the new `TeamMemberProfile.jsx` page's actual rendering/loading/empty/error states/mobile responsiveness were verified by code review + a successful production build only, since no browser automation tool was available — every API call and permission boundary those pages exercise was independently verified live and directly at the HTTP level. True concurrent-request races were not tested. Ownership transfer was, per explicit client instruction, neither implemented nor simulated.

### 2026-08-18 (session 18) — Phase 13 implementation, superseded above as the "Current Active Phase" now that Phase 14 has landed; kept below for its own historical detail

**Phase 13 (Real Template Builder) was implemented 2026-08-18 (session 18), per explicit client instruction ("Start Phase 13 only").** A new, real structural template system was built alongside (not replacing) the pre-existing shallow `reportTemplates` collection/`/reports/templates` endpoints: a new `templates` Firestore collection (`backend/services/templateService.js`) covering name/description, wizard field defaults, a template-chosen `requiredFields` subset, Phase-9-shaped custom `sections`, a Phase-11-shaped `photoLayout` export-defaults set, and `branding` (companyName/footerText/a Storage-backed logo) — with `personal`/`organization`/`flacron` scopes. This codebase has no separate "organizations" collection, so "organization" reuses the existing enterprise-team model from `teams.js` (`organizationId = user.teamOwnerId || user.uid`, role from the existing `teamRole` field) — a solo account's organization is just itself, so org templates degrade gracefully until that account has teammates. Real Create/Edit/Duplicate/Archive/Restore/Delete all landed (`backend/routes/templates.js`, mounted `/api/templates`), a real `/templates` list page + `/templates/new`/`/templates/:id/edit` builder (`Templates.jsx`/`TemplateBuilder.jsx`, standalone routes ahead of Phase 30, same precedent as `ReportPreviewPage.jsx`), and 6 seeded Flacron example templates (Residential Property, Water Loss, Roof, Wind/Hail, Fire, Commercial Property) each with a real cautious-language custom section. The wizard (`Dashboard.jsx`) gained a "Start From a Report Template" picker (`TemplatePickerModal.jsx`) that applies a template's fields + a new `templateId`, which `POST /generate` resolves server-side, enforces the template's required fields against (`400 TEMPLATE_REQUIRED_FIELD_MISSING`), and persists onto the new report (`templateId`/`templateGuidance`/`templateSections`/`exportDefaults`/`templateBranding`). A template's custom sections are **deterministically appended** to the generated content after generation (`appendTemplateSections`, new in `richContent.js`) — the same "don't trust the LLM alone" pattern Phase 8 established for photo observations — and re-applied correctly on retry/regenerate.

**Two real bugs found and fixed during live testing, both scoped inside this phase's own new branding-fallback work:** (1) a template's `logoObjectPath`/`logoUrl` could not be set through the general template-edit endpoint by design (only the dedicated `/logo` upload route can), closing an IDOR risk before it ever shipped — a client could otherwise have pointed a template's "logo" at an arbitrary Storage path it doesn't own (e.g. another user's private photo) and had it silently downloaded into every export from that template. (2) The export route's new `templateBranding` fallback for `companyName`/`reportFooter` resolved correctly into the export options object but never actually rendered, because the PDF/DOCX/HTML generators only ever *display* custom branding text when `hideFlacronBranding` is true (normally an enterprise white-label-only switch) — fixed with a narrowly-scoped additional fallback that only engages when there's no white-label config AND no personal `userData.company` to defer to instead, verified live across all three export formats.

**A pre-existing, unrelated gap found and flagged (not fixed, out of scope):** a *non-enterprise* user's own Settings → Profile "Company Name" field has the exact same silent-inert-ness this phase's bug (2) describes — it's stored and used for PDF `Author` metadata but never shown in visible export text unless the account is also enterprise white-label. This predates Phase 13 (the `hideFlacronBranding` gate already existed) and was deliberately left untouched to avoid scope creep into an already-shipped, unrelated feature.

**Fully live-verified via the real running API (real Firestore project, real Anthropic API) — no browser automation tool was available this session (neither `chromium-cli` nor a Playwright install existed, and installing one unprompted was judged disproportionate):** two throwaway Node scripts, run from OUTSIDE the backend directory specifically to avoid triggering `nodemon`'s file-watcher restart mid-background-job (one earlier in-`backend/` scratch file was found to do exactly that, killing an in-flight report-generation pipeline — a real, reproduced instance of Phase 7's own documented "no persistent job queue" tradeoff, not a Phase 13 defect), drove 5 real throwaway accounts (an org owner/editor/viewer sharing one organization, plus 2 fully unrelated outsiders) through 49 checks, all passing: Flacron seeding (all 6 named templates, visible to everyone); personal-template owner-only visibility; organization-template same-org visibility + outsider exclusion; role enforcement (viewer → 403 edit, editor → 200, outsider → 403/404); Flacron immutability (edit/delete both 403 for everyone); Duplicate always produces an owned personal copy; Archive/Restore; required-field validation (missing → 400 with the exact field name, present → 201 with `exportDefaults`/`templateBranding` correctly reflecting the template); a real end-to-end AI generation confirming the template's custom section appended verbatim; logo upload/removal + cross-user upload rejection; and the branding-fallback fix confirmed actually visible in real exported HTML and DOCX (`word/document.xml` inspected via `pizzip`). All test data (accounts, templates, reports, Storage objects) was deleted afterward; `git status` confirms zero repo residue.

**Not covered live:** the wizard's "Start From a Report Template" UI, the `/templates`/builder pages' actual rendering, loading/empty/error states, and mobile responsiveness were verified by code review + a successful production build only — not by looking at a rendered page or a browser console, since no browser automation was available. This is a real gap relative to Phases 8-12's Playwright-based sessions; a follow-up session with browser automation (or the client's own manual click-through) is recommended before treating the frontend UI layer as fully QA'd, even though the API/data layer it calls is now thoroughly verified. Also not covered: a member leaving/being removed from a team whose organization template they created (no such flow exists yet, out of scope); templates-list pagination (no account has enough templates to paginate).

### 2026-08-18 (session 17) — Phase 12 implementation, superseded above as the "Current Active Phase" now that Phase 13 has landed; kept below for its own historical detail

**Phase 12 (My Reports & Claims Management Completion) was implemented 2026-08-18 (session 17), per explicit client instruction ("Start Phase 12 only").** My Reports (`Dashboard.jsx`) gained a collapsible Filters panel (Claim #, Loss Type, Report Type, Creator, Created-date range, Organization — all real server-side params on `GET /reports`, most newly added this session) and a "More actions" row menu (`RowActionsMenu`) adding Duplicate, Download PDF, Share, and Archive-or-Restore, with permanent Delete moved into the same menu and Archive given its own non-danger confirm dialog clearly distinct from Delete's danger one. A new `POST /:id/duplicate` copies the full Phase 5 claim/inspection field set into a clean draft (no photos/documents/content, per the phase's own risk-note recommendation), and a new `POST /:id/restore` reverses Archive back to a report's exact pre-archive status (draft or finalized) via a new `preArchiveStatus` field. The Claims list (`CRM.jsx`) gained real search/date filters and Edit/Archive/Restore/Delete row actions, all wired onto CRM's pre-existing `crmService`/`crm.js` endpoints (`PUT`/`DELETE /claims/:id`) that had simply never been called from any UI — Archive/Restore reuses the same update endpoint with a new `archived` boolean field, no new route needed. `CRMClaimProfile.jsx` became a real tabbed workspace (Overview/Reports/Photos), with a new Photos tab that aggregates real photos across every report linked to the claim (via the existing per-report photos endpoint), filterable by Report/Category/Review-status.

**The Claims tier-gating open question was deliberately left unresolved, not guessed at.** Phase 12's own 2026-08-16 amendment claimed this question was "added as an Open Question below," but it was never actually enumerated in that list — a documentation gap. Per the client's explicit instruction this session to stop and ask before implementing that specific behavior if still unresolved, this phase left Claims exactly as tier-gated as it already was (Agency/Enterprise only) — none of this phase's actual tasks (completing the existing Agency+ Claims feature's filters/actions) required deciding whether to expose Claims more broadly. The question is now properly logged as Open Question #15 in `PHASES.md` for a future decision.

**A real bug found and fixed during live testing:** duplicating a report set the editor's content state to the literal JavaScript `null` (a fresh duplicate's `content` field, since no AI generation has run for it), which the rich-text editor's markdown parser (`String(content)`) rendered as the literal text **"null"** inside the editor — a real, visible bug (not a crash) that looked like corrupted/fabricated data. Fixed by falling back to `''` at the one call site that didn't already follow the rest of the codebase's established null-guard convention.

**Fully live-verified this session** (2 throwaway Agency-tier QA accounts + one seeded CRM client/claim, created via the Firebase Admin SDK and fully deleted afterward along with a real end-to-end generated report and its Storage objects, confirmed zero repo residue via `git status`): My Reports filters (13/13 — every filter correctly isolated its expected report(s), Clear Filters restored the full list); My Reports actions (15/15 in a final clean run — Duplicate/Archive/Restore/permanent-Delete/Download/Share all confirmed, including Share correctly enabled-with-a-real-copied-link for a finalized report and disabled-with-a-tooltip for a draft); Claims tab (10/10 — search, empty state, date range, Edit persistence, Archive/Restore/Show-Archived); claim workspace (7/7 — direct URL load, all three tabs present, Reports tab shows the linked report(s), Photos tab correctly aggregated 2 real AI-analyzed photos from a genuine end-to-end wizard-generated report linked to the seeded claim, survives a refresh); cross-user authorization (5/5 — a second account gets a clean "Claim unavailable" with zero data leaked, and never sees the first account's reports); mobile 390×844 (5/5 — zero horizontal overflow, zero console/network errors). Several self-inflicted 429s from this session's own extensive rapid test iteration were each resolved via a backend restart and confirmed not regressions by clean re-runs, consistent with the same documented pattern from Phases 9-11.

**Not covered live:** a genuinely pre-Phase-6 legacy-shaped report (no `photos[]` array) was not specifically re-tested against the new filters/actions — these operate on fields that exist identically on legacy reports, and Duplicate never touches photo fields either way, so no legacy-specific behavior was introduced, but this wasn't independently re-confirmed with a real synthesized legacy record this session. Pagination beyond a single page wasn't exercised (the seeded test data fit on one page).

### 2026-08-18 (session 16) — Phase 11 implementation, superseded above as the "Current Active Phase" now that Phase 12 has landed; kept below for its own historical detail

**Phase 11 (Export Options Modal & PDF Layout Completion, expanded scope: Report Preview, Export Options & Document Layout Completion) was implemented 2026-08-18 (session 16), per explicit client instruction ("Start Phase 11 only").** A real, standalone `/reports/:id/preview` route (`ReportPreviewPage.jsx`) was added ahead of the full Phase 30 routing migration — same precedent as Phases 9 and 10, since this one page didn't need the whole `activeView` architecture migrated first. It fetches its report independently by ID, so a direct URL visit or a page refresh both work with no dependency on `Dashboard.jsx`'s state, and offers a Desktop/PDF toggle plus Edit/Approve/Export controls gated on report status. A new shared `ExportOptionsModal.jsx` (format picker + 5 checkboxes — cover page/photo captions/page numbers/photo appendix/company branding, all default ON for backward compatibility — plus a 1/2/4-per-page photo layout picker) is used by both the preview page and `Dashboard.jsx` (which also kept its old one-click quick-download buttons). `POST /:id/export` now validates and authorizes every option server-side, and the Photo Documentation appendix in all 3 export generators is now a real configurable layout showing each photo's number, caption, area/location, and reviewer-approved observation — built from Phase 8's real per-photo review state, never fabricated. See `PHASES.md` Phase 11's "Implementation summary" for full per-task detail.

**The most significant thing found this session: a real Golden Rule #4 (server-side entitlement enforcement) gap, pre-existing and unrelated to this phase's own task list.** `backend/config/tiers.js` has always defined `tier.exportFormats` (e.g. `['pdf']` for starter), but `POST /:id/export` never actually checked it — a starter-tier account could get a clean DOCX/HTML export by calling the API directly, bypassing the UI's own format-gating entirely. Fixed as part of this phase's own export-route work: the route now returns `403 EXPORT_FORMAT_NOT_ALLOWED` for a disallowed format. Verified live with a real captured bearer token (see Testing below).

**A second real inconsistency found during visual verification (a UX/consistency issue, not a security one):** unchecking "Include company branding" hid the small footer brand-attribution line in the DOCX and HTML generators but not in the PDF generator (whose footer text was never gated by that option to begin with). Standardized all three generators on the PDF's original behavior: this option only toggles the cover mark and header-bar logo/text; the footer's brand attribution (paired with the always-on confidentiality statement) is never hidden by it, in any format.

**Fully live-verified this session** (2 throwaway QA accounts — professional and starter tier — created via the Firebase Admin SDK and fully deleted afterward along with their reports/Storage objects, confirmed zero repo residue via `git status`): generated a real report, approved its photos for real reviewer-approved appendix data, opened it via "Open Full Preview" and confirmed the real `/reports/:id/preview` URL; Desktop/PDF toggle both ways; real PDF/DOCX/HTML downloads (35.8KB/12.1KB/41.6KB) through the actual export modal with non-default option combinations; real text extracted from the downloaded PDFs (via `pdfjs-dist`, rendered locally, since Playwright's bundled headless Chromium cannot render PDFs inline) confirmed every checkbox's effect is real and correct, the confidentiality statement + claim number appear in the footer of every export regardless of options, and report language stayed cautious throughout; real DOCX XML inspected directly (via `pizzip`) confirmed the same, and is what surfaced the branding-consistency bug above; the Approve modal on the preview page finalized the report with refresh-persistence confirmed; the Edit button correctly deep-links back into `Dashboard.jsx`'s existing review view via a new, narrowly-scoped `?openReport=<id>` handler; a cross-user authorization check confirmed a different account gets a clean "Report not found" with zero data leaked; the entitlement fix above was confirmed live via a captured real bearer token (403 for DOCX/HTML, 200 for PDF, as starter tier); mobile (390×844) confirmed zero horizontal overflow. Zero console errors and zero unexpected failed/5xx requests across every pass, aside from several self-inflicted local dev rate-limiter trips from rapid repeated test runs, each resolved via a backend restart (same documented pattern as Phases 9/10) — one pre-existing, unrelated stuck `processing` report from 2026-07-29 remains untouched, as before.

**Not covered live:** DOCX/HTML files were inspected via their real XML/text content, not opened in Microsoft Word or a browser — no rendering-engine-specific bugs can be ruled out beyond what that structural inspection shows. `EnterpriseDashboard.jsx` was not given its own export-modal/preview entry point (out of this phase's stated file list) but its reports remain fully reachable via a direct preview URL.

### 2026-08-18 (session 15) — Phase 10 implementation, superseded above as the "Current Active Phase" now that Phase 11 has landed; kept below for its own historical detail

**Phase 10 (Report Review Checklist & Approval Modal) was implemented 2026-08-18 (session 15), per explicit client instruction ("Start Phase 10 only").** A new, purely read-only `frontend/src/components/ReportReviewChecklist.jsx` computes 5 checklist rows (Claim Data, Inspection Data, Photos analyzed/reviewed, Documentation, Draft Sections) entirely from data the app already persists — no new backend endpoint was needed. Three warning banners (missing claim number, excluded/unreviewed photos, missing inspection date) render only when genuinely true. A confirmation modal (the existing shared `ConfirmDialog`, not a new component) now sits in front of the existing, unmodified `POST /:id/approve` call in both `Dashboard.jsx` and `EnterpriseDashboard.jsx`. See `PHASES.md` Phase 10's "Implementation summary" for full per-task detail, and this file's session-15 changelog entry above for the complete testing list.

**The one real engineering problem this phase had to solve:** Phase 8's interactive Photo Review gallery keeps its own live per-photo review state that the report doc's own `photos` snapshot goes stale against the moment a reviewer acts on a photo. `ReportPhotoGallery` gained an optional `onPhotosChange` callback threaded into a new `Dashboard.jsx` state so the checklist updates live with zero extra network calls; `EnterpriseDashboard.jsx` (no interactive gallery of its own) instead lets the checklist self-fetch `GET /:id/photos`.

**A real bug found and fixed during live testing:** wiring `onPhotosChange` initially called it from inside `setPhotos`'s updater-function form — this fires during React's render-phase state resolution, and calling a *different* component's setter from there triggered a genuine "Cannot update a component while rendering a different component" React warning on every photo review action. Fixed by computing the next photos array from the `photos` closure directly and calling both setters as independent top-level calls.

**A pre-existing, unrelated gap found and flagged (not fixed, out of scope):** `EnterpriseDashboard.jsx`'s report generation still assumes Phase 7's pre-pipeline synchronous model — a gap from Phase 7 never being retrofitted into that dashboard. The new checklist handles it gracefully (an honest empty/zero state, no crash) but the dashboard itself would benefit from the same async-aware polling `Dashboard.jsx` already has.

**Fully live-verified this session** (3 throwaway QA accounts — 2 agency-tier with different data-completeness profiles, 1 enterprise-tier — all created via the Firebase Admin SDK and fully deleted afterward along with their reports and Storage objects, confirmed zero repo residue via `git status`): a fully-complete report's checklist went from "0 of 2 reviewed" + a warning to "2 of 2 reviewed" + zero warnings live, with no reload, after approving both photos in the gallery; a deliberately incomplete report simultaneously showed all three warning types (missing inspection date, excluded photo, unreviewed photo) correctly at once; the confirmation modal's Cancel (stays draft) and Approve (finalizes exactly as before, signature persists, survives refresh) paths were both exercised on desktop; the modal was also exercised on a real 390×844 mobile viewport with zero horizontal overflow; an EnterpriseDashboard spot check confirmed the checklist self-fetches and doesn't crash on a still-processing/null-content report. Zero console errors and zero unexpected failed/5xx HTTP requests across every pass, aside from several self-inflicted local dev rate-limiter trips from this session's own rapid repeated test runs (each confirmed as a testing artifact via a backend restart + clean re-run, not a regression).

**Not covered live (flagged, not glossed over):** a full approve-and-finalize cycle completed specifically through `EnterpriseDashboard.jsx` (the spot check confirmed the checklist renders/self-fetches there; the approve call itself is the identical, already-live-tested `Dashboard.jsx` code path). True concurrent-editor races were not tested (same reasoning as Phases 7-9).

### 2026-08-16 (session 14) — Phase 9 implementation, superseded above as the "Current Active Phase" now that Phase 10 has landed; kept below for its own historical detail

**Phase 9 (Report Editor Rich-Text & AI Panel Upgrade) was implemented 2026-08-16 (session 14), per explicit client instruction ("Start Phase 9 only"), implemented ahead of Phase 30 (routing migration) — safe to do because Phase 9 upgrades the existing editor component in place rather than adding a new page/route.** `SectionedReportEditor.jsx` was rewritten from plain textareas to a full TipTap-based rich-text editor: bold/italic/underline, sub-headings, bullet/numbered lists, tables, single-photo and photo-grid insert (resolved only against the report's own owned photos, never a client URL), page breaks, undo/redo, section add/delete/rename/reorder (Move Up/Down), and 8 total AI entry points (7 new FLACRON ENGINE writing-assistance functions via a new `POST /:id/sections/assist`, plus the existing "Suggest" extended into the spec's distinct "Regenerate Section" workflow — instructions textarea → generated comparison → explicit Approve/Cancel). **`report.content` deliberately stays a single Markdown string** (an extended dialect documented in the new `backend/utils/richContent.js`, mirrored in a frontend copy) — no Firestore schema change, so version history, `PUT`/`approve`/`export`, sharing, and `Dashboard.jsx`/`EnterpriseDashboard.jsx`'s integration all needed zero changes; the editor's external `value`/`onChange` contract is byte-for-byte the same as before. `aiService.js`'s report-generation prompt's 9 section titles were reconciled with the spec's required list (Report Info, Claim Info & Insured Info, Property Info, Inspection Details & Overview, Area Observations, Photo Documentation, Additional Notes) — titles renamed, numbering/content preserved, so Phase 1/8's `ensureLossSummary`/`ensureConclusion`/`insertPhotoObservations` safety nets (which anchor on `SECTION\s*N`, not title text) needed zero changes.

**A real bug found and fixed during live testing:** `documentGenerator.js`'s own local section-splitter (distinct from the frontend's) silently dropped any content before the first `## ` heading — a low-stakes pre-Phase-9 gap (typically just the report's H1 title line) that became a real data-loss bug once Regenerate Section could legitimately place real content (a live-tested example: a new narrative, a single photo, and a photo grid) into exactly that position. Confirmed via a real DOCX export showing 0 embedded images/no page break despite correct `photoMap` input, while PDF/HTML (which never pre-split into sections) rendered correctly. Fixed by carrying that content through as a `{title: null}` pseudo-section rendered with no synthetic heading; 2 new regression tests added. See `PHASES.md` Phase 9's "Implementation summary" for full per-task detail, the sanitization approach, and the complete live-verification list.

**Fully live-verified this session** (a throwaway QA account + a second cross-user account, both created via the Firebase Admin SDK and fully deleted afterward along with their reports, Storage objects, and version subcollections — confirmed zero residue via `git status`): a real wizard-to-generate pass against the live Anthropic API; 9/9 formatting-control checks (bold/italic/underline/numbered-list/table/page-break/undo/redo); 5/5 section-management checks (add/rename/move/delete, including re-finding a renamed section after reorder); 4/4 photo-insert checks (single + grid, real uploaded-photo thumbnails); AI panel functions live-tested (Improve Writing, Expand, Review Photo Documentation) with Apply/Discard both exercised; 4/4 Regenerate Section checks (instructions modal, Cancel, live comparison, Approve & Replace) against the real API; a Save-Changes round-trip confirmed via direct Firestore read that the new photo/grid/bold tokens persisted correctly; a full Approve & Finalize pass (real signature fields, status → finalized, no watermark on the finalized export); PDF/DOCX/HTML export correctness verified via a direct authenticated API call (6-page PDF with the page break; DOCX with 2 real embedded JPEG media parts, 3 `<w:drawing>` elements, a real Word page break, real per-run bold — all XML confirmed well-formed; HTML with 3 base64-embedded photos); 7/7 authorization/sanitization checks (401 unauthenticated, 404 cross-user on both new AI endpoints, 400 invalid action, a 400,000-character content payload correctly capped at 300,000 before persisting); a genuinely synthesized legacy (pre-Phase-6/8/9) report opened with zero crashes; and a 390×844 mobile pass with zero horizontal overflow and zero console errors. Zero console errors and zero failed/5xx HTTP requests across every pass.

**Not covered live (flagged, not glossed over):** Check Consistency/Check Missing Information/Rewrite Professionally/Shorten were not individually driven through a live browser click (Improve Writing, Expand, and Review Photo Documentation were, covering both the plain-rewrite and context-aware code shapes) — correctness rests on the 8 `section-assist.test.js` prompt-construction tests plus all 7 functions sharing one already-live-tested execution path. True concurrent-editor races were not tested (same reasoning as Phase 8).
