### 2026-08-16 (session 13) — Phase 8 implementation, superseded above as the "Current Active Phase" now that Phase 9 has landed; kept below for its own historical detail

**Phase 8 (Per-Photo Analysis Review UI) was implemented 2026-08-16 (session 13), per explicit client instruction ("start phase 8 only").** The AI vision prompt (`backend/services/aiService.js`) now returns one structured classification per photo (`location`/`category`/`severity`/`observation`/`confidence`, guided by a new location/category taxonomy authored for this implementation — no external spec file was available in-repo to copy one from) instead of a single whole-batch summary. Each photo's result is persisted on its own per-photo record (`analysis`, alongside a new `review` state: `pending`/`approved`/`edited`/`excluded` + an optional note). A new interactive photo-review UI (`ReportPhotoGallery` in `Dashboard.jsx`, used both in the post-generation view and My Reports' detail modal) provides Edit/Approve/Exclude/Add Note/Include(restore) controls, clearly labeling AI output ("AI-Suggested Observation") versus a reviewer's own edit. A new pure function `buildEffectiveImageAnalysis()` builds `generateReport()`'s actual input from each photo's reviewed state (excluded photos dropped, edited text substituted) — used both for the initial automatic generation and a new `POST /:id/photos/regenerate` endpoint that rebuilds report content on demand after review changes.

**A deliberate, non-task-list addition made during live verification (not a bug, but a real gap in the phase's own acceptance criterion):** testing task 4's "confirm edits are reflected in the output" against the real Anthropic API showed the report-generation prompt's free-form prose may paraphrase or omit a single edited photo observation (Section 5's narrative is driven mainly by the adjuster's own `damagesObserved`/`lossDescription` text when provided, not the photo JSON) — inherent LLM behavior, not a defect, but it made the acceptance criterion merely probabilistic. Fixed with a new deterministic safety net, `insertPhotoObservations()` (mirrors the existing `ensureLossSummary`/`ensureConclusion`/`insertNoPhotoDisclaimer` pattern), which appends a verbatim, numbered list of every active photo's exact reviewed observation under Section 8 — guaranteeing an edit's exact text appears in the exported report and an excluded photo's text never does, independent of model behavior. See `PHASES.md` Phase 8's "Implementation summary" for full per-task detail.

**Fully live-verified this session** (throwaway QA account + a second cross-user test account, both created via the Firebase Admin SDK with `emailVerified: true` and fully deleted afterward, along with their reports/Storage objects/`analysisJobs` docs — confirmed zero residue via `git status`): a complete wizard-to-regenerate Playwright pass (15/15 checks) generated a real report against the live Anthropic API, edited one photo's observation, excluded another, and regenerated — direct Firestore inspection afterward confirmed `imageAnalysis.totalImagesAnalyzed` correctly dropped to 1, and the exported `content` literally contained the edited sentence verbatim (via the new appendix) while the excluded photo's text did not appear anywhere. 8 direct HTTP authorization/edge-case checks (unauthenticated, invalid action, unknown photo/report id, legacy synthetic id, a genuinely different second account denied 404 on the first account's report, approve-without-analysis rejected, note-without-analysis allowed) all passed. A retry-then-review-preservation check (photo forced into `needs_attention`, retried, re-analyzed fresh, and its prior edit+note confirmed intact afterward) and a legacy-shaped (pre-Phase-6) report compatibility check (synthesized `reviewable:false` entries, no crash) both passed via direct Firestore/API inspection. A separate persistence pass confirmed the reviewed state (summary count, edited text, "Edited" pill) survives navigating away to My Reports and back, and a true hard page refresh — 6/6 checks, zero console errors. A mobile-viewport (390×844) pass confirmed the gallery renders in the detail modal with zero horizontal overflow — 4/4 checks, zero console errors. This incidentally also gave Phase 7's frontend analysis-progress view its first-ever real-browser confirmation (no browser tool was available in session 12), closing out that phase's one remaining open item.

**Not covered live (flagged, not glossed over):** the `ANALYSIS_PENDING` guard branch specifically (rejecting review actions while a photo is still `queued`/`analyzing`, as distinct from the `needs_attention`/no-analysis case, which WAS tested) — timing a request into that narrow window against a fast real API call proved impractical; correct by code inspection only. True concurrent-editor races were not live-tested (an unlikely scenario for a single-adjuster workflow; the transactional write pattern makes it safe by construction regardless).

**Phases 10 and 11 have since landed, and Phase 12 has since landed on top of them (2026-08-18, session 17 — see "Current Active Phase" above). Awaiting explicit approval before starting Phase 13 or any other phase.** Client has also previously instructed: do not commit, push, or deploy anything yet — preserve all current local changes as-is (this instruction is treated as still standing; no commit/push was made this session).

## Known Issues / Blockers

- See `PHASES.md`'s "Open Questions Requiring Client/Team Decision" section — 9 open questions remain (brand palette resolved 2026-08-15), each scoped to the specific phase(s) it blocks (none block the entire roadmap). The newest (#15, logged 2026-08-18) is the Claims tier-gating question — should Claims/CRM stay Agency+-only or become available to all tiers — left deliberately unresolved by Phase 12 per explicit client instruction.
- **NEW (found 2026-08-18 during Phase 13, not fixed — pre-existing, out of Phase 13's scope):** a non-enterprise user's own Settings → Profile "Company Name" field is silently inert on exports — it's used for PDF `Author` metadata but never shown in the visible header/footer text of any export format, because that requires `hideFlacronBranding: true`, which only ever gets set for enterprise white-label accounts. The field is presented in the UI with no tier caveat, so any Starter/Professional/Agency user who fills it in today gets zero visible effect. Phase 13 found and fixed the identical bug for its own new template-branding feature (a template's companyName/footerText now correctly falls back into `hideFlacronBranding` when it's the only branding source present) but deliberately did not extend that same fix to the pre-existing `userData.company` field, to avoid scope creep into an unrelated, already-shipped feature. Worth a small follow-up task: either extend the same fallback to `userData.company`, or caveat the Settings UI copy to say it only affects white-label plans.
- **NEW (found 2026-08-18 during Phase 13, environment/testing-methodology note, not a product bug):** creating or editing any file inside `backend/` while a background report-generation pipeline is in flight (Phase 7's fire-and-forget in-process job model) causes `nodemon`'s file-watcher to restart the server, killing that in-flight job permanently (it's left stuck in `status: 'processing'` with no error, per Phase 7's own documented "no persistent job queue survives a restart" tradeoff). This is not new behavior, but was newly reproduced/confirmed this session by an unrelated scratch test script — future sessions doing live verification should run any test scripts from outside the `backend/` directory (or otherwise avoid touching watched files) while a generation is in flight.
- **RESOLVED 2026-08-15:** Brand palette authority — `#FD4403`/`#002A64` confirmed by the client as the current, authoritative palette (not the external spec's `#FF5A1F`/`#171C2B`). See Phase 2 in `PHASES.md` for full evidence.
- **NEW (found 2026-08-15, minor, not fixed — deliberately deferred, out of Phase 2's scope):** `frontend/src/pages/Pricing.jsx`'s `COLOR_MAP` object has stale/misleading key names — keys literally named `blue` and `purple` actually map to `brand-*` and `amber-*` classes respectively (a leftover from an earlier template where those keys presumably matched their literal color). Visually harmless (output is consistent), but confusing for future maintenance. Not fixed since it's a code-cleanliness item unrelated to the actual brand-color-correctness bug Phase 2 targeted.
- **RESOLVED 2026-08-15 (Phase 3, client-requested directly):** the recurring "Account data unavailable" bug (previously "fixed" 2026-08-02 and 2026-08-12, but kept recurring). Root cause found: `middleware/auth.js`'s `authenticateToken` only ever classified a *downstream Firestore-lookup* failure (after a successful token verification) as retryable (503) — it never handled `verifyIdToken()` itself failing transiently (e.g. a network hiccup fetching Google's signing certs on a long-running process), which fell straight through to a dead-end `401 INVALID_TOKEN` that the frontend correctly does NOT auto-retry (401 means "log in again"). Fixed: one immediate retry on a transient-looking `verifyIdToken` failure, and if still failing, a `503 AUTH_VERIFY_UNAVAILABLE` instead of 401 — letting the already-built 503/network-error retry + background auto-recovery actually engage for this failure mode. 9 new regression tests added (`backend/test/auth-transient-error.test.js`). **A live, real instance of a related-but-distinct process-management issue was encountered and resolved during this same session's verification** — an orphaned backend process from earlier kept re-claiming port 3000, silently running stale code — see Phase 3's Session/Change Log entry for full detail; this was an environment-hygiene issue in this session, not a product code bug, but is a vivid real-world illustration of exactly the failure class this fix targets.
- **RESOLVED 2026-08-15 (Phase 3, found during live verification):** a genuine pre-existing race condition in `backend/routes/users.js`'s `GET /profile` auto-create logic (plain non-merge `.set()`, vulnerable to being clobbered by a concurrent create/read on the same brand-new user's doc). Fixed with `db.runTransaction()`. This was surfaced by Phase 3's new signup flow (which fires several profile-touching calls right after registration) but is a fix to pre-existing, general-purpose code, not something Phase 3 introduced.
- SSO and White-Label features were **not deep-audited** in the original spreadsheet audit (out of scope for the six research passes) — their spreadsheet status (`NOT IMPLEMENTED` and `PARTIALLY COMPLETE` respectively) should be treated as provisional until a dedicated verification pass is done.
- Local `.env` missing `FIREBASE_API_KEY` (see Constraints above) — verify before testing auth-dependent phases locally.
- ~~**NEW (found 2026-08-14 during Phase 1 live verification, pre-existing, unrelated to Phase 1):** `generateReport()` consistently omits **Section 9 (Conclusion / Items for Adjuster Review)** from generated reports~~ — **RESOLVED 2026-08-16 (Phase 1 re-audit):** raised `generateReport()`'s `maxTokens` from 4096→8192 and added a new `ensureConclusion()` safety net in `aiService.js` (mirrors the existing `ensureLossSummary()` pattern for Section 7) that generates just Section 9 as a focused follow-up call if it's still missing/stub-short. Live-verified against the real Anthropic API with a real text-only report-generation call: all 9 sections present, Section 9 substantive (237 words), correct cautious-language framing, no fabricated certification.
- **NEW (found 2026-08-16 during Phase 1 re-audit, fixed same session):** the frontend's global axios client (`frontend/src/services/api.js`) applied a blanket 120s timeout to every API call, including `reportsAPI.generate` — with up to 10 vision batches at concurrency 3 (4 waves) plus the report-generation call on top, a 100-photo request could realistically approach/exceed 120s and abort client-side, which would have made Phase 1's own "no timeout on a 100-photo report" acceptance criterion false. Fixed by giving `reportsAPI.generate` its own 300000ms (5 min) override. The actual end-to-end timing at 50-100 photos was still not live-tested (client explicitly declined the paid-API-cost test) — this remains the one open residual risk for Phase 1, not a known failure.
- **NEW (found 2026-08-14, minor):** `POST /api/reports/analyze-images` has its own separate 20-image cap (vs. the main flow's 100), is unused by the frontend UI, and is undocumented — not a functional bug (doesn't affect the main report-generation flow), but an inconsistency worth resolving (either raise to 100 or document the lower limit) if this endpoint is ever exposed to real API consumers.
- **RESOLVED 2026-08-15 (Phase 4, found during live verification):** the new `GET /api/reports/dashboard-summary` endpoint's Firestore `.aggregate({sum('imageCount')})` query threw `FAILED_PRECONDITION` (missing composite index) in the real project — this repo has no `firestore.indexes.json`/`firebase.json` at all, so index provisioning here is ad hoc/console-driven, not CLI-deployed. Fixed by switching to a `.select('imageCount')` projection summed in Node instead of a server-side aggregate, scoped to the requesting user's own reports only — see Phase 4 in `PHASES.md` for full detail.
- **RESOLVED 2026-08-15 (found during Phase 4 live verification, fixed same day on explicit follow-up request):** on mobile, the fixed-position sidebar-toggle FAB (`Dashboard.jsx` ~line 973, git-blamed to 2026-07-31, predates Phase 4) visually overlapped whatever content scrolled into the bottom-left viewport region on every view (site-wide, not Phase-4-specific — the new home view just made it visible). Fixed by adding a mobile-only spacer (`<div className="h-20 md:hidden" />`) right after the shared `<AnimatePresence>` in `<main>`, guaranteeing scrollable clearance below real content on every `activeView` without touching the FAB itself. Re-verified via Playwright on both Dashboard-home and My Reports at true bottom-of-scroll.
- **RESOLVED 2026-08-15 (found during Phase 4, fixed same day):** the sidebar profile-card banner's hardcoded hex gradient (leftover from before Phase 2's brand-token cleanup) replaced with `bg-gradient-to-br from-brand-500 via-brand-400 to-amber-400` — the same brand-token gradient pattern already used twice elsewhere in `Dashboard.jsx` for avatar circles.
- **RESOLVED 2026-08-15 (Phase 5, found and fixed during live verification):** the new Insured First/Last Name → Insured Name (and Street/City/State/Zip → Property Address) "auto-compose while empty" convenience logic had a live bug: typing into the first sub-field made the combined field non-empty, which then permanently blocked the *second* sub-field from ever contributing (Insured Name froze at "Jane" instead of becoming "Jane Verifier"). Fixed by comparing against the previous composed value instead of only checking emptiness — see Phase 5 in `PHASES.md` for full detail. Re-verified: both compositions now update correctly as each part is typed.
- ~~**`aiLimiter` applied to the entire `/reports` router**~~ — **RESOLVED 2026-08-16 (Phase 6 addendum):** found 2026-08-15 during Phase 5, re-confirmed as still-tripping during Phase 6's own live QA 2026-08-16 — `backend/server.js`'s router-mounting table applied the AI-specific `aiLimiter` (10 req/60s) to the entire `/reports` router, so ordinary reads (list/templates/dashboard-summary/the new Phase 6 gallery endpoints) shared the same tight budget as actual AI calls. **Fixed:** removed the router-level `pre` middleware entirely; `aiLimiter` (now in `backend/middleware/rateLimiters.js`, which already existed unused in the repo from an earlier attempt) is applied directly to only `POST /generate`, `POST /analyze-images`, `POST /:id/images`, `POST /:id/sections/suggest`. Live-verified: 30 rapid reads (`GET /reports` ×15, `GET /dashboard-summary` ×15) all returned 200/304 with zero 429s; a control test confirmed `/generate` itself still correctly 429s after 10 rapid calls within 60s, proving the actual protection wasn't weakened, only its scope.
- **NEW (found 2026-08-18 during Phase 10, fixed same session):** wiring `ReportPhotoGallery`'s new `onPhotosChange` callback (added to keep Phase 10's checklist synced with live photo-review state) initially called it from inside `setPhotos`'s updater-function form in `Dashboard.jsx` — since that fires during React's render-phase state resolution, calling a *different* component's setter from there (the parent's `setReviewPhotos`) triggered a real "Cannot update a component while rendering a different component" React warning on every Approve/Exclude/Edit action. **Fixed:** compute the next photos array from the `photos` closure directly and call both setters as independent top-level statements instead of nesting one inside the other's updater.
- **NEW (found 2026-08-18 during Phase 10, flagged not fixed — pre-existing, out of Phase 10's scope):** `EnterpriseDashboard.jsx`'s `handleGenerate()` still assumes Phase 7's pre-pipeline synchronous model — it treats the immediate `POST /generate` response as the finished report (`res.data.report`) and calls `autoPreviewPdf()` right away, even though the backend has responded instantly with `status: 'processing'`/`content: null` since Phase 7 and finishes the real work in the background. This dashboard never received Phase 7's polling/analysis-view treatment that `Dashboard.jsx` has. Not a Phase 10 regression — Phase 10's new checklist handles it gracefully (an honest empty/zero state, confirmed live, no crash) — but a real completeness gap in that dashboard worth a future fix.
- **RESOLVED 2026-08-18 (Phase 11, found and fixed during export-route implementation — Golden Rule #4):** `backend/config/tiers.js` has always defined `tier.exportFormats` (e.g. `['pdf']` for starter tier), but `POST /:id/export` never actually checked it before this phase — a starter-tier account could get a clean DOCX/HTML export by calling the API directly, bypassing the UI's own format-gating entirely (pure client-side enforcement, not server-side). **Fixed:** the route now returns `403 EXPORT_FORMAT_NOT_ALLOWED` for a disallowed format. Live-verified with a real captured bearer token: starter tier → 403 for `docx`/`html`, 200 for `pdf`.
- **RESOLVED 2026-08-18 (Phase 11, found during visual export verification):** unchecking the new "Include company branding" export option hid the small footer brand-attribution line in the DOCX and HTML generators but not in the PDF generator (whose footer text was never gated by that option). **Fixed:** standardized all three generators on the PDF's original behavior — the option only toggles the cover mark and header-bar logo/text; the footer's brand attribution (paired with the always-on confidentiality statement) is never hidden by it, in any format.
- **RESOLVED 2026-08-18 (Phase 12, found and fixed during live testing):** duplicating a report set the editor's content state to the literal JavaScript `null` (a fresh duplicate's `content` field is null until AI generation runs, which a duplicate deliberately never triggers), and `SectionedReportEditor`'s markdown parser (`String(content)`) rendered that as the literal text **"null"** inside the editor — a real, visible bug, not a crash. **Fixed:** the one call site that set `editableContent` from a duplicate's response now falls back to `''`, matching the null-guard convention every other call site in `Dashboard.jsx` already followed.
- **Claims tier-gating scope remains an open question (see `PHASES.md` Open Question #15, logged 2026-08-18):** Phase 12 completed the existing Agency+ Claims feature's filters/actions without deciding whether Claims should also be exposed to Starter/Professional tiers — deliberately left unresolved per explicit client instruction rather than guessed at.

## Important Files/Components (most referenced across phases — not exhaustive, see `CLAUDE.md` section 5 "Project Map" for the full map)

- `frontend/src/pages/Dashboard.jsx` — the largest, most central file; contains the report wizard, editor, review/approval, My Reports, and billing views via `activeView` state switching (not routes).
- `backend/routes/reports.js` — report/photo CRUD, generate, approve, export, share, versions.
- `backend/services/aiService.js` — all AI prompt logic (report generation, image analysis, section suggestions); contains the Phase 1 critical bug (`.slice(0, 10)`).
- `frontend/src/components/SectionedReportEditor.jsx` — now a full TipTap rich-text editor (Phase 9, 2026-08-16); see the dedicated entry below.
- `frontend/src/pages/EnterpriseDashboard.jsx` — enterprise-tier nav/views (Team, White-Label, API Keys, Settings), relevant to Phases 14, 16, 17.
- `frontend/src/pages/Settings.jsx` — current 5-tab settings, target of Phase 18.
- `backend/routes/teams.js`, `backend/routes/crm.js`, `backend/services/crmService.js` — team and claims backends.
- `backend/routes/webhooks.js`, `backend/services/auditLogService.js` — complete backends awaiting frontend UI (Phases 16, 17).
- `frontend/tailwind.config.js`, `frontend/src/index.css` — brand token source of truth, target of Phase 2.
- `frontend/src/pages/Home.jsx`, `Pricing.jsx`, `Security.jsx`, `Auth.jsx` — public/marketing pages; `Auth.jsx` (+ `App.jsx`'s `AuthRedirect`/`AuthLegacyRedirect`) was the target of Phase 3, now complete — remaining public-page gaps (Features/Photo Analysis/Solutions, Home.jsx completeness) are Phase 23.
- `backend/middleware/auth.js` — token verification for every authenticated request; now transient-failure-resilient (Phase 3). `backend/routes/users.js` — profile CRUD; `GET /profile`'s auto-create is now transaction-safe (Phase 3).
- `frontend/src/pages/Dashboard.jsx` now defaults to a new `activeView === 'home'` overview (Phase 4) instead of opening straight into the wizard; `backend/routes/reports.js`'s new `GET /dashboard-summary` (defined before any `/:id` route) backs its metric cards.
- The 5-step Generate Report wizard (`Dashboard.jsx`, still `activeView === 'generate'`) now captures the full claim/inspection/documentation data set (Phase 5): Policy Number/Insurance Company/Insured First-Last/Claim Type/Property Type in Step 1, structured Property Address + a new Inspection Details subsection in Step 2, a new Supporting Documents upload subsection in Step 4 (renamed "Upload Photos & Documents"), an explicit Save Draft button in the footer. Backend: `POST /generate` now accepts a `documents` file field alongside `images` (new `generateUpload` multer `.fields()` instance, `backend/routes/reports.js`), validated via new `backend/utils/documentValidation.js` (magic-byte sniffing, mirrors `imageValidation.js`) and stored under `users/{uid}/reports/{reportId}/documents/` (new `reportDocumentObject()` in `config/storage.js`); a new `GET /:id/documents/download` route retrieves them.
- **Phase 6 (2026-08-16):** Step 4's photo upload UX in `Dashboard.jsx` was rewritten (grid/list toggle, per-photo status/size/remove/rotate/preview, bulk actions, client-side dedup/corrupt detection) and the report doc gained a new `photos` per-photo-record array (`backend/routes/reports.js`, both `POST /generate` and `POST /:id/images`) alongside the still-derived `imagePaths`/`imageCount`. New `backend/utils/photoBatchProcessor.js` (per-photo isolation + SHA-256 dedup) and `backend/utils/thumbnailService.js` (`sharp`-based thumbnails + EXIF-orientation normalization) are the new backend core; `backend/config/storage.js` gained `reportThumbnailObject()`. New read-only gallery routes `GET /:id/photos` and `GET /:id/photos/:photoId/image` (with backward-compatible synthesis for pre-Phase-6 `imagePaths`-only reports) back a new `ReportPhotoGallery` component inside `ReportDetailModal`.
- **Phase 8 (2026-08-16):** each photo's `analysis` is now a structured per-photo classification (`location`/`category`/`severity`/`observation`/`confidence`, from a new taxonomy in `aiService.js`) instead of one whole-batch summary, plus a `review` state (`pending`/`approved`/`edited`/`excluded` + `note`) the reviewer controls via new `PUT /:id/photos/:photoId/review` (Edit/Approve/Exclude/Include/Note actions) and `POST /:id/photos/regenerate` endpoints. `ReportPhotoGallery` (`Dashboard.jsx`) gained an `interactive` mode used in both the post-generation view and My Reports' detail modal. `generateReport()`'s input is now built by the new `buildEffectiveImageAnalysis()` from each photo's reviewed state (not the raw AI batch output), and a new deterministic `insertPhotoObservations()` appendix guarantees a reviewer's exact edited text is verbatim visible in the exported report.
- **Phase 9 (2026-08-16):** `frontend/src/components/SectionedReportEditor.jsx` rewritten as a TipTap-based rich-text editor (same external `value`/`onChange` props as before) with a toolbar (bold/italic/underline/sub-heading/bullet+numbered list/table/photo/photo-grid/page-break/undo/redo), section add/delete/rename/reorder, a "FLACRON Tools" AI panel (7 functions via new `POST /:id/sections/assist`), and a distinct "Regenerate Section" modal (`POST /:id/sections/suggest` extended with `instructions`). Three new custom TipTap nodes live in `frontend/src/components/reportEditor/extensions.js` (`PageBreak`/`ReportPhoto`/`ReportPhotoGrid`). New `backend/utils/richContent.js` (mirrored in `frontend/src/utils/richContent.js`) defines the extended-Markdown dialect (`++underline++`, `1. numbered`, `{{page-break}}`, `![[photo:ID|caption]]`, `[[photos cols=N]]...[[/photos]]`) both sides parse/serialize — `report.content` itself is unchanged, still a single Markdown string. `aiService.js`'s 9 report-generation section titles were renamed to match the spec (numbering/content preserved, so Phase 1/8's regex-anchored safety nets needed no changes). `properPdfGenerator.js`/`documentGenerator.js`/`reports.js`'s `generateHTML` all gained real inline bold/italic/underline rendering, numbered-list support, `{{page-break}}` → real page breaks, and single/grid photo embedding (DOCX gained genuine OOXML `<w:drawing>` image embedding — media parts, relationships, `sharp`-read EMU sizing). A real bug (DOCX silently dropping any content before the first `## ` heading) was found and fixed in `documentGenerator.js`'s local `parseReportSections()`.
- **Phase 10 (2026-08-18):** new `frontend/src/components/ReportReviewChecklist.jsx` (Claim Data/Inspection Data/Photos/Documentation/Draft Sections + warnings, all computed client-side from existing data — no new backend endpoint). `ReportPhotoGallery` (`Dashboard.jsx`) gained an optional `onPhotosChange` prop so the checklist stays live-synced with Phase 8's per-photo review state without an extra fetch; `EnterpriseDashboard.jsx` (no interactive gallery) lets the checklist self-fetch `GET /:id/photos` instead. The "Approve & Finalize" button's `onClick` was split into a validation step (`handleApproveClick`) and the actual `POST /:id/approve` call (`handleApprove`, unchanged), now gated behind a `ConfirmDialog`-based "Approve this report?" Cancel/Approve modal in both dashboards.
- **Phase 11 (2026-08-18):** new `frontend/src/pages/ReportPreviewPage.jsx` (real `/reports/:id/preview` route, added to `App.jsx`) and `frontend/src/components/ExportOptionsModal.jsx` (shared by the preview page and `Dashboard.jsx`). `backend/routes/reports.js`'s `POST /:id/export` gained 5 validated boolean options + a clamped `photoLayout`, a new `buildAppendixPhotoList()` helper (Phase 8-aware, real reviewer-approved data only), and — a real fix, not new scope — an actual `tier.exportFormats` server-side check that didn't exist before. `properPdfGenerator.js`/`documentGenerator.js`/`generateHTML` all gained a togglable cover page, togglable company branding (cover mark + header logo/text only, never the footer attribution), a togglable page-number field, an always-on confidentiality-statement + claim-number footer line, and a real 1/2/4-photos-per-page appendix layout (number/caption/area/observation per photo) replacing the old fixed 2-column image-only grid. `Dashboard.jsx` gained a `?openReport=<id>` query-param handler (the preview page's "Edit" deep link) and several new "Open Full Preview" entry points (generate view, My Reports row, `ReportDetailModal`).
- **Phase 12 (2026-08-18):** `Dashboard.jsx`'s My Reports view gained a collapsible Filters panel and a new local `RowActionsMenu` component (Duplicate/Download/Share/Archive-or-Restore/Delete); `backend/routes/reports.js`'s `GET /` gained `reportType`/`clientId`/`claimNumber`/`creator` query params (alongside the pre-existing `lossType`/`status`/`startDate`/`endDate`/`search`), plus new `POST /:id/duplicate` and `POST /:id/restore` endpoints and a `createdByEmail`/`preArchiveStatus` field pair on the report doc. `CRM.jsx`'s Claims tab gained real search/date filters and Edit/Archive/Delete row actions (`NewClaimModal` gained an edit mode; Archive/Restore reuses the existing `PUT /claims/:id` with a new `archived` boolean field — no new backend route); `crmService.js`'s `getClaims` gained `startDate`/`endDate`/`archived` filtering. `CRMClaimProfile.jsx` was rebuilt from stacked sections into a real tabbed workspace (Overview/Reports/Photos), with a new `ClaimPhotosPanel` component aggregating real per-report photos (via the existing `GET /reports/:id/photos`) with Report/Category/Review-status filters.

All 15 unit/pure tests use dependency-injected mocks (no network access).

All batching/aggregation mechanics passed (15/15 analyzed, 0 failures, 0 skips, correct 2-batch split, concurrent execution, no timeout/API/parse errors). Not yet live-tested: full-scale 50-100 real photo sets, and real HEIC/mobile-camera images.

Frontend 0 lint errors/15 warnings, 4/4 tests, build succeeds. **Real, unmocked verification against the real Firebase project** (fresh throwaway QA account, deleted after use): (1) a genuine ~5.7MB real photo (not a tiny synthetic fixture) generated via `sharp`, uploaded through the actual wizard, produced an `originalPath` object byte-for-byte identical to the source file (5,685,172 bytes exactly) and a *separately-sized* `objectPath` display copy (5,767,932 bytes, re-encoded) at a different Storage path — direct, conclusive proof of 3-tier storage working correctly, not just unit-tested; (2) a direct HTTP call to `POST /:id/images` re-uploading an already-attached photo under a different filename was correctly flagged `duplicate` against the *existing* photo's stored filename, and correctly did not re-store it; a genuinely new photo added in the same call still uploaded normally; (3) 30 rapid direct HTTP reads (`GET /reports` ×15, `GET /dashboard-summary` ×15) all returned 200/304 with zero 429s (previously would have started 429ing after the 10th) — a control test also confirmed `POST /generate` itself still correctly 429s after 10 rapid calls, proving the fix rescoped the limiter without weakening it; (4) real per-photo upload-progress ticks were observed in a live browser using the same large real photo (genuine byte-progress: e.g. `2981888/5691393` → `5691393/5691393`, not simulated) — confirmed via a temporary debug log added and removed within this session, and via the UI's live "Photo N of M — X%" readout; (5) mobile viewport (390×844) confirmed both capture buttons visible with zero horizontal overflow; (6) Take Photo / Choose From Library buttons and the ready/failed/duplicate count breakdown all confirmed working in a full Playwright wizard walkthrough. HEIC support was tested directly against this real runtime (not assumed): `sharp` here can encode/decode real AV1-coded HEIF but explicitly cannot encode HEVC-coded HEIF (`Unsupported compression`) — strong, sourced evidence that genuine iPhone-style `.heic` won't decode either, though a literal real HEVC `.heic` file was not available in this sandboxed session to confirm decode directly (no camera, no licensed encoder, no internet-fetch capability). All test reports, the throwaway QA account (Auth user + Firestore profile + Storage objects), and every temporary QA/fixture/proxy script were deleted/removed before finishing — confirmed via `git status`, zero residue in the repo.

### 2026-08-18 (session 18) — Phase 13 implementation: Real Template Builder
- **Trigger:** explicit client instruction ("Start Phase 13 only"), following session 17's Phase 12 completion.
- **What was implemented:** see `PHASES.md` Phase 13's "Implementation summary" for full per-task detail. Summary: a new, real structural template system (new `templates` Firestore collection, `backend/services/templateService.js` + `backend/routes/templates.js` at `/api/templates`) alongside the untouched, still-working legacy shallow `reportTemplates` collection. Covers name/description/wizard-field defaults/a template-chosen required-field subset/Phase-9-shaped custom sections/Phase-11-shaped photo-layout export defaults/org branding, across `personal`/`organization`/`flacron` scopes. "Organization" reuses the existing enterprise-team model (`teams.js`'s `teamOwnerId`/`teamRole`), not a new concept. Real Create/Edit/Duplicate/Archive/Restore/Delete, a standalone `/templates` list page + `/templates/new`/`/templates/:id/edit` builder (ahead of Phase 30, same precedent as `ReportPreviewPage.jsx`), a `TemplatePickerModal` wired into the wizard's new "Start From a Report Template" step, and 6 seeded Flacron example templates (Residential Property, Water Loss, Roof, Wind/Hail, Fire, Commercial Property). `POST /generate` resolves `templateId` server-side, enforces the template's `requiredFields`, and persists `templateId`/`templateGuidance`/`templateSections`/`exportDefaults`/`templateBranding` onto the report; a template's custom sections are deterministically appended to the generated content (`appendTemplateSections`, new in `richContent.js`) rather than left to prompt luck, and this re-applies correctly on retry/regenerate.
- **Two real bugs found and fixed, both inside this phase's own new branding-fallback work:** (1) a template's `logoObjectPath`/`logoUrl` were designed to be settable ONLY via the dedicated `/logo` upload route, never through the general edit endpoint — closing a potential IDOR (a client could otherwise reference an arbitrary Storage path, e.g. another user's private photo, and have it silently downloaded into every export from that template) before it ever shipped. (2) The new `templateBranding` fallback for `companyName`/`reportFooter` in `POST /:id/export` resolved correctly but never actually rendered, because the export generators only show custom branding text when `hideFlacronBranding` is true (an enterprise white-label-only switch) — fixed with an additional narrowly-scoped fallback that only engages when there's no white-label config and no personal `userData.company`, verified live across HTML/DOCX/PDF.
- **A pre-existing, unrelated gap found and flagged, not fixed:** non-enterprise users' own Settings → Profile "Company Name" field has the identical silent-inert-ness bug (2) above describes — predates this phase, left untouched to avoid scope creep into an already-shipped, unrelated feature.

### 2026-08-18 (session 15) — Phase 10 implementation: Report Review Checklist & Approval Modal
- **Trigger:** explicit client instruction ("Start Phase 10 only"), following session 14's Phase 9 completion.
- **What was implemented:** see `PHASES.md` Phase 10's "Implementation summary" for full per-task detail. Summary: a new, purely read-only `frontend/src/components/ReportReviewChecklist.jsx` computes 5 checklist rows (Claim Data, Inspection Data, Photos analyzed/reviewed, Documentation, Draft Sections) entirely from data the app already persists — no new backend endpoint was needed, since every field was already composable client-side. Three warning banners (missing claim number, excluded/unreviewed photos, missing inspection date) render only when genuinely true, using neutral language. A confirmation modal (the existing shared `ConfirmDialog` component, not a new one) now sits in front of the existing, unmodified `POST /:id/approve` call in both `Dashboard.jsx` and `EnterpriseDashboard.jsx` — the "Approve & Finalize" button's client-side validation now opens the modal instead of calling the API directly; the modal's own Approve button fires the unchanged backend call.
- **The one real engineering problem this phase had to solve:** Phase 8's interactive Photo Review gallery keeps its own live per-photo review state that the report doc's own `photos` snapshot goes stale against the moment a reviewer Approves/Excludes/Edits a photo. Rather than add a backend read-endpoint, `ReportPhotoGallery` gained an optional `onPhotosChange` callback (fired on its initial fetch and every successful review action) that `Dashboard.jsx` threads into the checklist, so it updates live with zero extra network calls; `EnterpriseDashboard.jsx` (which has no interactive gallery of its own) instead lets the checklist component self-fetch `GET /:id/photos` when no `photos` prop is supplied.
- **A real bug found and fixed during live testing:** wiring `onPhotosChange` initially called it from inside `setPhotos`'s updater-function form in `ReportPhotoGallery` — this fires during React's render-phase state resolution, and calling a *different* component's setter (`Dashboard`'s `setReviewPhotos`) from there triggered a genuine "Cannot update a component while rendering a different component" React warning on every photo review action. Fixed by computing the next photos array from the `photos` closure directly and calling both setters as independent top-level calls.
- **A pre-existing, unrelated gap found and flagged (not fixed, out of scope):** `EnterpriseDashboard.jsx`'s report generation still assumes Phase 7's pre-pipeline synchronous model (treats the immediate `POST /generate` response as the finished report) — a gap from Phase 7 never being retrofitted into that dashboard. The new checklist handles it gracefully (an honest empty/zero state, no crash, no fabricated data) but the dashboard itself would benefit from the same async-aware polling `Dashboard.jsx` already has.
- **Files created:** `frontend/src/components/ReportReviewChecklist.jsx`. **Files modified:** `frontend/src/pages/Dashboard.jsx` (checklist card, confirmation modal, `onPhotosChange` wiring + the setState-during-render fix), `frontend/src/pages/EnterpriseDashboard.jsx` (checklist card, confirmation modal, mirroring `Dashboard.jsx`). No backend files changed.
- **Tests/checks performed and results:** backend `npm test` → **135/135 passing** (unchanged — no backend code touched), `npm run lint` → 0 errors, 43 warnings (unchanged baseline). Frontend `npm run lint` → 0 errors, 15 warnings (unchanged baseline). Frontend `npm test` → **15/15 passing** (unchanged). Frontend `npm run build` → succeeds.
- **Live verification (real Firebase project + real Anthropic API, 3 throwaway QA accounts — 2 agency-tier with different data-completeness profiles, 1 enterprise-tier — created via the Firebase Admin SDK and fully deleted afterward along with their reports and Storage objects; confirmed zero repo residue via `git status`):** see "Testing Status" above for the complete list. Standout results: a fully-complete report's checklist correctly went from "0 of 2 reviewed" with a warning to "2 of 2 reviewed" with zero warnings live, with no page reload, after approving both photos in the gallery; a deliberately incomplete report simultaneously surfaced all three warning types (missing inspection date, excluded photo, unreviewed photo) correctly; the confirmation modal's Cancel path (leaves the report as an untouched draft) and Approve path (finalizes exactly as before, signature persists, survives a refresh) were both exercised on desktop and the Approve/Cancel affordance was also exercised on a real 390×844 mobile viewport with zero horizontal overflow; an EnterpriseDashboard spot check confirmed the checklist self-fetches its data there and doesn't crash on a still-processing/null-content report. Zero console errors and zero unexpected failed/5xx HTTP requests across every pass, aside from a handful of local dev rate-limiter (10 req/60s on `/generate`, 100 req/15min globally) trips caused by this session's own rapid repeated automated test runs — each confirmed as a testing artifact (not a regression) by restarting the backend (which resets the in-memory limiter) and re-running cleanly.

### 2026-08-16 (session 14) — Phase 9 implementation: Report Editor Rich-Text & AI Panel Upgrade
- **Trigger:** explicit client instruction ("Start Phase 9 only"), following session 13's Phase 8 completion. Implemented ahead of Phase 30 (routing migration) in the Phase Summary Table's stated dependency order — judged safe because Phase 9 upgrades `SectionedReportEditor.jsx` in place rather than adding any new page/route; its external `value`/`onChange` contract to `Dashboard.jsx`/`EnterpriseDashboard.jsx` is unchanged.
- **What was implemented:** see `PHASES.md` Phase 9's "Implementation summary" for full per-task detail. Summary: `SectionedReportEditor.jsx` rewritten from plain textareas to a TipTap 2 rich-text editor (chosen over Slate/hand-rolled contentEditable for its ProseMirror schema-based safety and first-class custom-node support) with a full formatting toolbar, section add/delete/rename/reorder, a "FLACRON Tools" AI panel (7 writing-assistance functions via new `POST /:id/sections/assist`), and a distinct "Regenerate Section" modal (instructions → live comparison → Approve/Cancel, built by extending the existing `POST /:id/sections/suggest` with an `instructions` field rather than adding an 8th near-duplicate action). `report.content` deliberately stays a single Markdown string — an extended dialect (`++underline++`, `1. numbered`, `{{page-break}}`, `![[photo:ID|caption]]`, `[[photos cols=N]]...[[/photos]]`) documented in new `backend/utils/richContent.js` (mirrored in `frontend/src/utils/richContent.js`) and rendered by three new custom TipTap nodes (`frontend/src/components/reportEditor/extensions.js`). `aiService.js`'s 9 report-generation section titles were renamed to reconcile with the spec's required list, numbering/content preserved so Phase 1/8's regex-anchored safety nets (`ensureLossSummary`/`ensureConclusion`/`insertPhotoObservations`/`insertNoPhotoDisclaimer`) needed zero changes. All 3 export generators (`properPdfGenerator.js`, `documentGenerator.js`, `reports.js`'s `generateHTML`) gained real inline bold/italic/underline, numbered lists, real page breaks, and photo/photo-grid embedding (DOCX via genuine OOXML `<w:drawing>` image embedding with `sharp`-read EMU sizing).
- **A real bug found and fixed during live testing:** `documentGenerator.js`'s own local section-splitter silently dropped any content before the first `## ` heading — a low-stakes pre-existing gap (typically just the report's H1 title line) that became a genuine data-loss bug once Regenerate Section could place real content (a live-tested photo + photo grid + narrative) into exactly that position, since the frontend's own "Introduction" pseudo-section always re-serializes with no `##` prefix. Confirmed via a real DOCX export showing 0 embedded images despite a correct `photoMap`, while PDF/HTML (which don't pre-split into sections) rendered correctly. Fixed by carrying that content through as a `{title: null}` pseudo-section with no synthetic Word heading rendered for it; re-verified against the same real report; 2 new regression tests added (`rich-export.test.js`).
- **Files created:** `backend/utils/richContent.js`, `backend/test/rich-content.test.js`, `backend/test/rich-export.test.js`, `backend/test/section-assist.test.js`, `frontend/src/utils/richContent.js`, `frontend/src/__tests__/richContent.test.js`, `frontend/src/components/reportEditor/extensions.js`. **Files rewritten:** `frontend/src/components/SectionedReportEditor.jsx`. **Files modified:** `backend/services/aiService.js`, `backend/routes/reports.js`, `backend/utils/properPdfGenerator.js`, `backend/utils/documentGenerator.js`, `frontend/src/services/api.js`, `frontend/src/index.css`, `frontend/package.json`/`package-lock.json` (9 new `@tiptap/*` deps).

### 2026-08-16 (session 13) — Phase 8 implementation: Per-Photo Analysis Review UI
- **Trigger:** explicit client instruction ("Start Phase 8 only"), following session 12's Phase 7 completion (which had been left awaiting explicit approval before any further phase work).
- **What was implemented:** see `PHASES.md` Phase 8's "Implementation summary" for full per-task detail. Summary: the vision-analysis prompt (`backend/services/aiService.js`) now requests one structured entry per photo (`location`/`category`/`severity`/`observation`/`confidence`, guided by a new 16-location/11-category taxonomy authored for this session — no external spec file existed in-repo to source one from) instead of a whole-batch summary, with `normalizePhotoEntries` guaranteeing exact 1:1 photo-to-entry alignment even on a parse failure. Each photo's result is persisted on its own record (new `analysis` field) via an extended `photoJobService.js` (`applyBatchAnalysisResults`, shared `makeOnBatchComplete`), alongside a new `review` field (`pending`/`approved`/`edited`/`excluded` + `note`) defaulted at upload time in `photoBatchProcessor.js`. New pure function `buildEffectiveImageAnalysis()` builds `generateReport()`'s actual input from each photo's reviewed state (excluded photos dropped, edited text substituted) — this is now used for EVERY generation, not just a manual regenerate. New endpoints `PUT /:id/photos/:photoId/review` (Edit/Approve/Exclude/Include/Note, transactional, ownership-checked, rejects legacy synthetic ids) and `POST /:id/photos/regenerate` (rebuilds content from current review state, mutex-guarded against duplicate concurrent clicks via a `regenerating` flag, reopens a finalized report as draft per Golden Rule #3). Frontend: `ReportPhotoGallery` (`Dashboard.jsx`) gained an `interactive` mode (review-status dots, an enhanced preview modal with location/category/confidence badges, an editable observation textarea, Approve/Exclude/Restore buttons, a note field, and a "Regenerate Report" action) used in both the post-generation view and My Reports' detail modal.
- **A deliberate, non-task-list addition made during live verification (not a bug, but a real gap in the phase's own acceptance criterion):** testing task 4's "confirm edits are reflected in the output" against the real Anthropic API showed the report-generation prompt's free-form prose may paraphrase or omit a single edited photo observation entirely (Section 5's narrative is driven mainly by the adjuster's own `damagesObserved`/`lossDescription` text when provided, not the photo JSON) — inherent LLM summarization behavior, not a defect, but it made the acceptance criterion merely probabilistic rather than actually guaranteed. Fixed with a new deterministic safety net, `insertPhotoObservations()` (mirrors the existing `ensureLossSummary`/`ensureConclusion`/`insertNoPhotoDisclaimer` pattern in `aiService.js`), appending a verbatim, numbered "Per-Photo Observations (reviewed)" list under Section 8 for every active photo — guaranteeing an edit's exact text appears in the export and an excluded photo's text never does, independent of model behavior.
- **Files created:** none (extends existing Phase 6/7 files). **Files modified:** `backend/services/aiService.js`, `backend/utils/photoBatchProcessor.js`, `backend/services/photoJobService.js`, `backend/routes/reports.js`, `frontend/src/services/api.js`, `frontend/src/pages/Dashboard.jsx`, `backend/test/image-analysis-batching.test.js` (rewritten for the new schema), `backend/test/photo-job-service.test.js` (7 new tests), `backend/test/photo-batch-processor.test.js` (1 new test).
- **Tests/checks performed and results:** backend `npm test` → **106/106 passing** (0 regressions, ~12 new/rewritten). `npm run lint` → 0 errors, 43 warnings (baseline 40 + 3 new accepted-pattern warnings). Frontend `npm run lint` → 0 errors, 15 warnings (unchanged). Frontend `npm test` → 4/4. Frontend `npm run build` → succeeds.
- **Live verification (real Firebase project + real Anthropic API, a throwaway QA account — `emailVerified:true` set directly via the Firebase Admin SDK so no real inbox was needed — plus a second throwaway account for the cross-user authorization test, both fully deleted afterward along with 2 test reports, their Storage objects, and `analysisJobs` docs; confirmed zero residue via `git status`):** see "Testing Status" above for the complete list. Standout results: a full wizard-to-regenerate Playwright pass (15/15 checks); direct Firestore inspection proving the edited text appears verbatim in `imageAnalysis.damages` AND in the exported `content` (via the new appendix), while the excluded photo's text appears in neither; 8/8 authorization/edge-case HTTP checks including a genuinely separate second account correctly denied 404 (not leaked) on the first account's report; a retry-preserves-review-state check (a photo forced into `needs_attention`, retried against the real API, re-analyzed fresh, with its prior edit+note confirmed still intact); a genuine legacy-shaped (pre-Phase-6) report confirmed compatible (`reviewable:false`, no crash); persistence across navigation + a true hard refresh (6/6); mobile viewport 390×844 (4/4, zero horizontal overflow) — this pass also incidentally gave Phase 7's analysis-progress view its first real-browser confirmation.
- **Not covered live (flagged, not glossed over):** the `ANALYSIS_PENDING` guard branch specifically (as opposed to the `ANALYSIS_NOT_AVAILABLE` branch, which WAS tested) and true concurrent-editor races — both reasoned as correct from code inspection only; see `PHASES.md` Phase 8 for why.
- **Updated phase statuses:** Phase 8 → Completed in `PHASES.md` and here. Phase 7's frontend-verification gap → closed (incidentally, via this session's own testing). All other phases unchanged.

### 2026-08-16 (session 12) — Phase 7 implementation: Async Photo Analysis Pipeline
- **Trigger:** explicit client instruction ("start phase 7"), following session 11's Phase 6 addendum completion. Mid-implementation, the client was asked (via a structured question, the only "response" received this session) to choose the job-queue technology per Phase 7's task 1 open question, and selected **in-process async queue** — no new infra, Firestore itself as the durable job-status store, accepting that in-flight work does not survive a Render restart mid-job.
- **What was implemented:** see `PHASES.md` Phase 7's "Implementation summary" for full per-task detail. Summary: new `backend/services/photoJobService.js` orchestrates the whole pipeline; new `analysisJobs` Firestore collection (spec §15's exact field list) tracks per-photo and per-generation-stage status; `POST /generate` uploads photos synchronously then responds immediately, running `runReportPipeline` as a background, internally-caught async function; new `GET /:id/analysis-status` polling endpoint; automatic in-batch retry (`MAX_BATCH_ATTEMPTS = 3` in `aiService.analyzeBatch`) landing failed photos in a distinct `needs_attention` status (task 7); manual `POST /:id/analysis/retry` re-downloads already-uploaded bytes and merges fresh results with prior successful ones via a new `mergeImageAnalysis()` helper; 409 `REPORT_PROCESSING` guards on `PUT /:id`/`POST /:id/approve`/`POST /:id/export`; `POST /:id/images` also rewired to the same async pattern. Frontend: a new FLACRON-ENGINE-branded `activeView === 'analysis'` view in `Dashboard.jsx` (task 9) with 3s polling, and My-Reports rows for `status: 'processing'` reports now route into this view (`openReport()`) instead of the normal detail modal, enabling navigate-away-and-back resumption (task 6).
- **A real bug caught and fixed before shipping (not a pre-planned task):** the initial `retryFailedAnalysis`/`runReportPipeline` design didn't actually use/merge `existingImageAnalysis` — a partial retry (re-analyzing just `needs_attention` photos) would have silently overwritten the entire `imageAnalysis` field, discarding every already-successful photo's findings. Fixed by threading `existingImageAnalysis` through and merging via `mergeImageAnalysis` whenever a partial retry result comes back.
- **A real, load-bearing performance defect found during this phase's own live verification (not a pre-planned task, but directly relevant to Phase 7's acceptance criteria):** `processPhotoBatch` (`backend/utils/photoBatchProcessor.js`) processed uploaded files fully sequentially (3 Storage round-trips per file: original/display/thumbnail) — measured at **28.7 seconds** for a 12-photo `POST /generate`, which would have left Phase 7's own "client never waits" promise measurably broken for its own target scenario (photo uploads, not just the AI stage, needed to stop blocking). Fixed by restructuring into a fast sequential CPU-only validation+SHA-256-dedup pass (kept sequential deliberately, so two copies of a duplicate can never both slip past the "not yet seen" check concurrently), followed by a concurrency-capped worker pool (`UPLOAD_CONCURRENCY = 8`, same wave-based pattern as the existing `VISION_BATCH_CONCURRENCY`) for the actual Storage I/O. Response time for the same request dropped to ~9-11 seconds (measured 11022ms/9589ms/9061ms across 3 separate live runs) — a 61-68% reduction.
- **Files created:** `backend/services/photoJobService.js`, `backend/test/photo-job-service.test.js` (5 tests). **Files modified:** `backend/services/aiService.js` (retry + `onBatchComplete` progress callback), `backend/utils/photoBatchProcessor.js` (concurrency-capped upload restructure), `backend/routes/reports.js` (async `/generate`/`/:id/images`, new `/analysis-status`/`/analysis/retry` routes, 409 guards), `backend/test/image-analysis-batching.test.js` (rewritten to key mock failures by content instead of call-index, since retries + concurrency make raw ordering non-deterministic), `backend/test/photo-batch-processor.test.js` (2 upload-failure tests rewritten the same way), `frontend/src/services/api.js` (`getAnalysisStatus`/`retryAnalysis`), `frontend/src/pages/Dashboard.jsx` (new analysis view, polling, `openReport()` resumption, simplified generation-step UI).
- **Tests/checks performed and results:** backend `npm test` → **94/94 passing** (0 regressions; 6 new: 5 `mergeImageAnalysis` pure-function tests + retry/onBatchComplete coverage in the batching test file), `npm run lint` → 0 errors (pre-existing warning count unchanged). Frontend `npm test` → 4/4, `npm run lint` → 0 errors (unchanged), `npm run build` → succeeds.
- **Live verification (real Firebase project + real Anthropic API, throwaway QA account created and fully deleted afterward — reports, `analysisJobs` docs, Firestore user doc, and the Firebase Auth user itself; confirmed zero residue via `git status`):** see "Testing Status" above for the complete list. Standout results: the 24-photo/3-batch run proving the Firestore-transaction fix holds under 3+ simultaneously-completing vision batches (not just 2, as the initial 12-photo test only exercised); the retry test proving the merge-not-replace fix works for real (analyzed count increased from 4 to 5 after retrying 1 photo, not reset to 1); all three 409 guards and the async `/:id/images` behavior confirmed correct.
- **Not verified this session:** the new frontend analysis view in an actual browser (rendering, live polling, resume-on-navigate-away-and-back, mobile viewport) — **no browser-automation tool was available in this session**, unlike every prior phase. This is an honest, explicitly-flagged gap, not something glossed over — see `PHASES.md` Phase 7 and "Current Active Phase" above.
- **Updated phase statuses:** Phase 7 → Completed in `PHASES.md` and here (with the browser-verification caveat noted). All other phases unchanged.
- **Client instruction, standing:** do NOT commit, push, or deploy anything — preserve all current local changes exactly as they are.
- **Exact next recommended step:** await explicit instruction. Recommend a real-browser verification pass for Phase 7's frontend (by the client directly, or in a future session with a browser-automation tool connected) before treating this phase as fully QA-complete, then proceed to Phase 8 (Per-Photo Analysis Review UI) once that's confirmed or the client accepts the current level of verification.

### 2026-08-16 (session 11) — Phase 6 addendum implementation + routing-migration plan
- **Trigger:** client approved session 10's full-spec gap report/phase mapping, then resolved two of its open questions directly: (1) routing architecture — build real, bookmarkable routes matching the spec, migrated incrementally without breaking current navigation/saved links, with the migration plan added to the roadmap *before* the phases that depend on it; (2) photo storage — confirmed the 3-tier model (untouched original / normalized-optimized display / thumbnail), original bytes never overwritten. Then directed: reopen the Phase 6 addendum, fix its two confirmed defects, implement every remaining Phase-6-compatible item, and move only genuinely queue-dependent work to Phase 7 — with real HEIC support specifically verified against the actual runtime, not assumed.
- **Planning work (no code):** added new **Phase 30 — Routing Migration: Real, Bookmarkable URLs** to `PHASES.md`, with a 5-stage incremental migration plan (shared layout + nested routes scaffold with zero visible change → migrate lowest-risk views first → preserve Stripe-checkout query-param flows → redirect handling → decide how filter/search state gets encoded in the URL). Updated the Phase Summary Table's Depends-On column so Phases 9,10,12,13,14,15,16,17,22 all reference it, with a note explaining why it's numbered last despite needing to land first. Marked Open Questions #10 (routing) and #14 (3-tier storage) RESOLVED with the client's decisions.
- **Code implementation — Phase 6 addendum:**
  - **3-tier photo storage (defect #4, FIXED):** new `reportOriginalObject()` path builder (`backend/config/storage.js`); `backend/utils/photoBatchProcessor.js` now uploads the untouched original first, then the EXIF-normalized display copy (same `objectPath` name/role as before, so every existing reader — AI analysis, exports, gallery — is unaffected), then the thumbnail; a storage failure on any one tier isolates to that photo without touching tiers already saved. `GET /:id/photos/:photoId/image` gained a `variant=original` option. Deletion cleanup extended to remove original-tier objects too.
  - **Cross-existing-report duplicate detection (defect #7, FIXED):** `processPhotoBatch()` gained a new `existingHashes` parameter (each attached photo's persisted `contentHash` + `fileName`), seeded into its dedup map before processing new files; each successful photo record now stores its own `contentHash`. Wired into `POST /:id/images` (the only route with pre-existing photos to check against).
  - **Mobile Take Photo / Choose From Library (item 1, IMPLEMENTED):** two explicit buttons in the wizard's Step 4 — "Take Photo" (new hidden `<input capture="environment">`) and "Choose From Library" (the existing input, relabeled) — plus a one-time continuous-capture hint.
  - **HEIC support (item 2, VERIFIED against this real runtime, not fixed):** directly tested this exact installed `sharp@0.33.5`/libvips/libheif build: it successfully encodes real AV1-coded HEIF (confirmed via inspecting the output's `ftyp avif` brand bytes) but throws `Unsupported compression` for HEVC-coded HEIF — the codec real iPhones use. This is strong, current-runtime evidence (not a documentation guess) that genuine `.heic` files won't decode either, since libheif registers a codec's encode/decode support together and only the license-clean AV1 path is bundled. Could not test decode against a literal real `.heic` file in this sandboxed session (no camera, no licensed HEVC encoder, no internet-fetch capability) — flagged as needing either a client-supplied real file or a decision to add a conversion library (`heic-convert`).
  - **Per-image upload progress (item 3a, IMPLEMENTED):** `reportsAPI.generate(formData, onUploadProgress)` now threads a real axios `onUploadProgress` callback through; the "Generating Your Report" overlay shows a genuine, byte-progress-driven "Photo N of M — X%" readout during the actual upload, falling back to the existing interval-based step advance only for later stages the backend doesn't yet report granular progress for. Item 3b (true per-file network concurrency) confirmed to require Phase 7's architecture, untouched.
  - **Counting breakdown (item 8, FIXED):** the wizard's photo-count header now shows "N ready · N failed · N duplicate" whenever any photos aren't ready, instead of one ambiguous total.
  - **`aiLimiter` rescoping (item 9, FIXED):** removed from `server.js`'s whole-router mount; applied directly (via the already-existing-but-unused `backend/middleware/rateLimiters.js`) to only `POST /generate`, `POST /analyze-images`, `POST /:id/images`, `POST /:id/sections/suggest`.
  - **Items deliberately NOT touched:** 5 (low-quality warning) and 6 (ordering/grouping/annotations) remain correctly assigned to Phase 24.
- **Files created:** none. **Files modified:** `backend/config/storage.js`, `backend/utils/photoBatchProcessor.js`, `backend/routes/reports.js`, `backend/server.js`, `backend/test/photo-batch-processor.test.js` (13 tests, up from 10), `frontend/src/pages/Dashboard.jsx`, `frontend/src/services/api.js`.
- **Tests/checks performed and results:** backend `npm test` → 85/85 (0 regressions), `npm run lint` → 0 errors/40 warnings (unchanged). Frontend `npm run lint` → 0 errors/15 warnings (unchanged), `npm test` → 4/4, `npm run build` → succeeds.
- **Live verification (real Firebase project, throwaway QA account created and fully deleted afterward):** see "Testing Status" above for the complete list — the standout result is a genuine ~5.7MB real photo producing byte-for-byte-identical original storage, a separately-sized re-encoded display copy, real multi-tick upload-progress readings, correct cross-report duplicate rejection via a direct HTTP call, and 30/30 rapid reads succeeding with zero 429s (versus a control confirming `/generate` itself still correctly rate-limits at 10/60s).
- **A real test-script confusion encountered and resolved (not a product bug):** an early pass of the large-photo upload test showed a report saved with only 2 of 3 intended photos, while the browser's own upload-progress event showed ~5.6MB transferred — investigated and resolved by re-running with a longer, explicit wait for client-side hash/decode validation to finish before clicking Generate (a large file's `crypto.subtle.digest` hashing genuinely takes longer than a 1200ms fixed wait); a cleaner, more deliberate re-test confirmed all 3 photos upload correctly when given adequate validation time. Documented here so a future session doesn't waste time rediscovering this same test-timing artifact.

### 2026-08-16 (session 9) — Phase 6 implementation: Photo Upload & Per-Photo UX Hardening
- **Trigger:** explicit client instruction to start Phase 6 only, per `PHASES.md`'s spec, without implementing Phase 7's async analysis pipeline. The phase-by-phase re-audit of already-"Completed" phases (Phases 1-4 done, Phase 5 not yet independently re-audited) was explicitly set aside for this — Phase 5's re-audit remains an open loose end, not abandoned.
- **What was implemented:** see `PHASES.md` Phase 6 for full detail. Summary: a new per-photo record model (`photos` array on the report doc: id/fileName/size/mimeType/status/objectPath/thumbnailPath/error/uploadedAt) replacing the implicit assumption that `imagePaths` was the only source of truth (kept in lockstep for backward compatibility); a new `backend/utils/photoBatchProcessor.js` that isolates one corrupt/duplicate photo's failure to its own record instead of rejecting the whole upload request (used by both `POST /generate` and `POST /:id/images`); a new `backend/utils/thumbnailService.js` using the already-installed `sharp` for real server-generated thumbnails plus EXIF-orientation normalization on the full stored image (fixes sideways photos in PDF/DOCX exports, which don't read EXIF themselves); SHA-256 content-hash duplicate detection, both client-side (instant wizard feedback) and server-side (authoritative, defense-in-depth); a full rewrite of the wizard's Step 4 photo UI in `Dashboard.jsx` (grid/list toggle, per-photo status/size/remove/rotate/preview, Select All/Remove Selected/Retry Failed Uploads bulk actions, the exact spec'd 100-photo-limit message); and a new read-only `ReportPhotoGallery` component (backed by new `GET /:id/photos` + `GET /:id/photos/:photoId/image` routes) inside `ReportDetailModal`, with full backward-compatible synthesis for reports created before this phase that only have the old flat `imagePaths`/`imageCount` shape.
- **Architectural decision:** deliberately stayed synchronous (photos still upload atomically with the report at generate-time, no pre-generate upload endpoint, no job queue) — per explicit instruction not to build Phase 7's async pipeline this session. Per-photo hardening splits into a client-side layer (wizard, pre-generate, since files aren't on the server yet) and a server-side layer (the two real upload moments: `/generate` and `/:id/images`) that does the same checks authoritatively.
- **Files created:** `backend/utils/photoBatchProcessor.js`, `backend/utils/thumbnailService.js`, `backend/test/photo-batch-processor.test.js` (10 tests, dependency-injected against fake Storage/sharp calls, matching the existing `aiService.analyzeImages`/`callVisionApi` DI test pattern — no real network in unit tests).
- **Files modified:** `backend/routes/reports.js` (multer `fileFilter` relaxed for the `images` field — a mislabeled/unsupported mimetype used to 500 the entire multipart request at the multer layer before any route code ran; now deferred to per-file magic-byte validation; `/generate` and `/:id/images` rewritten around `processPhotoBatch`; new `/:id/photos` + `/:id/photos/:photoId/image` routes; delete-cleanup extended to thumbnail objects; removed the now-dead `persistReportImages` helper), `backend/config/storage.js` (new `reportThumbnailObject()` path builder), `frontend/src/pages/Dashboard.jsx` (Step 4 rewrite + new state/handlers + `PhotoStatusBadge`/`ReportPhotoGallery` components), `frontend/src/services/api.js` (new `getPhotos`/`getPhotoImageBlob` methods).
- **Bugs found and fixed during this session (all in my own new code, caught before finishing, not shipped defects):** an initial draft imported unused `Copy`/`Maximize2` lucide icons (removed before final lint pass); an initial test for the storage-upload-failure path in `photo-batch-processor.test.js` used a substring match on the generated object path that never actually matched (the path uses a timestamp+random name, not the original filename) — fixed to fail by call-index instead, so the test genuinely exercises the failure branch instead of silently passing for the wrong reason.
- **Tests/checks performed and results:** backend `npm test` → 82/82 passing (72 pre-existing + 10 new, 0 regressions), `npm run lint` → 0 errors/40 warnings (baseline 38 + 2 new, matching the existing accepted `catch(err)`-unused pattern). ### 2026-08-16 (session 7) — Phase 3 re-audit: Auth Pages, with special focus on client-reported routing bugs
- **Trigger:** the client manually tested the live app and reported `/login`/`/signup` not visibly rendering, `/auth` not redirecting, and the dashboard greeting missing — all despite these phases being logged "Completed." Instructed to re-audit Phase 3 specifically with focus on the routing reports, implement any missing functionality, and do exhaustive live-browser verification (direct URL, refresh, back/forward, mode correctness, auth states, field persistence, Google auth, protected routes).
- **Critical finding surfaced immediately:** `git log` confirmed none of Phase 1-5's work has ever been committed (see the callout above) — this alone plausibly explains every originally-reported symptom if the client tested anything other than this exact local working tree.
- **Code audit:** re-read `App.jsx`, `Auth.jsx`, `ProtectedRoute.jsx` fully against Phase 3's spec — all documented functionality (dedicated routes, legacy redirect, route-driven mode, split name fields, Work Email, Company, CTA text, updated `/auth` links) confirmed present and structurally correct.
- **Real bug found and fixed:** `switchMode()` in `Auth.jsx` used `navigate(path, { replace: true })` for the Login/Signup tab toggle and the bottom mode-switch link — this replaced the browser history entry instead of pushing one, so pressing Back after switching modes skipped past both `/login` and `/signup` entirely, landing on whichever page preceded the auth flow. Live-reproduced with Playwright (goto /login → click Signup tab → Back → landed on /signup instead of /login). **Fixed** by removing `replace: true` so mode switches push real history entries; re-verified Back/Forward correctly step between the two modes.
- **False lead investigated and cleared:** two early throwaway signup-flow test runs showed `firstName`/`lastName`/`company` missing from the resulting Firestore document, suggesting a persistence bug. Full request/response tracing revealed the real cause: `persistSignupProfileDetails()` is an intentional fire-and-forget `GET /profile` → `PUT /profile` sequence that takes ~4s against the real Firebase backend; my test scripts were closing the browser (aborting the in-flight PUT) before it completed. A run that waited a full 12s showed the complete GET 200 → PUT 200 sequence and correct Firestore persistence of all fields. **No code change needed — field persistence works correctly**, this was purely a test-harness timing artifact.
- **Live-browser verification (exhaustive, per the client's explicit checklist):** direct URL entry (`/login`, `/signup`, `/auth`, `/auth?mode=signup(&plan=)`), refresh on each route, back/forward mode-toggling (post-fix), unauthenticated-protected-route redirects, login-then-return-to-original-destination, session-persists-on-refresh, already-authenticated bounce-away from `/login`/`/signup`, tier-gated route (Upgrade Required, no crash), full real signup (consent-gate blocks submission, real account reaches Verify-your-email with correct field persistence), Google auth button + consent-gate (no popup without consent) — all at desktop, plus a dedicated mobile-viewport (390×844) pass for `/login`/`/signup` (no overflow, mobile login reaches dashboard). Zero console/network errors throughout except the already-documented, out-of-scope `aiLimiter` rate-limit artifact (Phase 5 finding) from rapid repeated automated logins — confirmed not a regression. Three throwaway QA accounts created and fully deleted from the real Firebase project.
- **Tests/checks performed and results:** backend `npm test` 72/72, `npm run lint` 0 errors/38 warnings; frontend `npm run lint` 0 errors/15 warnings, `npm test` 4/4, `npm run build` succeeds — identical to Phase 3's original sign-off.
- **Files modified:** `frontend/src/pages/Auth.jsx` only (the `switchMode` fix). Documentation: `PHASES.md`, this file, `PROGRESS.md`, `TASKS.md`.
- **Updated phase statuses:** Phase 3 → re-confirmed Completed (2026-08-16) with 1 fix. All other phases unchanged.
- **Exact next recommended step:** await the client's explicit "OK"/continue before starting the Phase 4 re-audit (which directly covers the dashboard-greeting report). Strongly recommend the client also address the uncommitted-work finding above independently of phase-by-phase progress, since it's very likely the actual explanation for the original bug reports.

- **What was implemented:** The 5-step wizard kept its existing step count (client explicitly confirmed keeping 5 steps rather than the spec's literal 7) and gained the missing content: Step 1 (`ClaimIdentityFields`) gained Policy Number, Insurance Company, Insured First/Last Name (live-composing into the existing canonical Insured Name field), Claim Type, Property Type; Step 2 gained structured Street/City/State/Zip (live-composing into the existing canonical Property Address) and a new Inspection Details subsection (Date/Time, Inspector Name/ID, Inspection Type, Weather, Occupancy Status, Contact Present); Step 4 (renamed "Upload Photos & Documents") gained a Supporting Documents upload subsection (PDF/DOC/DOCX/TXT, filename/size/status/remove, client-validated before staging); Step 5's review grid was extended with the new fields; an explicit "Save Draft" button was added to the footer alongside the pre-existing silent autosave. Backend: `POST /generate`'s multer usage changed to a `.fields([{images},{documents}])` instance (`generateUpload`, scoped to this one route), all 19 new fields validated when present and persisted onto the report doc (but deliberately NOT added to the AI-prompt input, keeping this phase's "no AI-prompt change" constraint provably true), documents magic-byte validated (new `backend/utils/documentValidation.js`) and stored privately under `users/{uid}/reports/{reportId}/documents/`, with a new `GET /:id/documents/download` retrieval route. `ReportDetailModal` extended to show the new fields/documents with conditional rendering for backward compatibility.

- **Bug found and fixed during live verification:** the First/Last Name → Insured Name (and address-parts → Property Address) "auto-compose while empty" convenience logic froze after the first sub-field was typed (typing First Name alone made Insured Name non-empty, which then blocked Last Name from ever contributing). Fixed by comparing against the *previous* composed value instead of only checking emptiness, so the sync keeps applying across multiple keystrokes/fields until the user actually diverges it manually. Re-verified: both fields now compose correctly end-to-end, confirmed by reading the generated report's Firestore document directly.
- **Important implementation decisions:** Kept Insured Name and Property Address as the single canonical fields every existing consumer (validation, AI prompt, exports, search) already depends on — the new structured fields are purely additive, live-composing into them only while they're still empty or unchanged, never overwriting a Quick Demo fill, a linked CRM claim, or a manual edit. Documents are uploaded atomically with the report (same multipart request as photos), not through a separate pre-generate upload endpoint, matching the existing photo-upload architecture and avoiding new async-state complexity that's explicitly out of scope until Phase 7. Text extraction / AI-prompt integration for uploaded documents deliberately NOT implemented (explicitly out of scope per the task and the original Phase 5 risk note).
- **Tests/checks performed and results:** Backend 72/72 tests (8 new), frontend 4/4, both packages 0 lint errors (38/15 warnings, +1 backend warning matching an existing accepted `catch(err)`-unused pattern), both builds succeed. Full live-browser verification (Playwright, real dev servers tree-killed/restarted fresh multiple times to work around the rate-limiter finding below, real Firebase project): complete wizard walkthrough with every new field, 1 photo + 2 documents uploaded (one removed), Save Draft, mid-wizard refresh (text fields restored, files correctly did not survive), a real successful generation against the live Anthropic API with every new field confirmed persisted by reading Firestore directly, required-field validation blocking, invalid-document-type client-side rejection, document download from the detail modal, a seeded pre-Phase-5 old-shape report opening with zero crashes, and desktop+mobile layouts. Test account, reports, and Storage objects deleted afterward.

(2) A significant PRE-EXISTING, out-of-scope finding: `backend/server.js` applies the AI-specific `aiLimiter` (10 req/60s) to the *entire* `/reports` router, not just generation endpoints — this repeatedly exhausted mid-test and is a genuine production-reliability risk for ordinary usage, not just automated testing (see Known Issues/`PHASES.md` Phase 5 for full detail). Worked around during verification by restarting the backend between test passes to reset the in-memory counter; NOT fixed, since it's unrelated to wizard-completion scope. (3) Two test-script-only mistakes (an ambiguous `button:has-text("Generate Report")` selector matching the sidebar nav item instead of the wizard's submit button; an assumption that the wizard's `step` state resets when re-entering via the sidebar nav, which it doesn't) — both fixed in the test script itself, not app bugs.

### 2026-08-15 (session 3) — Phase 4 implementation + live verification: Dashboard Home View
- **What was implemented:** `Dashboard.jsx`'s default `activeView` changed from `'generate'` to a new `'home'` overview: time-based greeting using `userProfile.firstName` (falls back to splitting `displayName`); 4 metric cards (Reports This Month from the existing profile field, Photos Analyzed/Awaiting Review/Completed Reports from a new backend endpoint); a 5-row Recent Reports table (own fetch/state, deliberately separate from the paginated "My Reports" tab) with the full spec'd columns; a Flacron Engine Usage panel (photos processed, reports this month, and storage usage honestly rendered as "Not yet available" — no byte-level tracking exists anywhere in this codebase, confirmed via grep, so Golden Rule #1 required reporting the gap rather than fabricating a number); a primary "Generate Report" CTA. Backend: new `GET /api/reports/dashboard-summary` in `backend/routes/reports.js` (placed before any `/:id` route), using Firestore `.count()` for the two status counts and a `.select('imageCount')` projection (summed in Node) for the photo total.
- **Phase/tasks completed:** `PHASES.md` Phase 4 — Dashboard Home View. Marked Completed with full detail in that file.
- **Files modified:** `frontend/src/pages/Dashboard.jsx`, `frontend/src/services/api.js`, `backend/routes/reports.js`.
- **Bug found and fixed during live verification:** the endpoint's first implementation used Firestore's `.aggregate({sum('imageCount')})` chained onto the `userId` equality filter, which threw `FAILED_PRECONDITION` (missing composite index) against the real project — this repo has no `firestore.indexes.json`, so index provisioning is ad hoc, not CLI-managed. Fixed by replacing the aggregate-sum with a `.select('imageCount')` projection scoped to the user's own reports, summed in JS — avoids the composite-index dependency without a full-collection scan.
- **Important implementation decisions:** Kept the new endpoint deliberately minimal (3 numbers) to avoid duplicating Phase 15's future Analytics work. Gave the home view's Recent Reports its own fetch/state rather than reusing the "My Reports" tab's paginated/searchable state, to keep both simple and independent.

All metrics cross-checked against the exact seeded Firestore values and matched. Both temporary QA accounts and their report docs were deleted afterward.

(2) A cookie-consent banner intercepted the login button in headless testing — worked around in the test script only, not an app bug.
